# Database schema

Field dictionary for the canonical, POS-agnostic schema in `supabase/migrations/`. Everything here is derived from the migrations; when they change, this file changes with them. Nothing in this document is specific to one shop: identifiers, keys and payloads are illustrative shapes only.

Two schemas exist:

- `public` holds every table, enum, view and query function and is exposed through the Data API to the `authenticated` and `service_role` roles. `anon` holds no privilege on it.
- `app` holds helper functions used by row-level-security policies and triggers, the fact-reading functions behind the analytics views, the analytics refresh, and the incremental sync schedule (its settings row, its dispatch tickets and their functions). It is not exposed through the API; `authenticated` may execute the helpers it needs, `service_role` alone may run the refresh, and only `postgres` (the role of the jobs, the Edge Functions `sync-incremental`, `oa-webhook` and `bot-webhook`, and `pg_cron`) reaches the schedule. The notification dispatcher's tables (link codes, bot polling state, its settings row and its tickets) and its functions live there too, reachable by `postgres` only (see "Notifications and alerts (phase 12)"), and so do the Zalo binding codes, the OA webhook's day counts and the functions of the Mini App sign-in and the webhooks (see "Zalo configuration (phase 13)"), and the staff pairing codes and wrong-code counts with the roster, consent and pairing functions (see "Staff roster, consent and pairing (phase 14a)").

## Conventions

- Every table has a `uuid` primary key `id` generated by `gen_random_uuid()`, except `tenant_members` (composite key) and `pos_account_tokens` (keyed by the account).
- Every row that belongs to a tenant carries `tenant_id`. Rows that belong to a store also carry `store_id`; rows that come from one POS account carry `pos_account_id`.
- Identifiers assigned by a POS are kept verbatim in `external_id` columns (`text`). Foreign relations to POS entities are expressed by external id, never by our uuid, so a re-synced catalogue never breaks facts.
- Timestamps are `timestamptz`. Calendar days are `date`. Money is `numeric(14,2)` in the tenant's currency. Quantities are `numeric(12,3)`. Percentages (`_pct` columns) are `numeric(6,3)` where `1.100` means 1.1 %. Rates and shares in the aggregate tables and query functions are fractions where `0.250` means 25 %.
- Master-data tables carry `active`, `first_seen_at` and `last_seen_at`, maintained by the sync: a row disappearing from the POS catalogue is marked inactive, never deleted.
- Manual-input tables carry `created_by` (defaults to `auth.uid()`, frozen by a trigger on update), `created_at` and `updated_at` (set by a trigger). The trigger `app.keep_created_by()` lets only the foreign key's own action set `created_by` to null, so removing a login keeps the rows it wrote and drops its name from them (migration `settings_writes_hardening`).
- No table is ever written by the browser except the manual-input tables, `strategy_reports`, the two legal ledgers `consent_records` and `data_subject_requests` (a `notifications` consent excepted), the notification settings `alert_rules` and `channel_policies` (owners with an unlimited scope only), the settings of phase 10 (`report_settings`, `item_category_overrides`, `payment_kind_overrides`, `legal_documents`), the Zalo configuration of phase 13 (`brands`, `zalo_apps`, `oas`, `mini_apps`, `zbs_templates`, `gmf_groups`; owners with an unlimited scope only) and, through column grants, the profile columns of `stores` and `tenants` ("Who can write what"); the staff roster of phase 14a, the tenant's staff revenue level and a person's own `staff_ops` consent and phone are written only through `security definer` functions that check the caller; every other write comes from the sync jobs and the messaging jobs running as `service_role`. The aggregate tables (`agg_*`) are written only by `app.refresh_analytics()`, which the sync jobs call and which an owner or manager may trigger through `refresh_my_analytics()`.

| Enum | Labels | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tenant_role` | `owner`, `manager`, `staff`, `viewer`, `publisher` | `tenant_members.role` |
| `pos_account_status` | `active`, `paused`, `error`, `auth_failed` | `pos_accounts.status` |
| `payment_kind` | `cash`, `card`, `bank_transfer`, `ewallet`, `voucher`, `other` | `payment_methods.kind`, `payment_methods.pos_kind`, `payment_kind_overrides.kind` |
| `line_kind` | `item`, `modifier`, `service`, `package`, `other` | `invoice_lines.line_kind` |
| `invoice_status` | `completed`, `edited`, `voided`, `refund` (migration `invoice_status_refund`, ADR-071) | `invoices.status` |
| `invoice_event_kind` | `voided`, `edited`, `line_removed`, `reissued` | `invoice_events.kind` |
| `sync_kind` | `incremental`, `nightly`, `backfill`, `master_data`, `reconciliation` | `sync_runs.kind` |
| `sync_status` | `running`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `partial` | `sync_runs.status` |
| `period_kind` | `month`, `week` | `targets.period_kind` |
| `strategy_source` | `skill`, `routine`, `pasted` | `strategy_reports.generated_via` |
| `item_type_role` | `drink`, `topping`, `food`, `snack`, `other` | `item_type_roles.role` |
| `holiday_kind` | `public`, `tet`, `custom` | `holidays.kind` |
| `legal_form` | `household_business`, `micro_enterprise`, `small_enterprise`, `enterprise`, `unknown` | `tenants.legal_form` |
| `e_invoice_status` | `unknown`, `not_configured`, `configured`, `disabled` | `stores.e_invoice_status` |
| `tran_no_reset_period` | `daily`, `yearly`, `never`, `unknown` | `stores.tran_no_reset_period` |
| `pos_feature_status` | `unknown`, `enabled`, `disabled` | `stores.member_capture_status` |
| `legal_document_kind` | `privacy_notice`, `data_protection_rules`, `mini_app_terms`, `staff_notice`, `loyalty_consent_text`, `marketing_consent_text` | `legal_documents.kind` |
| `legal_document_status` | `draft`, `published`, `retired` | `legal_documents.status` |
| `consent_subject` | `customer`, `staff` | `consent_records.subject_type`, `data_subject_requests.subject_type`, `outbound_messages.subject_type` |
| `consent_purpose` | `loyalty`, `marketing`, `staff_ops`, `notifications` | `consent_records.purpose`, `outbound_messages.purpose`, `channel_policies.consent_purpose` |
| `channel_kind` | `zalo_bot`, `email`, `telegram`, `webhook`, `zalo_oa`, `zalo_gmf`, `zalo_zbs`, `mini_app`, `sms` | `notification_channels.kind`, `channel_policies.channel_kind`, `app.bot_poll_state.kind` |
| `message_class` | `operational`, `transactional`, `advertising` | `channel_policies.message_class`, `outbound_messages.message_class` |
| `alert_severity` | `info`, `warning`, `critical` | `alert_rule_catalog.default_severity`, `alert_rules.severity`, `alert_events.severity`, `outbound_messages.severity` |
| `dsr_type` | `access`, `correction`, `deletion`, `export`, `objection`, `withdraw_consent` | `data_subject_requests.request_type` |
| `dsr_status` | `received`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `rejected` | `data_subject_requests.status` |

`strategy_source` says how a plan reached the database: `skill` when the local strategy skill published it, `routine` when a scheduled cloud routine did, `pasted` when a person pasted it in the web app.

`consent_purpose` `notifications` (added by its own migration, `notify_consent_purpose`, so the label commits before any row uses it) covers the daily digests and operational alerts a member receives; it is separate from `staff_ops`, so withdrawing one never stops the other. `channel_kind` lists every place a message can go; only `zalo_bot`, `email`, `telegram` and `webhook` have an adapter in phase 12 (`app.channel_kind_available`), the other kinds exist so their policies are data. `message_class` separates internal operational messages, which are not advertising (Decree 91/2020 Art. 3(1)), from the customer classes of phases 17 to 19.

`legal_form` records the owner's entity type under the personal-data law: a household business or micro-enterprise is exempt from the cross-border transfer dossier, a small enterprise may defer it, an enterprise must file it. `unknown` is the default until the owner confirms. `e_invoice_status` says whether the store's POS issues cash-register e-invoices. `tran_no_reset_period` says how often the POS restarts its receipt numbering, which is why receipt claims are keyed on store, business date and receipt number together.

## Tenancy and access

### `tenants`

One row per owner organisation. Everything else hangs off it.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `slug` | text | no | URL-safe unique name |
| `name` | text | no | Display name |
| `country_code` | text | no | ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, default `VN`; selects the global holiday rows |
| `currency_code` | text | no | ISO 4217, default `VND`; the unit of every money column of the tenant |
| `timezone` | text | no | IANA zone, default `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`; default for new stores |
| `legal_form` | legal_form | no | Entity type of the owner, default `unknown`; drives the cross-border dossier decision; written by an owner with an unlimited scope |
| `default_language` | text | no | `vi` (default) or `en`: the web app's first language for a member without a stored preference; written by an owner with an unlimited scope |
| `staff_revenue_visibility` | text | no | What members with role `staff` read of revenue (FR-STF-13): `all` (the default, the owner's choice: every revenue row of their stores, as a manager of those stores reads them), `store_totals` (only today's net revenue and invoice count of their stores, through `today_store_totals()`) or `none` (no revenue at all). Owners, managers and viewers are not affected. Written only through `set_staff_revenue_visibility()` by an owner with an unlimited scope; the column grant does not include it, so a direct update is refused (`42501`). Migration `operations_rls` (#211, #216) |
| `profile_updated_at`, `profile_updated_by` | timestamptz, uuid | yes | When and by whom `legal_form`, `default_language` or `staff_revenue_visibility` last changed (`tenants_stamp_profile`); the catch-up columns never stamp them |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation |
| `analytics_inputs_version` | bigint | no | Default 0; moved by every write to a table that refresh-time aggregates read (`item_type_roles`, `item_category_overrides`, `payment_fee_rates`); never written by the browser (phase 10) |
| `analytics_inputs_changed_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the version last moved |
| `analytics_pending_from`, `analytics_pending_to` | date | yes | The business dates whose refresh-time aggregates may still use earlier mappings: 2023-01-01 to `current_date + 1` after a change, null when caught up; both set or both null, never reversed |

### `tenant_members`

Grants a login access to a tenant with a role and an optional store scope.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant (cascade delete); part of the primary key |
| `user_id` | uuid | no | `auth.users.id` (cascade delete); part of the primary key |
| `role` | tenant_role | no | See the RLS model below |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | yes | Null grants every store of the tenant; otherwise the member sees and manages only the listed stores |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation |

## POS connectivity

### `pos_accounts`

One row per login to a POS vendor. Holds metadata and external ids only; credentials live in CI secrets and local env files, never here.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key; also unique together with `tenant_id` for tenant-consistent foreign keys |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `connector` | text | no | Connector package name that speaks this POS |
| `account_key` | text | no | Name of the credential entry the sync looks up; unique per tenant |
| `display_name` | text | no | Label shown in the app |
| `external_company_id` | text | yes | Company id on the POS side |
| `external_brand_id` | text | yes | Brand id on the POS side |
| `status` | pos_account_status | no | Default `active`; `paused` skips the sync, `error` marks a failing account, `auth_failed` is set by a runner when the POS rejects the login and cleared by the next login that succeeds; the incremental schedule skips `paused` and `auth_failed` accounts |
| `last_synced_at` | timestamptz | yes | End of the last successful run |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation |
| `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Set by trigger |

### `pos_account_tokens`

Short-lived session token cache. Service role only: no policy, no grant to `authenticated`.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Primary key, references `pos_accounts` (cascade delete) |
| `token` | text | no | Current session token |
| `expires_at` | timestamptz | no | When the token stops working |
| `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Set by trigger |

## Stores and master data

### `stores`

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key; unique together with `tenant_id` |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Account the store is synced from |
| `external_id` | text | no | Store id on the POS; unique per account |
| `code` | text | yes | Short code for labels |
| `name` | text | no | Display name |
| `address` | text | yes | Street address |
| `timezone` | text | no | IANA zone of the store, default `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` |
| `business_day_start_minutes` | integer | no | Minutes after local midnight at which the business day starts, default 0, range 0–1439 |
| `opening_hours` | jsonb | yes | Weekly opening hours, see below; checked by `stores_opening_hours_valid` |
| `seat_count` | integer | yes | Seats available, zero or more; null when unknown |
| `opened_on` | date | yes | First trading day; aligns since-opening comparisons |
| `e_invoice_status` | e_invoice_status | no | Whether the POS issues cash-register e-invoices, default `unknown` |
| `tran_no_reset_period` | tran_no_reset_period | no | How often the POS restarts receipt numbering, default `unknown`; entered by an owner or manager, and it wins over `receipt_numbers_reset` unless `unknown` (`app.receipt_numbers_reset`) |
| `receipt_numbers_reset` | tran_no_reset_period | no | How often the POS restarts receipt numbering as the POS reports it (iPOS `extra_data.reset_tran_no_period`), written by every sync: `daily`, `yearly` or `unknown` (check `stores_receipt_numbers_reset_reported`), default `unknown`; no API role writes it |
| `member_capture_status` | pos_feature_status | no | Whether the POS member feature is switched on, default `unknown`; entered by an owner or manager |
| `profile_updated_at`, `profile_updated_by` | timestamptz, uuid | yes | When and by whom a member last changed a profile column (`stores_stamp_profile`); the sync never stamps them |
| `settings` | jsonb | no | Free-form store settings, default `{}`; `{"incremental_sync": false}` keeps the store out of the incremental schedule (the seeded demo stores carry it, their accounts have no credentials) |
| `active` | boolean | no | False once the POS no longer lists the store |
| `sync_resumed_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the incremental sync last started watching the store again: set by trigger when an update makes it active and opted in again, or when its account leaves `paused`; the dead-man's switch counts no opening time before it |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Original POS payload |
| `first_seen_at` | timestamptz | no | First sync that saw the store |
| `last_seen_at` | timestamptz | no | Last sync that saw the store |

`opening_hours` is an object keyed by weekday `mon`, `tue`, `wed`, `thu`, `fri`, `sat`, `sun`. Each value is a list of `[open, close]` pairs in local `HH:MM`, so a split day is two pairs and a closed day is an empty list or a missing key:

```json
{"mon": [["07:00", "22:30"]], "sat": [["07:00", "12:00"], ["16:00", "23:00"]]}
```

The check constraint `stores_opening_hours_valid` (`app.opening_hours_valid`) refuses any other shape: the value is null or such an object, its keys are those seven weekdays, each value is a list, and each pair holds two times `H:MM` or `HH:MM` from `0:00` to `23:59`, or `24:00`. An object without a single pair (`{}`, empty lists) is allowed and counts as unknown hours for the schedule. `authenticated` and `service_role` may execute the two validators, so a future writer of stores passes the constraint; the trigger functions that set `sync_resumed_at` (`app.mark_store_sync_resumed`, `app.mark_account_sync_resumed`) are `security definer`.

### Catalogue tables synced per POS account

`item_types`, `item_classes`, `units`, `packages`, `customizations`, `sources`, `payment_methods`, `promotions` and `devices` share one shape: `id`, `tenant_id`, `pos_account_id`, `external_id` (unique per account), `name`, `active`, `raw`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`, plus the specific columns below.

| Table | Extra columns | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `item_types` | — | Menu categories as the POS groups items for reports |
| `item_classes` | — | Coarser grouping above item types (for example drinks versus food) |
| `units` | — | Units of sale |
| `packages` | `list_price` numeric(14,2) null | Combos sold as one line |
| `customizations` | — | Modifier options such as sugar or ice level |
| `sources` | — | Order channels such as dine-in or takeaway |
| `payment_methods` | `kind` payment_kind not null default `other`; `pos_kind` payment_kind not null default `other` | Tenders; `kind` is the effective classification used by shift cash and payment-mix reports: the tenant's `payment_kind_overrides` row when one exists, else `pos_kind`, the kind the sync classified (`payment_methods_apply_kind_override`) |
| `promotions` | `starts_at`, `ends_at` timestamptz null | Promotion catalogue with validity window |
| `devices` | `code`, `name`, `store_external_id` text null; `store_id` uuid null | POS terminals; `store_id` is resolved by the sync and set to null if the store disappears |

| `base_name` | text | yes | Name without the size suffix, for size-mix reports |

| `item_type_external_id` | text | yes | Item type by external id |
| `item_class_external_id` | text | yes | Item class by external id |

| `unit_name` | text | yes | Unit name at sync time |

| `cost_price` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Cost the POS reports, if any; manual costs live in `item_costs` |
| `is_modifier` | boolean | no | True for items that only appear as modifier lines |

| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Original payload |
| `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at` | timestamptz | no | Lifecycle timestamps |

Per-store price overrides. Unique on (`store_id`, `item_external_id`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `item_external_id` | text | no | Item by external id |
| `list_price` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Store price |
| `takeaway_price` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Store takeaway price |
| `active` | boolean | no | Lifecycle flag |

### `customers`

Customers attached to invoices, and loyalty members. Unique on (`tenant_id`, `phone`) where `phone` is present. `authenticated` selects every column but `phone` (column grant, #253; see [Customer phones and the owner's member reads](#customer-phones-and-the-owners-member-reads-253)).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `external_id` | text | yes | Customer id on the POS, when it has one |
| `phone` | text | yes | Phone number as captured at the till, E.164 for a loyalty customer; no API role selects it |
| `name` | text | yes | Name as captured |
| `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at` | timestamptz | no | First and last invoice seen |

## Sales facts

### `invoices`

One row per receipt. Unique on (`store_id`, `external_id`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Account |
| `external_id` | text | no | Transaction id on the POS |
| `receipt_no` | text | yes | Printed receipt number |
| `sold_at` | timestamptz | no | Moment of sale |
| `business_date` | date | no | Business day, see the rule below |
| `opened_at` | timestamptz | yes | Order opened |
| `closed_at` | timestamptz | yes | Order closed |
| `table_name` | text | yes | Table or card label |
| `area_name` | text | yes | Seating area |
| `sale_type` | text | yes | POS sale type code |
| `source_external_id` | text | yes | Order channel by external id |
| `source_name` | text | yes | Channel name at sale time |
| `employee_external_id` | text | yes | Cashier id |
| `employee_name` | text | yes | Cashier name |
| `shift_external_id` | text | yes | Shift the sale belongs to |
| `device_code` | text | yes | Terminal code |
| `guest_count` | integer | yes | Number of guests when the POS records it |
| `customer_id` | uuid | yes | Resolved customer (set null on delete) |
| `customer_phone` | text | yes | Phone as printed on the receipt |
| `customer_name` | text | yes | Name as printed |
| `gross_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Sum of line gross amounts |
| `item_discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Sum of line-level discounts |
| `bill_discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Bill-level discount applied as a price reduction |
| `extra_discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Second bill-level discount the POS reports separately |
| `bill_discount_name` | text | yes | Label of the bill-level discount |
| `voucher_code` | text | yes | Voucher code redeemed |
| `voucher_name` | text | yes | Voucher name |
| `voucher_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Voucher value applied |
| `partner_marketing_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Discount funded by a delivery or payment partner |
| `service_charge_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Service charge added |
| `shipping_fee_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Delivery fee added |
| `vat_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | VAT included in the total |
| `net_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Amount after all discounts and additions |
| `paid_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Amount tendered |
| `primary_payment_method_external_id` | text | yes | Largest tender by external id |
| `primary_payment_method_name` | text | yes | Its name |
| `status` | invoice_status | no | `completed` by default; `edited` once an edit or line removal was detected; `voided` once a void was detected; `refund` for the negative document of a native refund handed back on a later business day than its order's (ADR-071): in every sum, never counted as an invoice |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Null when the sync wrote the row; the payload is in `sale_payloads`, see the storage rule |
| `synced_at` | timestamptz | no | Last time the sync wrote the row |
| `has_customer_phone` | boolean | no | Stored generated column, `customer_phone is not null`: a member was attached at the till. Reports count member invoices from it (`member_share_by_shift`, #236) without reading the phone, so they keep working when `customer_phone` loses its column grant |
| `has_customer_identity` | boolean | no | Stored generated column, `customer_phone is not null or customer_id is not null`, the identified invoices of the same report |

Indexes serve the report access paths: (`store_id`, `business_date`), (`store_id`, `sold_at`), (`tenant_id`, `business_date`), (`store_id`, `shift_external_id`) and `customer_id`.

### `invoice_lines`

One row per line on a receipt. Unique on (`invoice_id`, `external_id`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `invoice_id` | uuid | no | Invoice (cascade delete) |
| `external_id` | text | no | Line id on the POS, or a stable id the sync derives |
| `parent_line_external_id` | text | yes | For a modifier line, the `external_id` of the item line it belongs to; null on item lines |
| `line_kind` | line_kind | no | `item`, `modifier`, `service`, `package` or `other` |
| `item_external_id` | text | yes | Item by external id |
| `item_name` | text | no | Name at sale time |
| `base_name` | text | yes | Name without size |
| `size_label` | text | yes | Size |
| `item_type_external_id`, `item_type_name` | text | yes | Item type at sale time |
| `item_class_external_id`, `item_class_name` | text | yes | Item class at sale time |
| `unit_external_id`, `unit_name` | text | yes | Unit at sale time |
| `package_external_id` | text | yes | Combo this line was sold inside |
| `quantity` | numeric(12,3) | no | Quantity, default 1 |
| `list_unit_price` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Menu price at sale time; differs from `unit_price` on a manual override |
| `unit_price` | numeric(14,2) | no | Price actually charged per unit |
| `gross_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | `quantity × unit_price` |
| `discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Line discount |
| `net_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Gross less discount |
| `promotion_external_id`, `promotion_name` | text | yes | Promotion that produced the discount |
| `note` | text | yes | Free-text note from the cashier |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Null when the sync wrote the row; the payload is in `sale_payloads`, see the storage rule |

### `invoice_payments`

One row per tender on a receipt. Unique on (`invoice_id`, `sequence_no`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `invoice_id` | uuid | no | Invoice (cascade delete) |
| `sequence_no` | smallint | no | Position of the tender in the invoice payment list |
| `business_date` | date | no | Copied from the invoice so payment reports need no join |
| `payment_method_external_id` | text | yes | Tender by external id |
| `payment_method_name` | text | yes | Tender name at sale time |
| `amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Amount tendered |
| `fee_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Fee the POS reports for this tender |
| `reference_no` | text | yes | Bank or wallet reference |
| `partner_transaction_id` | text | yes | Transaction id on the payment partner's side |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Null when the sync wrote the row; the payload is in `sale_payloads`, see the storage rule |

### `invoice_events`

Changes detected on an invoice after it was first synced: voids, edits, removed lines and reissues. Written by the sync when a re-sync differs from the stored row (`source = resync_diff`) or when a POS adjustment report lists the change (`source = pos_report`). Unique on (`store_id`, `invoice_external_id`, `kind`, `occurred_at`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `invoice_id` | uuid | yes | Invoice (set null on delete) so the event survives a hard delete on the POS |
| `invoice_external_id` | text | no | Transaction id, kept even without the invoice row |
| `kind` | invoice_event_kind | no | `voided`, `edited`, `line_removed`, `reissued` |
| `occurred_at` | timestamptz | no | When the change happened, or when it was detected if the POS gives no time |
| `amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Money involved: the invoice net amount of a void, the line net amount of a removal, the difference of an edit |
| `reason` | text | yes | Reason text from the POS or the cashier |
| `source` | text | no | How the sync learned of it, for example `resync_diff` or `pos_report` |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Original adjustment payload |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation |

Indexes: (`store_id`, `occurred_at`) and `invoice_id`.

### `sale_payloads`

The POS payloads of the sales of one store and business date, kept here instead of in `invoices.raw`, `invoice_lines.raw` and `invoice_payments.raw` (see the storage rule). Primary key (`store_id`, `business_date`); deleted with its store or tenant.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `business_date` | date | no | Business date of the sales, as on `invoices` |
| `payload` | text | no | One JSON record per sale, records separated by line breaks and ordered by `external_id`, compressed with lz4: `external_id` (the invoice's), `invoice` (the sale payload), `lines` (the payload of each invoice line keyed by its `external_id`) and `payments` (the payload of each tender keyed by its `sequence_no`) |
| `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Last write |

Row level security is on with no policy, and `anon` and `authenticated` hold no privilege: only the service role reads and writes it. The sync writes it through `app.store_sale_payloads(tenant, store, business_date, records text[])` (service role only) in the transaction that writes the store-day, before the invoice rows: the call replaces the records of the given sales, keeps every other record of the day (a voided sale keeps its payload), and takes a sale whose business date changed out of the document of the date its invoice row still carries, removing a document left empty. It refuses a record without `external_id` or the same sale twice (22023). A data-subject deletion rewrites the records that held the erased customer (see "Data-subject deletion"); `updated_at` then moves too.

### `shifts`

One row per cash-register shift. Unique on (`store_id`, `external_id`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `external_id` | text | no | Shift id on the POS |
| `name` | text | yes | Shift label |
| `cashier_external_id`, `cashier_name` | text | yes | Cashier who opened the shift |
| `opened_at` | timestamptz | no | Shift open |
| `closed_at` | timestamptz | yes | Shift close; null while open |
| `sales_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Net sales the POS attributes to the shift |
| `orders_count` | integer | no | Number of receipts |
| `cash_drawer_amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Cash counted at close |
| `opening_float_amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Cash in the drawer at open |
| `expected_cash_amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Opening float plus cash tenders of the shift, computed by the sync |
| `cash_variance_amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Generated: `cash_drawer_amount − expected_cash_amount` |
| `changed_after_close` | boolean | no | True when an invoice of the shift changed after the shift closed |
| `status` | text | yes | Shift status as the POS reports it |
| `device_code` | text | yes | Terminal code |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Full original payload |

## Sync bookkeeping

### `sync_runs`

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Account synced |
| `store_id` | uuid | yes | The one store the run covered: every incremental run, and a window run given a single store; null when the run covered every store it was given. References `stores` with the tenant (cascade delete) |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | yes | Every store the run covered: the incremental run's store, the stores given to a window run; null on master-data runs and on rows written before migration `20261108104800`, which count as covering every store of their account |
| `kind` | sync_kind | no | Mode of the run |
| `range_start`, `range_end` | date | yes | Business-date window covered |
| `started_at` | timestamptz | no | Start |
| `finished_at` | timestamptz | yes | End; null while running |
| `status` | sync_status | no | `running`; `succeeded`; `partial` when an incremental run stopped cleanly before syncing every changed day (its CPU or wall-time guard, or a store held by another run) and left the rest to the nightly job; `failed` |
| `rows_upserted` | jsonb | no | Counts per table, for example `{"invoices": 120}` |
| `error_message` | text | yes | Failure text |
| `triggered_by` | text | yes | `schedule`, `manual`, `seed`, … |

An index on (`store_id`, `started_at desc`) where `store_id` is not null serves the per-store freshness reads. Data members read the runs of their tenant that touch their store scope: a run whose `store_ids` overlaps it, a single-store row of an older run for a store in it, and runs without either (master data, older account-wide runs); a manager of one store does not see another store's incremental runs.

### `sync_checks`

Per store-day reconciliation of our totals against the POS totals. Unique on (`store_id`, `business_date`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `business_date` | date | no | Day checked |
| `source_invoice_count` | integer | no | Receipts the POS reports |
| `source_net_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Net sales the POS reports |
| `local_invoice_count` | integer | no | Receipts stored here |
| `local_net_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Net sales stored here |
| `is_matched` | boolean | no | True when both pairs agree within tolerance |
| `checked_at` | timestamptz | no | Time of the check |

### `app.tenant_ipos_sources`

Where a tenant's iPOS data comes from (migration `tenant_ipos_sources`): one row per tenant with `source` `api`, written by a run with `IPOS_REAL_API=on`, or `snapshot`, written by a run that replayed the local iPOS snapshot with `IPOS_REAL_API=off`, and `recorded_at`. `app.claim_ipos_source(p_tenant_id, p_source)` records the source at the tenant's first write and returns the recorded one, which the runners compare with their own before they write anything, so replayed data never mixes with real data ([sync.md](sync.md#real-or-fake-ipos)); any other source is refused with `22023`. The migration marked every tenant that already had an `ipos-fabi` account as `api`. The table and the function are revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`, so only `postgres`, the role of the CLI and the Edge Function, reaches them; the function is `security invoker` with an empty `search_path`.

### Incremental schedule

Migration `20261108084100_incremental_sync_schedule.sql` installs `pg_cron` (in `pg_catalog`) and `pg_net` (in `extensions`) and keeps the schedule of the `sync-incremental` Edge Function in `app`. `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` hold no privilege on these tables and may execute none of these functions; `postgres` owns them, and the `pg_cron` job runs as `postgres`. `docs/sync.md` ("Incremental runner") describes how they work together.

`app.incremental_sync_settings` has exactly one row:

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `singleton` | boolean | no | Primary key, always true |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Default false: the schedule is inert until the owner enables it |
| `function_url` | text | yes | `http(s)` URL of the function, set when enabling |
| `request_timeout_ms` | integer | no | `pg_net` timeout of each call, default 30000, between 1000 and 150000 |
| `ticket_lifetime` | interval | no | How long a ticket can be claimed, default 10 minutes, between 1 minute and 1 hour |
| `stale_after_minutes` | integer | no | Opening minutes without a successful sync after which a tick opens a `sync_stale` alert, default 90 like the runners, between 30 and 1440 |
| `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Last change |

`app.incremental_sync_tickets` holds the single-use dispatch tickets:

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ticket` | uuid | no | Primary key, random; the only thing the call to the function carries |
| `tenant_id`, `store_id` | uuid | no | The store to sync; references `stores` with the tenant (cascade delete) |
| `due_at` | timestamptz | no | The tick the store was selected for |
| `issued_at`, `expires_at` | timestamptz | no | Issue time and the end of the ticket's lifetime |
| `request_id` | bigint | yes | The `pg_net` request that carried it |
| `source` | text | no | `schedule` (default) or `manual`; becomes `sync_runs.triggered_by` |

Indexes: `expires_at` and `store_id`.

`app.sync_alerts` holds what the scheduler raises at every tick without the Edge Function (migration `20261108104800_incremental_sync_hardening.sql`):

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | bigint | no | Primary key, identity |
| `tenant_id`, `store_id` | uuid | no | The store; references `stores` with the tenant (cascade delete) |
| `kind` | text | no | `sync_stale` (a watched store over the threshold) or `dispatch_failed` (a scheduled ticket expired unclaimed) |
| `opened_at`, `last_seen_at` | timestamptz | no | When the condition was first and last found |
| `resolved_at` | timestamptz | yes | When it cleared: a fresh tick for `sync_stale`, a claimed ticket for `dispatch_failed`, or disabling the schedule; null while open |
| `occurrences` | integer | no | Ticks (or expired tickets) that found it, at least 1 |
| `minutes` | integer | yes | `sync_stale`: the latest opening minutes without a successful sync |
| `http_status`, `error` | integer, text | yes | `dispatch_failed`: the HTTP status `pg_net` kept for the last call, or `no answer recorded`, `timed out`, `pg_net`'s error (200 characters at most) or `answered without claiming the ticket` |
| `notified_at` | timestamptz | yes | For the phase 12 channels |

A unique index on (`store_id`, `kind`) where `resolved_at` is null keeps one open row per store and kind; another on `resolved_at` serves the retention.

| Function | Returns | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `enable_incremental_sync(p_function_url, p_schedule default '*/30 * * * *')` | bigint | Stores the URL, enables the row and schedules the `pg_cron` job `sync-incremental`; refuses a URL that is not `http(s)` (22023) |
| `disable_incremental_sync()` | void | Disables the row, resolves every open alert and unschedules the job |
| `dispatch_incremental_sync(p_at default now())` | integer | The job body: `record_unclaimed_incremental_sync_tickets()`, `prune_incremental_sync_history()`, returns 0 while disabled, otherwise `evaluate_incremental_sync_alerts(p_at)` and one ticket per store of `incremental_sync_targets(p_at)` posted as `{"ticket": …}` to the function with `net.http_post`; `p_at` simulates the tick for tests |
| `record_unclaimed_incremental_sync_tickets()` | integer | Deletes the expired tickets and records each scheduled one as a `dispatch_failed` alert of its store, with the status or error of its `net._http_response` row |
| `evaluate_incremental_sync_alerts(p_at default now())` | integer | Opens or refreshes a `sync_stale` alert for every watched store whose `sync_gap_minutes` is over `stale_after_minutes`, resolves the open ones that are not; returns the stores found stale |
| `prune_incremental_sync_history()` | void | Retention: incremental `sync_runs` older than 35 days (90 when not succeeded), `cron.job_run_details` older than 7 days, resolved alerts older than 90 days, `analytics_refreshes` older than 35 days except each tenant's newest |
| `issue_incremental_sync_ticket(p_store_id)` | uuid | One manual ticket for a store, whether or not the schedule is enabled; 22023 for an unknown store |
| `claim_incremental_sync_ticket(p_ticket)` | table (`tenant_id`, `store_id`, `due_at`, `source`) | Deletes and returns an unexpired ticket and resolves the store's open `dispatch_failed` alert, or returns nothing; the function calls it before anything else |
| `store_watched_for_sync(p_active, p_settings, p_account_status)` | boolean | `immutable`; the one rule for which stores the incremental sync watches: active, account not `paused`, not opted out in `settings` |
| `incremental_sync_watched(p_store_id)` | boolean | The rule above for one store by id; false for an unknown store |
| `incremental_sync_targets(p_at default now())` | table (`tenant_id`, `store_id`, `hours_known`, `open_now`) | Watched stores, except those of `auth_failed` accounts, due by `store_due_for_incremental` |
| `store_due_for_incremental(p_opening_hours, p_timezone, p_day_start_minutes, p_at, p_interval default '30 minutes')` | boolean | Open at `p_at`, open at any moment of the interval before it (the closing run, however soon after a tick the store closed), or opening hours unknown |
| `opening_hours_valid(p_opening_hours)`, `opening_time_valid(p_value)` | boolean | `immutable`; the shape the check constraint on `stores.opening_hours` requires |
| `opening_hours_known(p_opening_hours)` | boolean | `immutable`; whether the hours hold at least one span `opening_spans` lays out; null, `{}` and empty lists are unknown hours |
| `opening_spans(p_opening_hours, p_timezone, p_day_start_minutes, p_from, p_to)` | table (`opens_at`, `closes_at`) | The opening spans overlapping a range as instants: a weekday key holds the spans of the business dates of that weekday, a time before the business-day start is on the next calendar day, a close at or before the open runs past midnight; malformed spans are ignored |
| `store_open_at(p_opening_hours, p_timezone, p_day_start_minutes, p_at)` | boolean | Open at the instant; null when the opening hours are unknown (never entered or without a usable span) |
| `opening_minutes_between(p_opening_hours, p_timezone, p_day_start_minutes, p_from, p_to)` | integer | Whole opening minutes between two instants, overlapping spans counted once; every minute when the hours are unknown |
| `sync_run_covers_store(p_run_store_id, p_run_store_ids, p_store_id)` | boolean | `immutable`; the store is in `store_ids`, else it is `store_id`, else (older rows without either) the run covered the whole account |
| `sync_run_reached_business_date(p_range_end, p_finished_at, p_timezone, p_day_start_minutes)` | boolean | `immutable`; the run's range ended no earlier than the day before the store's business date at the run's end |
| `last_successful_sync(p_store_id, p_at default now())` | timestamptz | End of the last `succeeded` run before `p_at` (not `master_data`) that covered the store and reached its current days by the two rules above; a backfill of an older month or a window run of other stores does not count |
| `sync_gap_minutes(p_store_id, p_at default now())` | integer | Opening minutes since that run or, when later, since `stores.sync_resumed_at`, looking back at most 7 days; null for a store that is not watched or was never synced. The dead-man's switch fires above 90 |
| `longest_sync_gap_minutes(p_store_id, p_from, p_to)` | integer | The longest stretch of opening time from `p_from` (or `sync_resumed_at`, when later) to `p_to` without a successful sync; null for a store that is not watched or was never synced by `p_to`. The nightly run checks the last business day with it |
| `stale_analytics_dates(p_store_id, p_from, p_to)` | table (`business_date`) | The store's dates in the range whose `sync_checks` row was written after the start of every finished `analytics_refreshes` row covering them; the incremental run refreshes them again |

## Manual inputs

### `item_costs`

Cost per item, tenant-wide or per store, with validity from a date. Unique on (`tenant_id`, `store_id`, `item_external_id`, `effective_from`) with nulls treated as equal, so one tenant-wide and one per-store row may coexist for the same date.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | yes | Null applies to every store of the tenant |
| `item_external_id` | text | no | Item by external id |
| `cost_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Cost per unit sold, a whole number of đồng from 0 to 100,000,000 (check `item_costs_amount_whole_dong`, migration `item_costs_amount_whole_dong`) |
| `effective_from` | date | no | First business date the cost applies to |
| `created_by` | uuid | yes | Author, default `auth.uid()`, frozen on update |
| `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Last writer, set to `auth.uid()` by trigger on every insert and update (null for service-role writes); backfilled from `created_by` (phase 10) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

### `payment_fee_rates`

Fee a payment method costs the shop, tenant-wide or per store, with validity from a date. Same unique rule as `item_costs` on (`tenant_id`, `store_id`, `payment_method_external_id`, `effective_from`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | yes | Null applies to every store of the tenant |
| `payment_method_external_id` | text | no | Payment method by external id |
| `fee_pct` | numeric(6,3) | no | Percentage of the paid amount; `1.100` is 1.1 %; default 0; 0 to 20, with a fixed fee of whole đồng from 0 to 100,000 (check `payment_fee_rates_within_limits`, migration `fee_and_target_limits`) |
| `fixed_fee_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Fixed fee per tender, default 0 |
| `effective_from` | date | no | First business date the rate applies to |
| `created_by` | uuid | yes | Author, default `auth.uid()`, frozen on update |
| `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Last writer, set to `auth.uid()` by trigger on every insert and update (null for service-role writes); backfilled from `created_by` (phase 10) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

### `item_type_roles`

Maps each POS item type to a canonical role so attach-rate and mix reports work for any menu. Unique on (`pos_account_id`, `item_type_external_id`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Account the item type belongs to |
| `item_type_external_id` | text | no | Item type by external id |
| `role` | item_type_role | no | `drink`, `topping`, `food`, `snack` or `other` (default) |
| `created_by` | uuid | yes | Author, default `auth.uid()`, frozen on update |
| `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Last writer, set to `auth.uid()` by trigger on every insert and update (null for service-role writes); backfilled from `created_by` (phase 10) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

### `holidays`

Calendar of non-trading-pattern days. Global rows (`tenant_id` null) are maintained by the service role for a country; tenants add their own rows. Unique on (`tenant_id`, `country_code`, `holiday_date`, `name`) with nulls treated as equal.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | yes | Null applies to every tenant of the country |
| `country_code` | text | no | ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 |
| `holiday_date` | date | no | Calendar day |
| `name` | text | no | Label |
| `kind` | holiday_kind | no | `public` for statutory holidays, `tet` for every day of the Lunar New Year window, `custom` for tenant rows |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Author (must be the caller on insert, frozen afterwards) and last writer (set by trigger); null on the global rows |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation and last change (set by trigger) |

Migration `vn_public_holidays` ships the Vietnamese statutory calendar for 2024–2028 as 61 global rows: 1 January, 30 April, 1 May, 2 September with the adjacent day granted by law, the Hùng Kings' Commemoration (10th day of the 3rd lunar month) and the Tết window from the last day of the old lunar year (both the 29th and 30th when the month has 30 days) through the 5th day of the new year. It inserts a row only when no global row with the same country, date and name exists, so a database that already holds the calendar is unchanged; the seed no longer inserts holidays. `VIETNAM_HOLIDAYS` in `packages/core` holds the same rows for the demo, and its unit test parses the migration to keep the two equal. Lunar dates from 2029 on are not shipped yet.

### `targets`

Net-sales targets per store and period. Unique on (`store_id`, `period_kind`, `period_start`).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | no | Store |
| `period_kind` | period_kind | no | `month` or `week` |
| `period_start` | date | no | First day of the period |
| `net_target_amount` | numeric(14,2) | no | Target net sales, a whole number of đồng of at least 1 (check `targets_amount_whole_dong`, migration `fee_and_target_limits`) |
| `created_by` | uuid | yes | Author, default `auth.uid()`, frozen on update |
| `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Last writer, set to `auth.uid()` by trigger on every insert and update (null for service-role writes); backfilled from `created_by` (phase 10) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

### `strategy_reports`

Strategy plans: immutable documents written only by `publish_strategy_plan` (migration `strategy_plans`, phase 11b, #169). See "Strategy plans (phase 11)" below for the validators, the trigger and the policies.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `store_id` | uuid | yes | The one store of a single-store plan; null for a plan of two to ten stores |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | no | The 1 to 10 stores of one tenant the plan covers (GIN index) |
| `horizon` | text | no | `30d`, `90d`, `180d` or `12m` |
| `language` | text | no | `vi` or `en` |
| `generated_via` | strategy_source | no | `skill`, `routine` or `pasted` |
| `title` | text | no | 1 to 120 characters; with `archived_at`, the only column an owner or manager may change |
| `content_markdown` | text | no | The normalised plan text, at most 200,000 bytes |
| `data_pack` | jsonb | no | The full pack the plan was written from, at most 2,000,000 bytes |
| `kpis` | jsonb | no | Per metric id: scope, store ids, baseline, target, `lo`, `hi`, initiatives, `due_on` and `window_days` 28 |
| `plan_format` | text | no | `fnb-plan/1` |
| `prompt_version`, `pack_schema_version` | text | no | Semantic versions |
| `pack_id` | text | no | 64 hex characters, the id of the stored pack |
| `as_of_date` | date | no | The as-of day of the pack |
| `content_sha256` | text | no | sha256 of the text; unique per tenant, so a repeat publish returns the stored plan |
| `lint` | jsonb | no | The lint summary and warning codes the client recorded |
| `archived_at` | timestamptz | yes | Set when archived; archived plans are listed only on request |
| `created_by` | uuid | yes | The caller of `publish_strategy_plan`; set to null only when that login is deleted |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

## Strategy plans (phase 11)

- **Guard.** The migration stops when `strategy_reports` already holds rows.
- **Check constraint** `strategy_reports_plan_check`: the format, 1 to 10 stores without nulls, `store_id` equal to the one store or null, horizon, language, title length, content and pack size, the hash, pack id and version shapes, and `kpis`, `lint` and `data_pack` as JSON objects.
- **Content trigger** `strategy_reports_keep_content` on `app.keep_plan_content()`: an update may change `title` and `archived_at` and may set `created_by` to null (the cascade when the publishing login is deleted); anything else raises `42501 plan_immutable`. The phase 2 attribution trigger is removed from this table only; `app.keep_created_by()` keeps serving the other tables.
- **Helpers.** `app.trackable_metric_ids()` (the 18 ids KPI follow-up can track, held equal to `trackableMetricIds()` by `packages/datapack/test/consistency/sql-constants.test.ts`), `app.plan_section_titles(language)`, `app.plan_fold(text)` (lower case with the Vietnamese capitals folded and spaces collapsed, whatever the collation), `app.plan_front_matter(content)`, `app.plan_body(content)`, `app.can_manage_stores(tenant_id, store_ids)` and `app.can_publish_stores(tenant_id, store_ids)`.
- **Validators**, each returning ordered codes and returning early where a later check cannot run: `app.plan_structure_errors(content, language)` (`content_not_normalized`, `front_matter_missing`, `section_missing:<n>`, `section_extra:<n>`, `section_order`, `initiatives_count:<k>`), `app.plan_kpi_errors(kpis, store_ids)` (`kpis_count:<k>`, `kpi_unknown:<id>` for any id outside the 18, `kpi_entry_invalid:<id>`, `kpi_target_equals_baseline:<id>`, `kpi_scope_outside_plan:<id>`) and `app.plan_pack_errors(...)` (`front_matter_mismatch:format|pack|title|horizon|language|kpis`, `plan_pack_mismatch:pack|pack_id|sample|schema_version|prompt_version|as_of|horizon|stores|kpi|kpi:<id>`).
- **`public.publish_strategy_plan(...)`** (`security definer`, empty `search_path`): `42501 plan_scope_denied` without a session or without publish scope on every store; `22023 plan_rejected` with the codes in `detail` (comma-separated) for `plan_stores_invalid` (unknown stores, two tenants, more than ten, repeats), the validators' codes, `lint_invalid`, `title_invalid`, `content_too_large`, and `plan_rate_limited` above 20 plans of the tenant in 24 hours. Idempotent on the content hash, checked before the rate limit: the stored id with `already_published` true.
- **Policies.** Reading needs a data role and every store of the plan in the caller's data scope (`store_ids <@ current_user_data_store_ids()`), so the publisher reads no plan and a one-store manager reads no two-store plan. Renaming, archiving and deleting need an owner or manager whose scope covers all the stores of the plan (`can_manage_stores`). `authenticated` has no `INSERT`. Only `authenticated` executes the RPC and the two scope helpers; the validators and constants are revoked from every API role.
- **Tests.** `supabase/tests/195_strategy_plans.sql` (84 assertions: columns, constraint, indexes, trigger, privileges, every rejection code, publishing per role, the repeat, immutability, deleting the publishing login, reading per role, the rate limit); `05` and `08` now expect a direct insert to be refused.

## Settings and mappings (phase 10)

Migration `report_settings_and_mappings` adds the tenant's report thresholds, item category overrides and the analytics catch-up. Every write to a mapping or rate that the refresh turns into aggregates is tracked on the tenant row, never inferred from `analytics_refreshes` (the scheduler prunes that log after 35 days).

### `report_settings`

One row per tenant; a tenant without a row uses the column defaults. `app.store_report_settings()` is the only reader, and `DEFAULT_REPORT_SETTINGS` in `@fnb/reports` mirrors the defaults.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Primary key, tenant |
| `menu_window_days` | smallint | no | Menu-engineering window, 7 to 91, default 28 |
| `menu_popularity_factor` | numeric(4,3) | no | Popularity factor, 0.1 to 1.0, default 0.7 |
| `menu_low_sample_items` | smallint | no | Fewest items per role before the matrix is flagged as a low sample, 2 to 50, default 10 |
| `cash_abs_threshold` | numeric(14,2) | no | Drawer variance worth a flag in the tenant's currency, default 50,000 |
| `cash_rel_threshold` | numeric(6,4) | no | Drawer variance as a share of cash sales, 0 to 0.1, default 0.005 |
| `peak_hours` | smallint | no | Hours counted as peak, 1 to 6, default 3 |
| `dead_hour_ratio` | numeric(4,3) | no | An hour below this share of the mean hour is quiet, 0.1 to 0.9, default 0.5 |
| `busy_hour_ratio` | numeric(4,3) | no | An hour at or above this multiple of the mean hour is busy, 1.05 to 3, default 1.25 |
| `promo_pre_weeks` | smallint | no | Pre-period weeks of the promotion lift, 2 to 8, default 4 |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Author (frozen) and last writer (set by trigger) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

The z-score threshold stays the constant 2 of `app.shift_variance_scores()`.

### `item_category_overrides`

An item moved to another POS item type of the same account, for reports only; the facts keep the type the POS sent. Unique on (`pos_account_id`, `item_external_id`); the target type is a foreign key to `item_types` of the same account.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Tenant and POS account (composite key to `pos_accounts`) |
| `item_external_id` | text | no | The item key of `agg_item_daily`: the line's item id, else its name |
| `item_type_external_id` | text | no | The item type that replaces the item's own |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Author (frozen) and last writer (set by trigger) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

### Triggers and helpers

| Name | On | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `<table>_set_updated_by` → `app.set_updated_by()` | before insert or update on `item_costs`, `payment_fee_rates`, `item_type_roles`, `targets`, `report_settings`, `item_category_overrides`, `payment_kind_overrides`, `legal_documents`, `holidays`, and the six Zalo configuration tables of phase 13 | Sets `updated_by` to `auth.uid()`, whatever the request carries |
| `<table>_touch_analytics_inputs` → `app.touch_analytics_inputs()` | after insert, update or delete, per row, on `item_type_roles`, `item_category_overrides`, `payment_fee_rates`, `payment_kind_overrides` | Moves the tenant's `analytics_inputs_version`, stamps `analytics_inputs_changed_at` and marks 2023-01-01 to `current_date + 1` pending; `security definer`, executable by no API role |
| `app.opening_hours_present(jsonb)` | function | The body of phase 7's private `app.opening_hours_known`, executable by `authenticated` and `service_role` |
| `app.store_report_settings(store_ids)` | function | One row per visible store in `store_ids` order: the tenant's settings or the defaults, and `z_threshold` 2 |
| `app.item_category_map(store_ids)` | function | The overrides of the visible stores' POS accounts with the target type's name |
| `app.effective_item_types(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | function | Per item key sold at the visible stores in the range: the account of the first store that sold it, the effective type and name, `category_source` (`override`, `pos`, `none`) and the role, from that account first (migration `theme_menu_functions`) |
| `app.shrink_analytics_pending(p_tenant, p_from, p_to, p_version)` | function | `security definer`, executable by no API role, called at the end of every refresh: with the tenant's earliest `agg_daily_sales` date as the history start, it updates the tenant row only while `analytics_inputs_version` still equals `p_version`, emptying the pending range when the refreshed dates cover it, when the tenant has no history yet or when the range ends before the history starts, moving its start or end inward when they cover one end, and changing nothing for a refresh strictly inside it (migration `refresh_overrides_and_catch_up`) |
| `app.item_cost_periods(store_ids)` | function | Per visible store, item and scope (store or tenant-wide): each `item_costs` row with the date it stops applying, the start of the next row of the same scope (migration `theme_menu_functions`) |

Migration `settings_writes_and_legal` (10b) opens the settings pages' writes: the profile columns of `stores` and `tenants` through column grants, `payment_kind_overrides`, versioned `legal_documents` with two publishing functions, and the authors of tenant `holidays`. Its trigger functions all pin an empty `search_path` and are executable by no API role.

| Name | On | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `payment_methods_apply_kind_override` → `app.apply_payment_kind_override()` | before insert, or update of `kind`, on `payment_methods` | On insert, copies `kind` into `pos_kind` (an override cannot exist before its method row). On an update that sets `kind`, such as the sync's upsert, copies the incoming kind into `pos_kind` and sets `kind` to the override's kind when one exists, else to `pos_kind`. An update that leaves `kind` out, such as the sync's deactivation, does not fire it. `security definer` |
| `payment_kind_overrides_apply` → `app.apply_payment_kind_overrides_row()` | after insert, update or delete on `payment_kind_overrides` | Rewrites `kind` of the method the row names from its `pos_kind` while the transaction setting `app.payment_kind_override_write` is `on`, so the first trigger applies or removes the override without touching `pos_kind`; deleting the override restores the POS kind. `security definer` |
| `stores_stamp_profile` → `app.stamp_store_profile()` | before update on `stores` | Only for a member's write (`auth.uid()` set; the sync's upserts pass untouched): a change of `opening_hours` by anyone who is not an owner of that store raises `42501`; a `code` that, trimmed and upper-cased, equals another store's code in the tenant raises `23505` naming `stores_code_unique_in_tenant` (there is no unique index, because two POS accounts may use one code; the check runs under a transaction lock per tenant, so two members cannot give two stores one code at the same moment); a change of any profile column stamps `profile_updated_at` and `profile_updated_by`. `security definer`, so it sees every store of the tenant |
| `tenants_stamp_profile` → `app.stamp_tenant_profile()` | before update on `tenants` | Stamps `profile_updated_at` and `profile_updated_by` when `legal_form` or `default_language` changes, never for the catch-up columns |
| `legal_documents_freeze` → `app.freeze_legal_document()` | before update on `legal_documents` | A published or retired text never changes (`55000`); the status moves only from `draft` to `published` and from `published` to `retired` (`55000`); a published text keeps its publication date and a retired one its retirement date |
| `set_updated_at`, `keep_created_by` | before update on `payment_kind_overrides`, `legal_documents`, `holidays` | As on the manual-input tables |

`public.publish_legal_document(p_document_id)` and `public.retire_legal_document(p_document_id)` (`security definer`, empty `search_path`, executable by `authenticated` only) check first that the caller is an owner with an unlimited scope of the document's tenant (`42501` otherwise, and for an id the caller cannot see, before any lock), then take a transaction lock per tenant, kind and language and lock the document, raise `22023` for a wrong status and take their dates from `now()`. Publishing retires the published version and publishes the draft in one call, and two publications of one kind and language wait for each other instead of deadlocking, so a kind and language never has two published versions; the partial unique index `legal_documents_one_published_key` holds the same rule for the service role.

### `payment_kind_overrides`

A tenant's payment kind for a POS payment method (10b); `payment_methods.kind` follows it and `pos_kind` keeps the POS value. Unique on (`pos_account_id`, `payment_method_external_id`); the method is a foreign key to `payment_methods` of the same account, so a tender without a catalogue row cannot be overridden.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `pos_account_id` | uuid | no | Tenant and POS account (composite key to `pos_accounts`) |
| `payment_method_external_id` | text | no | The method's `external_id` |
| `kind` | payment_kind | no | The kind reports use for it |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Author (frozen) and last writer (set by trigger) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

Recomputing the expected cash of past shifts after a kind changes is not automatic: the owner re-syncs those months with `fnb sync window`.

### `legal_documents`

Versioned legal texts of a tenant (10b): a privacy notice, data-protection rules, Mini App terms, a staff notice and the loyalty and marketing consent texts, each per language. Unique on (`tenant_id`, `kind`, `language`, `version`), and at most one published row per tenant, kind and language.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `kind` | legal_document_kind | no | Which text |
| `language` | text | no | `vi` (default) or `en` |
| `version` | text | no | Lower-case letters, digits, dots and hyphens, at most 40 characters, for example `privacy-2026-12`; the label consent records will store as `policy_version` from phase 14 |
| `title` | text | no | 1 to 200 characters |
| `body_markdown` | text | no | The text in Markdown, 1 to 100,000 characters |
| `status` | legal_document_status | no | `draft` (default), `published` or `retired` |
| `published_at`, `retired_at` | timestamptz | yes | Set by the two functions; check `legal_documents_status_dates` keeps them consistent with the status |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | Author (frozen) and last writer (set by trigger) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

`public.analytics_catch_up_status(p_tenant_id)` reads the pending range for a data member of the tenant: `pending_from` starts at the tenant's first aggregate date, `pending_days` is 0 when nothing is pending, and `can_refresh` follows `refresh_my_analytics`. Each refresh shrinks the range through `app.shrink_analytics_pending` (see "Refresh"): refreshing the newest 92 days and then the remaining days from the history start empties it, a refresh strictly inside it leaves it, and a refresh that raced a later mapping write changes nothing. It reads no refresh history, so the scheduler's pruning of `analytics_refreshes` cannot make old dates pending again. A tenant without any aggregate has nothing stale: since migration `refresh_overrides_and_catch_up` the status reports no pending day for it and its first refresh empties the range.

## Consent and legal records

These tables implement the duties of the personal-data law and the anti-spam decree described in ADR-021: a per-purpose consent ledger with evidence and a working opt-out, data-subject requests tracked against their statutory deadlines, and an outbound-message log kept at least one year.

### `consent_records`

Append-only ledger of consent. One row per grant; a revocation sets `revoked_at` on the row instead of deleting it, so the evidence of both events survives. At most one active row (`revoked_at` null) exists per (`tenant_id`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`, `purpose`), enforced by the partial unique index `consent_records_one_active_per_subject_purpose_key`; a new grant after a revocation is a new row.

#### Append-only rule

The trigger `consent_records_keep_grant` (`app.keep_consent_grant()`, before update) enforces the ledger for every role, the service role included, because it is a data rule rather than an access rule. Every column except `revoked_at` is frozen once the row exists: an update that touches `tenant_id`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`, `purpose`, `policy_version`, `channel`, `granted_at`, `evidence` or `created_at` fails with `consent_records is an append-only ledger: only revoked_at may change`. `revoked_at` may go from null to a timestamp once; clearing it or moving it fails with `consent_records revocation is final`, and a value before `granted_at` fails with `consent_records revocation cannot precede the grant`. `authenticated` holds no `DELETE` privilege on the table, so a wrong grant is corrected by revoking it and recording a new one. Since migration `data_subject_deletion` (#252) the triggers `consent_records_keep_on_delete` and `consent_records_no_truncate` (`app.keep_consent_records()`) also refuse every delete and truncate, for every role, the service role and the database owner included, except inside the data-subject deletion (`app.dsr_erase_customer` sets the transaction-local flag `app.dsr_erasure`, which helps only the database owner) and the cascade of a deleted tenant.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `subject_type` | consent_subject | no | `customer` or `staff` |
| `subject_id` | uuid | no | `customers.id` for a customer, `tenant_members.user_id` for staff; no foreign key, so the record outlives the subject row and the two subject kinds share one column |
| `purpose` | consent_purpose | no | `loyalty`, `marketing`, `staff_ops` or `notifications` |
| `policy_version` | text | no | Version label of the privacy notice the subject agreed to |
| `channel` | text | no | Where the consent was captured: `mini_app`, `oa_form`, `till`, `web`, … |
| `granted_at` | timestamptz | no | Moment of consent |
| `revoked_at` | timestamptz | yes | Moment of withdrawal; must not precede `granted_at`; null while active |
| `evidence` | jsonb | yes | Proof of the act, for example the form payload, a checkbox state or a signed-notice reference |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation |

Indexes: (`tenant_id`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`, `purpose`) and the partial unique index above.

A `notifications` row is written only by the `security definer` functions that run at the member's own acceptance (`app.ensure_notifications_consent`, called by `app.accept_channel_link` and `app.confirm_email_link`) and revoked only by `app.withdraw_notifications` and the CLI: the restrictive policies `consent_records_notifications_not_inserted_from_browser` and `consent_records_notifications_not_updated_from_browser` keep every browser role, owners included, from inserting such a row or touching its `revoked_at`, so no manager can forge a member's consent or silence the owner's digests. A `staff_ops` row follows the same rule since migration `staff_notice_consent` (#212): it is written only by `app.record_staff_ops_consent`, at the person's own acceptance of the published staff notice (in the pairing step or through `accept_staff_notice`), and revoked only by `app.revoke_staff_ops_consent`, reached through the person's own `withdraw_staff_ops_consent`; the restrictive policies `consent_records_staff_ops_not_inserted_from_browser` and `consent_records_staff_ops_not_updated_from_browser` keep owners and managers from inserting one or touching its `revoked_at` ("Staff roster, consent and pairing (phase 14a)"). `loyalty` and `marketing` keep the rule of "Who can write what".

`app.has_active_consent(tenant, subject_type, subject, purpose)` returns true when an active row exists. It runs with the caller's privileges, so under RLS a member sees only the consent of their own tenant; the messaging jobs call it as `service_role` before every send.

### `data_subject_requests`

One row per request a data subject makes: access, correction, deletion, export, objection or withdrawal of consent.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `subject_type` | consent_subject | no | `customer` or `staff` |
| `subject_id` | uuid | yes | The subject once identified; null while the request is being matched |
| `contact` | text | no | How the requester asked to be answered |
| `request_type` | dsr_type | no | Kind of request |
| `received_at` | timestamptz | no | Receipt, default now |
| `uses_processor` | boolean | no | True when a processor handles the data for this request, default false; extends completion from 20 to 30 calendar days |
| `first_response_due_at` | timestamptz | no | Filled by trigger when omitted, see the deadline rule |
| `completion_due_at` | timestamptz | no | Filled by trigger when omitted |
| `status` | dsr_status | no | `received` by default, then `in_progress`, `completed` or `rejected` |
| `handled_by` | uuid | yes | `auth.users.id` of the handler (set null on delete) |
| `notes` | text | yes | Free text |
| `completed_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the request was closed |
| `responded_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the first response was given, compared with `first_response_due_at`: stamped by the trigger `data_subject_requests_stamp_response` when the status first leaves `received` (unless given), or by the deletion; set once (`22023` on a change) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Audit timestamps |

Index: (`tenant_id`, `status`, `completion_due_at`) for the open-requests queue; unique (`tenant_id`, `id`) for the log below.

#### Deadline rule

`app.dsr_deadlines(received, country_code, time_zone, uses_processor)` returns the two deadlines:

- `first_response_due_at` is `received` plus two working days. A working day is Monday to Friday in `time_zone` that is not a global `holidays` row (`tenant_id` null) of `country_code`; tenant-specific custom holidays do not count. Counting starts on the day after receipt and keeps the time of day, so a request received Friday 10:00 is due Tuesday 10:00, and one received on 29 April is due 5 May because 30 April, 1 May and the weekend are skipped. The function scans the 120 days after receipt and raises an exception if it cannot find two working days in that window, so the deadline is never silently null.
- `completion_due_at` is `received` plus 20 calendar days, or 30 when `uses_processor` is true.

The trigger `data_subject_requests_fill_deadlines` runs before insert, reads the tenant's `country_code` and `timezone`, and fills whichever of the two due columns was left null. Values given explicitly are kept.

#### Data-subject deletion

Migration `data_subject_deletion` (#252, the database part) carries out a customer's deletion request. `public.dsr_delete(request)` (owners with an unlimited scope, `42501` for everyone else) calls `app.dsr_erase_customer(request, actor, at)` (database owner only), which takes a `deletion` request of subject `customer` naming the customer (`22023` for any other request, one without a subject and a rejected one) and, in one transaction under the tenant's loyalty lock:

- quarantines the pool codes of the customer's held rewards, and settles the path `D` check of each invoice a confirmed reward was redeemed on with a `dismissed` `discount_without_redemption` item, so the reconciliation raises no false mismatch once the redemption is gone;
- erases every membership of the customer with `app.erase_loyalty_membership` (queue items, refusals, stamps, redemptions, claims, check-ins, ledger rows, loyalty requests and the membership with its per-brand Zalo user id);
- deletes the customer's `consent_records` (the only way past their append-only rule) and redacts their `outbound_messages` with `app.redact_outbound_for_subject`;
- deletes the customer's Mini App logins in `app.customer_logins` with their Auth users, whose sessions and identities go by the foreign keys (an Auth user that is also a member or a paired staff login would keep its user and lose only the customer login), then the `customers` row; the customer's invoices lose the link (`customer_id`) and their `customer_phone` and `customer_name` are blanked, those linked to the customer and those carrying the customer's phone; the phone is remembered only as a sha-256 hash in `app.erased_phones`, and the trigger `invoices_blank_erased_phone` blanks the two fields again whenever a later sync or the nightly re-pull brings the phone back (migrations `erasure_blanks_invoice_phones` and `erased_phone_blank_before_rejoin`); when the same phone joins again of its own accord (a new membership), invoices sold before the new membership stay blank and later ones carry the phone;
- scrubs the raw POS payloads in `sale_payloads` (G-47, migration `erasure_scrubs_sale_payloads`): in the records of the blanked sales, every string or number under a key that starts with `customer`, `buyer` or `inv_buyer` becomes JSON null (the POS's customer phone, name and id, the e-invoice buyer's name, address, e-mail, tax code and bank fields), and in every record of the tenant each string that writes the phone becomes JSON null, separators between digit groups included (`app.phones_in_text`; a longer digit run such as an id never reads as the phone), a record whose customer field held it losing its other customer fields too; `external_id` and every other field are kept, and untouched records keep their text byte for byte. The invoice trigger scrubs the record of a sale it blanks in the same way, so a later sync or the nightly re-pull cannot write the phone back into the payload either; the payloads of customers erased before the migration were scrubbed once by `app.scrub_erased_phones_from_sale_payloads`, which hashes every phone a record writes;
- completes the request (`completed_at`, `responded_at` and `handled_by` are kept when already set) and logs the action.

A second call answers the logged action with `already_done` true and deletes nothing. A token the customer still holds opens nothing afterwards, because the customer functions read the login and the customer row.

`data_subject_request_actions` is that log: one `deletion` row per request (a partial unique index) and one `export` row per answer of `dsr_export` (ADR-054, migration `dsr_export_recorded`: who made the copy and when, with the file's counts per table and never its content), with `acted_by`, `acted_at`, `counts` (rows per table, count-only by `app.facts_are_count_only`: `memberships`, `loyalty_ledger`, `stamps`, `redemptions`, `receipt_claims`, `pending_checkins`, `loyalty_queue`, `loyalty_refusals`, `loyalty_requests`, `coupon_codes_quarantined`, `redemption_invoices_settled`, `consent_records`, `outbound_messages_redacted`, `invoices_unlinked`, `invoices_blanked`, `sale_records_scrubbed`, `customer_logins`, `auth_users`, `customers`), both deadlines of the request and `responded_on_time` (the request's `responded_at`, or this action, by `first_response_due_at`) and `completed_on_time` (this action by `completion_due_at`). Read by owners and managers with an unlimited scope, like the requests; no API role writes it.

#### Data-subject tooling

Migrations `data_subject_tooling` and `data_subject_deletion_preview` (#252) add the functions an owner or manager works a request with; all of them check `app.dsr_scope_tenant_ids()` (the tenants where the caller is an owner or a manager with every store) and, for a request, `app.dsr_request_for_caller(request, caller)` (`42501` for a request outside those tenants or an unknown id, `22023` for a staff subject, a request naming no customer or a rejected one). `dsr_lookup(phone)` (POST only) summarises the customers with a phone, never returning it in full; `dsr_export(request)` returns every row of the customer and nothing else (the full phone for an owner, masked for a manager), moves a received request to `in_progress` and records an `export` action per answer (ADR-054); `dsr_correct(request, field, value)` corrects the name on a `correction` request; `dsr_deletion_preview(request)` counts what `dsr_delete` would remove, changing nothing; `dsr_due_soon(days)` lists the open requests whose first response or completion is overdue or due within the window. The alert rule `dsr_due_soon` (catalogue sort order 197, owners and managers, `days` 1 to 10 with default 3) is evaluated by `app.evaluate_dsr_due_soon(tenant, at)`: one tenant-level episode with the overdue and due-soon counts, resolved when nothing is due. Signatures and rules are in [api.md](api.md#324-data-subject-requests-phase-17a).

#### Customer metric functions
