Migration `customer_metrics` (#251) adds three report functions (`security invoker`, reading through RLS) that return aggregates only, behind `app.identified_metric_scope(stores, from, to)`: `customer_metrics(stores, from, to)` (capture rate beside repeat rate, visit frequency, loyalty liability, breakage, reward cost and the member frequency uplift against identified non-members), `cohort_retention(stores, first_month, months, as_of)` and `rfm_quintiles(stores, as_of)`. The definitions and what is null are in [metrics.md](metrics.md#customers-and-loyalty) and [api.md](api.md#325-customer-metrics-phase-17a).

Not deleted here: the POS's own data (the cashier removes the customer from those bills in the POS), a phone written in a payload with three or more separators in a row or split across two fields, the customer's name written as free text, such as a note or a table name (only the phone is searched for in free text), copies in backups until they rotate, and OA webhook events (`oa_events`), which carry no customer link and expire after 90 days.

### `outbound_messages`

Log of every message sent to a customer or staff member on any channel, written only by the messaging jobs. Kept at least one year. Since phase 12 it is also the notification outbox: rows with a `kind` are queue rows with the extra columns, state machine and read policy described in "Notifications and alerts (phase 12)"; rows without a `kind`, such as the two seed rows, are plain log rows the queue never touches.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant |
| `channel` | text | no | `zalo_oa`, `zalo_zns`, `mini_app`, `sms`, … |
| `recipient_ref` | text | no | Opaque recipient reference such as a hashed phone or a platform user id; never the raw phone number |
| `subject_type` | consent_subject | yes | Kind of recipient when known |
| `subject_id` | uuid | yes | `customers.id` or `tenant_members.user_id` when known |
| `purpose` | consent_purpose | yes | Purpose the send relied on |
| `template_ref` | text | yes | Template identifier on the channel |
| `consent_record_id` | uuid | yes | The consent row that authorised the send (set null on delete) |
| `sent_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the provider accepted the message; no default, null while a queue row waits or when it was never sent |
| `status` | text | no | One of `queued`, `held`, `sending`, `sent`, `delivered`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `uncertain` (CHECK `outbound_messages_status_check`) |
| `provider_message_id` | text | yes | Id on the provider's side |
| `cost_amount` | numeric(14,2) | yes | Cost of the message in the tenant's currency |
| `raw` | jsonb | yes | Provider response |

Indexes: (`tenant_id`, `sent_at`) and (`tenant_id`, `subject_type`, `subject_id`), plus the queue indexes of phase 12.

## Analytics

Roadmap phase 8 (ADR-010, ADR-026). Aggregates are ordinary tables refreshed by a function, protected by the same store-scoped RLS as the fact tables, and read by `security invoker` query functions so that RLS always applies to the caller. The metric definitions that these objects implement are in `docs/metrics.md`.

### Hot and cold rule

Every store-day with `business_date` **before yesterday** is read from the aggregate tables; **yesterday and later** are computed live from the fact tables. `app.analytics_live_from()` returns the cut-over date (`current_date - 1`, evaluated in the database's UTC clock, which always covers the local "yesterday" of a UTC+7 store). The query functions apply the rule through `app.daily_sales()` and `app.hourly_sales()`, so today is correct after every incremental sync without a refresh, and yesterday is correct even before the nightly refresh has run. The live half passes the requested `store_ids` down to `app.sales_invoices()`, which reads `invoices` through the `(store_id, business_date)` index, so a call costs the requested stores' invoices of the live days and never a scan over other tenants. Since migration `live_half_lateral`, `app.daily_sales_facts()` reaches each invoice's lines and cash tenders with lateral lookups on `invoice_lines (invoice_id)` and `invoice_payments (invoice_id)` instead of hash joins over both tables; the signature, the columns and their expressions are unchanged. On a local two-store tenant with two years of history, two live days of both stores take about 2.5 ms instead of 49 ms, a 93-day refresh-sized read about 68 ms instead of 86 ms, and a full-history `app.daily_sales()` about 3 ms instead of 50 ms; `app.hourly_sales_facts()` keeps its joins (about 1.5 ms). Item, category, payment, shift, size, modifier, attach and basket aggregates have no live counterpart: they appear for a day once that day has been refreshed (the incremental sync refreshes the days it touched; the nightly job refreshes the trailing window).

The cut-over date is a freshness rule; the **as-of day** of month-to-date figures and forecasts is a separate, store-local rule. `app.analytics_as_of(store_ids, p_at default now())` is the earliest, over the given stores, of the store-local business date at `p_at` (store timezone and `business_day_start_minutes`) minus one day: the last complete business day. Between 00:00 and 07:00 in Vietnam the UTC date still names the local previous day, so a UTC rule would drop the completed local yesterday from month to date; the store-local rule does not, and that day is served live because it is never before the cut-over date of a UTC+7 store. With no visible store it falls back to the UTC date of `p_at` minus one.

### `dim_calendar`

One row per calendar day from 2023-01-01 to 2030-12-31, filled by the migration. Readable by every authenticated user; not tenant data.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `calendar_date` | date | no | Primary key |
| `iso_year` | integer | no | ISO 8601 week-numbering year |
| `iso_week` | smallint | no | ISO week 1–53 |
| `weekday` | smallint | no | ISO weekday, 1 Monday to 7 Sunday |
| `month_start` | date | no | First day of the month |
| `quarter` | smallint | no | Calendar quarter 1–4 |
| `is_weekend` | boolean | no | Saturday or Sunday |

Holidays are not a calendar column because they depend on tenant and country; `app.is_holiday(tenant_id, country_code, d)` is true when a global or tenant row of `holidays` exists for the day, and `app.tet_window(tenant_id, country_code, d)` is true when the day lies between two `tet` rows at most 14 days apart, so a gap inside the Tết run still counts. `app.is_excluded_day(store_ids, d)` combines the two for the tenants of the given stores. All three are `security invoker`, so a caller sees only global rows and the rows of the tenants they belong to.

### Aggregate tables

Every aggregate table carries `tenant_id` and `store_id`, a composite foreign key `(tenant_id, store_id)` to `stores`, a `refreshed_at` timestamp, a primary key leading with `(store_id, business_date)` and an index on `(tenant_id, business_date)` for the refresh's range delete. Sales columns count non-voided invoices only; `voided_invoices`, `voided_amount` and `edited_invoices` come from `invoices.status`.

#### `agg_daily_sales`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date)`.

| Column | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `invoices` | integer | Non-voided invoices |
| `gross_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `gross_amount` |
| `item_discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `item_discount_amount` |
| `bill_discount_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `bill_discount_amount` + `extra_discount_amount` |
| `voucher_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `voucher_amount` |
| `net_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `net_amount` |
| `paid_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `paid_amount` |
| `cash_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `invoice_payments.amount` whose payment method kind is `cash` |
| `items_sold` | numeric(12,3) | Σ quantity of lines of kind `item` only |
| `modifier_lines` | integer | Count of lines of kind `modifier` |
| `voucher_invoices` | integer | Invoices with `voucher_amount` > 0 or a `voucher_code` |
| `member_invoices` | integer | Invoices with a `customer_phone` |
| `identified_invoices` | integer | Invoices with a `customer_phone` or a `customer_id`; the numerator of `customer_capture_rate` |
| `voided_invoices` | integer | Invoices with status `voided` on the business date |
| `voided_amount` | numeric(14,2) | Σ `net_amount` of the invoices with status `voided` |
| `edited_invoices` | integer | Invoices with status `edited` |

#### `agg_hourly_sales`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, hour_local)`; `hour_local` is the clock hour 0–23 of `sold_at` in the store timezone. Columns `invoices`, `net_amount`, `items_sold` as above. The hours of one store-day sum exactly to its `agg_daily_sales` row.

#### `agg_item_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, item_external_id)`; `item_external_id` falls back to the item name when the POS sent no id. Item lines of non-voided invoices only, never modifiers. Columns `item_name`, `base_name`, `size_label`, `item_type_external_id`, `item_type_name`, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `invoices` (distinct invoices containing the item).

#### `agg_category_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, item_type_external_id)`, the text `uncategorized` standing in for a null type. `role` is the `item_type_roles` mapping of the type for the store's POS account, default `other`. Columns `item_type_name`, `quantity`, `net_amount`.

#### `agg_payment_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, payment_method_external_id)`, the text `unknown` standing in for a null method. `business_date` is the **invoice's** business date, not `invoice_payments.business_date`: a tender is attributed to its invoice, so Σ `amount` of a store-day equals that day's `agg_daily_sales.paid_amount` by construction, even when a resync has left a tender dated differently from its invoice. `payment_method_name` is the method's `payment_methods.name`, else the `invoice_payments.payment_method_name` of its tenders (the greatest when they differ), and null when neither names it. `kind` comes from `payment_methods`, default `other`. `invoices` is the distinct invoices paid with the method, `amount` the Σ tendered, `fee_amount` the fee the POS reported, `fee_estimate_amount` the Σ of `amount × fee_pct / 100 + fixed_fee_amount` using the latest `payment_fee_rates` row effective on the invoice's business date, store-specific before tenant-wide.

#### `agg_shift_cash`

Primary key `(store_id, shift_id)`, `shift_id` referencing `shifts` with cascade delete. `business_date` is the business date of `opened_at` under the store's day-start rule. `orders`, `sales_amount`, `opening_float_amount`, `expected_cash_amount`, `drawer_amount` and `variance_amount` (= drawer − expected) are copied from the shift row; `cash_sales_amount` is recomputed as the cash tenders of the shift's non-voided invoices. Also `shift_external_id`, `cashier_name`, `opened_at`, `closed_at`. Because the key is the shift and not the date, the refresh deletes both the rows dated in its range and the rows of every shift whose business date now falls in its range, so a shift whose `opened_at` was corrected, or whose store changed timezone or day start, moves to its new date instead of colliding with its old row.

#### `agg_size_mix_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, base_name, size_label)`; item lines with a `size_label` only, `base_name` falling back to the item name. Columns `quantity`, `net_amount`.

#### `agg_modifier_prefs_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date, modifier_name)`; `count` is the number of modifier lines of non-voided invoices carrying that name.

#### `agg_basket_pairs_28d`

Primary key `(store_id, as_of_date, item_a_external_id, item_b_external_id)` with `item_a_external_id < item_b_external_id`; an item id falls back to the item name when the POS sent none, as in `agg_item_daily`. Over the 28 days ending `as_of_date`, item lines of non-voided invoices, pairs within one invoice, at least 3 `pair_invoices`. `item_a_name` and `item_b_name` are the `invoice_lines.item_name` of each item in that window (the greatest when it sold under several names). `support_a` and `support_b` are the fractions of the window's invoices (those with at least one item line) containing each item; `lift` = (pair share) ÷ (`support_a` × `support_b`). Modifiers never appear. Each refresh rewrites the rows for `as_of_date = p_to`.

#### `agg_attach_daily`

Primary key `(store_id, business_date)`. Roles come from `item_type_roles`. `drink_invoices` are invoices with at least one item line of role `drink`; `topping_invoices` and `snack_invoices` those that also contain a `topping` or `snack` line; `topping_lines` and `snack_lines` count those lines on drink invoices; `attach_rate_topping` = `topping_invoices` ÷ `drink_invoices` and `attach_rate_snack` likewise. A rate is null when the day had no drink invoice, and also when no `item_type_roles` row of the store's POS account carries that role: an unmapped role is unmeasured, not zero. Once the role is mapped, a day without such a line reads 0. Summing numerators and denominators over the measured days of a period gives the period rate.

### `analytics_refreshes`

One row per `app.refresh_analytics()` call: `tenant_id`, `from_date`, `to_date`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `rows` (rows written across every aggregate table). Readable by the data roles of the tenant.

### Refresh

`app.refresh_analytics(p_tenant uuid, p_from date, p_to date) returns bigint` is `security definer` and executable by `service_role` only. It first takes the transaction-level advisory lock `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('refresh_analytics'), hashtext(p_tenant::text))`, so overlapping refreshes of one tenant (the nightly job, an incremental sync, a manager's button) queue behind each other instead of racing on delete-then-insert, while refreshes of different tenants run in parallel. Then, in the same transaction, it logs the start, deletes every aggregate row of the tenant in the range (`agg_basket_pairs_28d` for `as_of_date = p_to`; `agg_shift_cash` also by shift, see above), re-inserts them from the fact tables, refreshes the basket pairs for `p_to`, and closes the log row with the row count. A rolled-back refresh leaves no log row. Since migration `refresh_overrides_and_catch_up` it reads `tenants.analytics_inputs_version` right after the lock; in `agg_category_daily` and in the role lines that feed `agg_attach_daily` a line takes the type of the `item_category_overrides` row of its invoice's POS account and item key (the type's name from `item_types`), else its POS type, while `agg_item_daily` keeps the POS type because the query-time functions apply overrides themselves; after the last insert it calls `app.shrink_analytics_pending(p_tenant, p_from, p_to, version)`. Without overrides it writes exactly what the previous body wrote (pgTAP on the seed, and every aggregate row of the local tenant over 14 days), and its timing did not move (3 days about 0.43 s, 93 days about 0.79 s on the local tenant). The seed calls it for the demo tenant, so `supabase db reset` leaves the aggregates populated. On the seed (two stores, seven days, about 350 invoices) a refresh takes about 30 ms; on a synthetic tenant of two stores with about 54,000 invoices over 91 days a 92-day refresh takes about 1.3 s, far under the two-minute acceptance limit of phase 8, and `month_pace` and `forecast_accuracy` answer in under 0.1 s.

`public.refresh_my_analytics(p_from date, p_to date, p_tenant uuid default null) returns bigint` is the browser-callable wrapper for owners and managers. It rejects a range longer than 92 days, rejects `p_tenant` when the caller is not an owner or manager of it, and with a null `p_tenant` refreshes every tenant where the caller holds one of those roles, in tenant id order so two such calls take the tenant locks in the same order (raising `insufficient_privilege` when there is none). Staff, viewers, publishers and `anon` cannot refresh.

### Views

`v_recent_daily_sales` and `v_recent_hourly_sales` (`security_invoker = true`) select from `app.daily_sales_facts(null, current_date - 45, current_date + 1)` and `app.hourly_sales_facts(…)`, which compute the same columns as `agg_daily_sales` and `agg_hourly_sales` directly from `invoices`, `invoice_lines`, `invoice_payments` and `payment_methods`. Because the views run as the caller, RLS on the fact tables decides what they return; `anon` cannot select from them. They are the reconciliation tool for **every store the caller can see**: a filter on `store_id` applied to the view is not pushed into the function, so each select computes the 46 days for all visible stores (and, for `service_role`, every tenant). Reports read the query functions, which take `store_ids`; the views are for the app's own checks and for comparing live figures with the aggregates.

### Query functions

All `security invoker`, `stable`, with an empty `search_path`, executable by `authenticated` and `service_role`. `store_ids` is always an array; RLS silently drops stores the caller may not see, so a summary over two stores returns one store's figures to a store-limited member.

| Function | Returns | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `same_weekday_baseline(store_ids, target_date, weeks default 8)` | `weekday`, `sample_days`, `baseline_net_amount`, `baseline_invoices`, `baseline_avg_ticket` | Medians (`percentile_cont(0.5)`) over the same weekday of the previous N weeks, excluding holidays and Tết and skipping days without data: of the daily net, of the daily invoice count, and of the daily average ticket |
| `period_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | `days_with_data`, `invoices`, `net_amount`, `gross_amount`, `discount_amount`, `paid_amount`, `cash_amount`, `items_sold`, `avg_ticket`, `items_per_ticket`, `avg_item_price`, `cash_share`, `voucher_share`, `member_share`, `identified_share` | Sums are zero and ratios null when the period has no data; `identified_share` = Σ `identified_invoices` ÷ Σ `invoices` |
| `period_compare(store_ids, grain, anchor)` | rows `current`, `previous`, `last_year` and, for `day`, `baseline` with `from_date`, `to_date`, `days_with_data`, `invoices`, `net_amount`, `gross_amount`, `discount_amount`, `avg_ticket`, `items_per_ticket`, `avg_item_price`, `cash_share`, `voucher_share`, `member_share`, `identified_share` | Grain `day`, `week`, `month`, `year`; previous and last-year periods cover the same elapsed days as the current one; values null without data; the boundaries and the meaning of the `baseline` row (medians, ratios null except the median ticket) are in `docs/metrics.md` |
| `weekday_profile_forecast(store_ids, target_date, origin default null)` | numeric | Level × weekday index taken at the origin: the mean daily net of the 28 days ending at the origin × (mean net of the target's weekday ÷ mean daily net over the 84 days ending at the origin), excluded days left out, index 1 when the weekday has no clean day; null when the 28 days hold no clean day. The origin is `origin`, else `app.analytics_as_of(store_ids)`, and never later than `target_date - 1` |
| `seasonal_naive_forecast(store_ids, target_date, origin default null)` | numeric | Net of the most recent day with sales on the target's weekday within the 56 days ending at the origin (same origin rule), holidays not excluded; null when there is none. Defined for any horizon |
| `month_pace(store_ids, month_start, as_of default null)` | `period_start`, `as_of_date`, `elapsed_days`, `remaining_days`, `month_to_date_net_amount`, `month_to_date_invoices`, `forecast_weekday_profile_net_amount`, `forecast_seasonal_naive_net_amount`, `target_net_amount` | `as_of_date` is `as_of`, else `app.analytics_as_of(store_ids)`, capped at the month end; it is also the forecast origin, so for a future month it lies before `period_start`, `elapsed_days` is 0 and every day of the month is forecast. Month to date covers `period_start` to `as_of_date`; each forecast is month to date plus the per-day forecast of every remaining day, computed from one daily series (the weekday profile once, the seasonal-naïve values in one pass), and is null when any remaining day has no forecast; the target is the Σ of monthly `targets` of the stores |
| `forecast_accuracy(store_ids, as_of_date, days default 28)` | rows `forecast_seasonal_naive` and `forecast_weekday_profile` with `sample_days`, `mae`, `mape` | Every day of the `days` days ending `as_of_date` that has sales and is not excluded is forecast one day ahead (origin = the day before) by both methods from one daily series; only days where both forecasts exist are scored, so `sample_days` is equal on both rows; `mae` = mean \|actual − forecast\| in VND, `mape` = mean \|error\| ÷ actual; null when no day qualifies |
| `hour_weekday_heatmap(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | `weekday`, `hour_local`, `days_with_data`, `invoices`, `net_amount`, `avg_net_amount`, `avg_invoices` | Averages divide by the number of distinct dates of that weekday in the range |
| `item_velocity(store_ids, as_of_date)` | per item: `recent_quantity`, `prior_quantity`, `recent_net_amount`, `prior_net_amount`, `quantity_change_pct`, `net_change_pct` | Trailing 28 days versus the 28 days before, from `agg_item_daily` |
| `menu_matrix(store_ids, as_of_date, popularity_factor default 0.7)` | per item: `role`, `item_external_id`, `item_name`, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `menu_mix_pct`, `popularity_threshold_pct`, `avg_unit_price`, `unit_cost`, `margin_axis`, `margin_threshold`, `provisional`, `low_sample`, `menu_class` | Kasavana–Smith per `item_type_roles` role over the 28 days ending `as_of_date`; the cost is the latest `item_costs` row effective on `as_of_date`, store-specific before tenant-wide. The axis is chosen per role: when every item of the role has a cost, `margin_axis` is the contribution margin per unit; when any item lacks one, the whole role is `provisional` and `margin_axis` is the average price for every item (costed items still report `unit_cost`). `margin_threshold` is the quantity-weighted mean of the role's axis, so it is always taken over one kind of value. `low_sample` is true when the role has fewer than 10 items |
| `cash_variance_flags(store_ids, from_date, to_date, abs_threshold default null, rel_threshold default null)` | flagged shifts with `variance_amount`, `z_score`, the three flags and `reasons` | A shift is returned when its absolute variance exceeds the absolute threshold, exceeds `rel_threshold` × its cash sales (null thresholds read `app.store_report_settings()` of the first visible store since migration `theme_basket_and_cash_functions`, 50,000 and 0.005 without a settings row), or lies more than 2 sample standard deviations from the mean of the store's shifts in the trailing 56 days (needing at least 3 such shifts) |
| `metric_value(store_ids, metric_id, from_date, to_date)` | numeric | One value of a `docs/metrics.md` metric over the stores and business dates, for exactly the metrics that are available now and have one value per store and period (`metricValueIds()` in `packages/metrics`; a test keeps the two lists equal). Sums over stores and days come before every ratio. It reads `period_summary` for the sales, ticket, share and capture metrics, `app.daily_sales` for the void and edit rates and the per-opening-hour metrics, `app.hourly_sales` for the peak and dead-hour shares, `agg_attach_daily` (measured days only) and `agg_size_mix_daily` for the attach rates and the size L share, and `invoice_events` with the item lines of the sales for the removed-line rate (a removal is dated by the business date of `occurred_at`). Null when the range has no sales, when a ratio has no denominator, when no item type carries the attach role, and for the per-opening-hour metrics when a store with sales in the range has no `opening_hours`. An id it does not compute, or a range that starts after it ends, raises `22023` |

Internal helpers in `app`: `opening_minutes(p_opening_hours, p_date)` (`immutable`; the opening minutes of one weekday from `stores.opening_hours`, null when the hours were never entered, 0 for a weekday without spans, and a span whose close is not after its open runs past midnight), `sales_invoices(p_tenant, p_from, p_to, p_store_ids default null)` (the invoice rows with discount columns folded; three mutually exclusive branches so that a store list, a tenant, or neither each reach their own index), `daily_sales_facts(p_tenant, p_from, p_to, p_store_ids default null)` and `hourly_sales_facts(…)` (the live computations shared by the refresh and the views), `daily_sales(store_ids, from, to)` and `hourly_sales()` (the hot and cold union), `summarize_daily()` (one summary row with nulls when empty), `daily_net_series(store_ids, from, to)` (net per date over the stores with the excluded-day flag, the single series every forecast derives from), `weekday_profile(store_ids, origin)` (the level and the seven weekday indices at an origin), `seasonal_naive_series(store_ids, origin, from, to)`, `analytics_live_from()` and `analytics_as_of(store_ids, p_at default now())`. `sales_invoices()`, `daily_sales_facts()`, `hourly_sales_facts()`, `daily_sales()` and `hourly_sales()` declare `rows 10000` so that joins on them are planned for realistic sizes; `daily_net_series()` declares `rows 100`, about one row per day of a report range; `summarize_daily()`, `weekday_profile()`, `seasonal_naive_series()` and `dsr_deadlines()` keep the default estimate of 1000 rows, as do the set-returning query functions in `public`.

### Report functions (phase 9)

Migration `report_functions` adds the functions that the periodic reports Ngày, Tuần, Tháng and Năm call through `supabase.rpc()`; the rules of their cards are in `docs/metrics.md`, section Report conventions. They follow the conventions of the query functions above (`security invoker`, `stable`, empty `search_path`, one signature per name, executable by `authenticated` and `service_role` and not by `anon`), and also these:

- `store_ids` holds 0 to 50 ids, and an empty array returns no rows. A store the caller cannot read contributes nothing. Per-store rows appear only for stores the caller sees in `stores`, once each, in the order of their first position in `store_ids`, so the publisher gets store rows with null totals and no item, payment, promotion or shift row.
- Argument errors raise `22023` with a message naming the limit: a missing date, a range whose start is after its end, a range longer than the function's limit, an unknown grain, a compare range with one bound only. A call that would return more than 900 rows raises `22023` with a hint, so PostgREST's `max_rows = 1000` can never cut a result silently.
- Sums and ratios use the expressions of `app.summarize_daily()`: `avg_ticket` and `avg_item_price` to 2 digits, `items_per_ticket` to 4, shares to 6, and `discount_rate` = (item + bill + voucher discount) ÷ gross to 6. A total is null, not zero, when its range has no data (`days_with_data` = 0).
- Ties on text sort with `collate "C"`, so PostgreSQL and the PGlite parity suite return rows in the same order.

| Function | Returns | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `period_compare_by_store(store_ids, grain, anchor)` | `store_id`, `period`, `from_date`, `to_date` and the 25 columns of `app.period_totals()` | One block for all stores (`store_id` null), then one per visible store, each holding the rows of `app.period_bounds(grain, anchor)`: current, previous, last_year and, for `day`, baseline. Non-baseline rows are `app.period_totals()` of the block over the row's range; the baseline row is `same_weekday_baseline(block, anchor, 8)` as in `period_compare` (sample days, rounded median invoices, median net, median ticket, every other column null). The all-stores block equals `period_compare` on every shared column |
| `period_series(store_ids, grain, from_date, to_date, include_stores default true)` | per bucket and block: `store_id`, `bucket_start`, `bucket_end`, `iso_year`, `iso_week`, `weekday`, `calendar_days`, `excluded_days`, `tet_days`, `holiday_kind`, `checked_days`, `matched_days` and the totals | Grain `day`, `week` (ISO weeks, `iso_year` and `iso_week` of the Monday), `month` or `year`, each bucket clipped to the range, or `weekday` (the seven ISO weekdays, each over the whole range). Every bucket appears for all stores and, unless `include_stores` is false, for each visible store, with the totals of `app.period_totals()` over the bucket. `excluded_days` and `tet_days` count the dates that `app.is_excluded_day()` excludes and that lie in a Tết window of the stores' tenants; `holiday_kind` (day grain only) is `tet` in a Tết window, else the kind of a holiday row of the date, `public` before `custom`; `checked_days` and `matched_days` count store-days with a `sync_checks` row and with `is_matched`. At most 1,100 days and buckets × (visible stores + 1) ≤ 900, or buckets ≤ 900 without the store rows; migration `period_series_store_rows` added `include_stores` |
| `daily_baselines(store_ids, from_date, to_date, weeks default 8)` | per date: `business_date`, `weekday`, `sample_days`, `baseline_net_amount`, `baseline_invoices`, `baseline_avg_ticket` | `same_weekday_baseline()` of each date, computed from one `app.daily_sales()` read and one excluded-day check per candidate date. At most 62 dates; `weeks` from 1 to 26 |
| `hourly_profile(store_ids, target_date, weeks default 8)` | 24 rows: `hour_local`, `current_invoices`, `current_net_amount`, `current_items_sold`, `sample_days`, `baseline_invoices`, `baseline_net_amount` | Current values from `app.hourly_sales()` summed over the stores: null on every hour when no store has a daily row on the date, 0 for an hour without sales. The baseline of an hour is the median, to 2 digits, of that hour's net and invoices over the eligible same-weekday days (not excluded, with a daily row), an hour without sales counting 0; `sample_days` is the number of eligible days. `weeks` from 1 to 26 |
| `metric_compare(store_ids, metric_ids, grain, anchor)` | `metric_id`, `period`, `from_date`, `to_date`, `value` | `metric_value()` of each of 1 to 25 ids over current, previous and last year of `app.period_bounds()`, ordered by the position of the id; an unknown id raises `22023` |
| `item_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date, compare_from default null, compare_to default null)` | per item: `item_external_id`, `item_name`, `base_name`, `size_label`, `item_type_external_id`, `item_type_name`, `role`, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `invoices`, `days_sold`, `net_share`, `compare_quantity`, `compare_net_amount`, `compare_net_share`, `first_sold_on`, `last_sold_on` | From `agg_item_daily`, so never a modifier; one row per item sold in either range. Shares are of the range's item net, to 6 digits. Compare columns are null without a compare range and 0 for an item not sold in it. First and last sold are all-time over the stores. `item_type_external_id`, `item_type_name` and `role` are the effective ones (below, "Effective category and role"), read at query time. At most 400 days per range; ordered by net, then quantity |
| `payment_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date, compare_from default null, compare_to default null)` | per method: `method_key`, `kind`, `payment_method_name`, `invoices`, `amount`, `share`, `fee_amount`, `fee_estimate_amount`, `compare_amount`, `compare_share` | From `agg_payment_daily`, grouped by kind and name (the name, else the method id), so methods of two POS accounts with the same kind and name merge, as on Hôm nay; `method_key` joins the kind and the name with a vertical bar. Compare rules and limits as `item_summary` |
| `promotion_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per promotion: `promotion_kind`, `promotion_key`, `promotion_name`, `invoices`, `lines`, `discount_amount`, `first_used_on`, `last_used_on`, `days_used` | The non-voided invoices of the stores, read through the `(store_id, business_date)` index, with their item lines reached per invoice. `item`: item lines with a discount, keyed by promotion id, else name; `bill`: bill plus extra discount, keyed by `bill_discount_name`; `voucher`: a voucher amount or code, keyed by voucher name, else code; `unnamed` without a key. `lines` is null for bills and vouchers. At most 400 days (92 until migration `promotion_and_shift_functions`) |
| `shift_review(store_ids, from_date, to_date, abs_threshold default null, rel_threshold default null)` | every shift of `agg_shift_cash` in the range: `shift_name` from `shifts`, the cash columns, `trailing_shifts`, `z_score`, the three flags, `flagged`, `reasons` | Scores from `app.shift_variance_scores()`; a shift without a variance has null scores, false flags and no reason. Null thresholds read `app.store_report_settings()` of the first visible store (since migration `theme_basket_and_cash_functions`; 50,000 and 0.005 without a settings row), explicit ones win. The flagged rows equal `cash_variance_flags()`. At most 92 days |
| `item_lifecycle(store_ids, as_of_date)` | per item sold up to `as_of_date`: names, type, `role`, `first_sold_on`, `last_sold_on`, `months_sold`, `quantity_28d`, `quantity_prior_28d`, `quantity_ytd`, `quantity_last_ytd`, `net_ytd`, `net_last_ytd`, `quantity_lifetime`, `net_lifetime` | From `agg_item_daily`: the 28 days ending `as_of_date` and the 28 before (as `item_velocity`), year to date and last year to date (the year rule of `period_compare`), and lifetime; a window without sales is null |
| `store_coverage(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per visible store: `opened_on`, `first_sales_on`, `last_sales_on`, `history_start`, `store_as_of_date`, `sales_days`, `checked_days`, `matched_days`, `unmatched_days`, `unchecked_sales_days`, `stale_days`, `last_refreshed_at` | First and last days with sales from `agg_daily_sales`, from the live days when the store has no aggregate yet; `history_start` = `greatest(opened_on, first_sales_on)`; check counts from `sync_checks`; `stale_days` counts the days from `analytics_live_from()` on whose item or payment aggregates disagree with the live items sold or paid amount, or are missing on a day with sales; `last_refreshed_at` is the tenant's last finished refresh. At most 400 days |
| `store_targets(store_ids, period_kind, period_start)` | `store_id`, `net_target_amount` | The `targets` rows of the visible stores for that period |

Helpers in `app`: `period_bounds(grain, anchor)` (`immutable`; the boundaries of `period_compare` by date arithmetic, plus the 8-week baseline range for `day`; another grain raises `22023`), `period_totals(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` (one row of sums and ratios over `app.daily_sales()`), `shift_variance_scores(store_ids, from_date, to_date, abs_threshold, rel_threshold)` (the trailing 56-day scores and flags of every shift with a variance; `cash_variance_flags()` is rebuilt on it with the same signature, columns, filter and order), `visible_stores(store_ids)`, and the argument checks `require_store_ids()`, `require_date_range()`, `require_compare_range()` and `require_report_rows()`. On a local two-store tenant with two years of history, the slowest call of each report takes under 50 ms.

### Thematic report functions (phase 10)

Migration `theme_revenue_hours_functions` adds the first functions of the thematic reports, which run over a date range, migration `theme_menu_functions` adds the menu functions and rebuilds `item_summary()`, and migration `theme_basket_and_cash_functions` adds `basket_pairs()` and `modifier_summary()` and lets the cash thresholds of `shift_review()` and `cash_variance_flags()` follow the settings. They follow the conventions of the report functions above; thresholds passed as null take `app.store_report_settings()`.

| Function | Returns | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `report_settings_of(store_ids)` | per visible store: the columns of `app.store_report_settings()`, `seat_count`, `opening_hours_known`, `fee_rates_configured`, `costs_configured`, `roles_configured` | The store's settings and whether its opening hours, fee rates (its own or tenant-wide), item costs and item-type roles exist; a publisher gets the defaults and false flags |
| `range_totals(store_ids, from_dates, to_dates)` | per range and block: `range_index`, `store_id`, `from_date`, `to_date` and the 25 columns of `app.period_totals()` | 1 to 6 ranges of at most 1,100 days each, with equal-length lists; a block for all stores, then one per visible store |
| `opening_productivity(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per block: `days_with_sales`, `invoices`, `net_amount`, `opening_minutes`, `seat_count`, `seat_minutes`, `sales_per_opening_hour`, `invoices_per_opening_hour`, `revpash` | Over the store-days of `app.daily_sales()`; opening minutes from `app.opening_minutes()`, null when any store with sales has no opening hours; seats summed over the stores with sales, null when one lacks them; per-hour values equal `metric_value()`. At most 1,100 days |
| `hour_profile(store_ids, from_date, to_date, peak_hours, dead_hour_ratio, busy_hour_ratio)` | per hour of the scopes `all`, `weekdays`, `weekend` with a summary each, then per ISO weekday: `days_with_data`, totals, means per day, `net_share`, `level`, `peak_share`, `dead_share` and the thresholds used | Hours of `app.hourly_sales()` placed from the first store's day-start hour; the open span runs from the first to the last hour with sales, empty hours inside it as zeros; `all` marks the largest hours `peak` (ties to the earlier hour) and hours below the quiet ratio × the span's mean `dead`; the weekday groups are `busy` or `quiet` by invoices per day; comparisons use unrounded values. At most 400 days |
| `menu_engineering(store_ids, as_of_date, window_days, popularity_factor, low_sample_items)` | per item of the window: `role`, `role_mapped`, `item_external_id`, `item_name`, the effective `item_type_external_id`, `item_type_name` and `category_source`, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `menu_mix_pct`, `popularity_threshold_pct`, `avg_unit_price`, `unit_cost`, `margin_axis`, `margin_threshold`, `provisional`, `low_sample`, `menu_class`, then `window_from` and the settings used | Phase 8's `menu_matrix()` method over a trailing window ending on `as_of_date` (7 to 91 days), per effective role, each store's sales taking the role of its POS account: popular when the item's share of the role's quantity reaches the factor × an equal share; the margin axis is the contribution margin when every item of the role has a cost and the average price otherwise (`provisional`); the threshold is the quantity-weighted mean of the axis; classes `star`, `plowhorse`, `puzzle`, `dog`. An item whose type has no role is in `other` with `role_mapped` false; `low_sample` when a role has fewer items than the minimum. Null arguments read `app.store_report_settings()` |
| `item_margins(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per item: names, effective type and role, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `costed_quantity`, `costed_net_amount`, `cost_amount`, `unit_cost_latest`, `margin_amount`, `margin_rate`, `cost_coverage` | From `agg_item_daily`, so never a modifier; costs by business date as below; the cost and the margin cover the costed days only and are null without any; `unit_cost_latest` is the cost in force on `to_date` at the first requested store that has one. At most 400 days and 900 rows |
| `item_attach(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | a summary row (`item_external_id` null), then one row per drink item: `drink_invoices`, `topping_base_invoices`, `topping_invoices`, `snack_base_invoices`, `snack_invoices`, `topping_rate`, `snack_rate` | Non-voided invoices of the range; each item line takes the role of its effective type on the invoice's POS account; an invoice counts once per drink item it holds. A rate is measured only at stores whose POS account maps that role, exactly as `agg_attach_daily` and `metric_value()`, so a store without the role neither dilutes nor feeds it; no mapping gives a null rate. At most 92 days |
| `basket_pairs(store_ids, from_date, to_date, min_pair_invoices default 3, max_pairs default 50)` | per pair: both keys and names, `pair_invoices`, `invoices_a`, `invoices_b`, `window_invoices`, `support_a`, `support_b`, `confidence_a_to_b`, `confidence_b_to_a`, `lift` | The definitions of `agg_basket_pairs_28d` over any range and over the stores together: non-voided invoices with an item line, the distinct item keys of each invoice, unordered pairs oriented with `collate "C"`, kept from `min_pair_invoices` (1 to 50); supports and confidences to 6 digits and lift to 4 on unrounded supports; names `max(item_name)`; ordered by pair invoices, lift and the keys, the first `max_pairs` (1 to 900). At most 92 days |
| `modifier_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date, compare_from default null, compare_to default null)` | per modifier name: `lines`, `share`, `compare_lines`, `compare_share` | Sums of `agg_modifier_prefs_daily` per name seen in either range; shares of all modifier lines to 6 digits; compare columns null without a compare range and 0 for a name it lacks. Each range at most 400 days |
| `promotion_days(store_ids, from_date, to_date, kind_filter default null, key_filter default null)` | per store, date and promotion: `promotion_kind`, `promotion_key`, `promotion_name`, `invoices`, `lines`, `discount_amount` | Over `app.discount_rows()`, optionally one kind or one kind and key; `lines` only for items; at most 400 days and 900 rows (migration `promotion_and_shift_functions`, 10b) |
| `promotion_catalogue(store_ids, as_of_date)` | per promotion: names, `in_catalogue`, `catalogue_active`, `starts_at`, `ends_at`, `first_used_on`, `last_used_on`, `days_used_365`, `invoices_365`, `discount_365` | The `promotions` of the stores' accounts and every key used up to the as-of day; first and last use all-time, the `_365` columns over the 365 days ending on the as-of day |
| `promotion_lift(store_ids, promotion_kind, promotion_key, from_date, to_date, comparator, pre_weeks default null)` | a row per day (series, reference, counterfactual, lift) and a summary row with counts, lifts, `lift_rate` and `flags` | Difference-in-differences on gross revenue, below; at most 92 days |
| `promotion_compliance(store_ids, from_date, to_date, cap_rate default 0.5)` | per promotion, then `invoice_total`: rows checked, largest partial rate, rows over the cap and full rows with their discount and dates | A row is full when its discount reaches its base and is never over the cap; at most 400 days |
| `item_discount_days(store_ids, calendar_year, as_of_date default null)` | per item: price-discount days (line and partial bill), gift days, voucher days, first and last price-discount day | Days counted over the stores together in the calendar year up to the as-of day |
| `shift_adjustments(store_ids, from_date, to_date, z_threshold default null)` | per shift: orders, item lines, voids, edits, removed lines with amounts, edits after close, rates, trailing shifts, one-sided z-scores, `flagged`, `reasons` | Below; at most 92 days |
| `member_share_by_shift(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per store and shift: `business_date`, `shift_ordinal`, `opened_at`, `closed_at`, `invoices`, `member_invoices`, `identified_invoices`, `member_share`, `identified_share` | Below; at most 92 days and 900 rows (migration `member_share_by_shift`, phase 16a, #236) |
| `data_quality_items(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per item: POS type, override, effective type, `type_known`, `role`, `cost_known`, `catalogue_modifier`, `quantity`, `net_amount`, `net_share` | Item keys of `agg_item_daily`; the effective type and role of `app.effective_item_types()`; a cost in force on the last day; at most 400 days and 900 rows (migration `data_quality_functions`, 10b) |
| `data_quality_summary(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | a row for all stores, then per store: line counts and the modifier share, catalogue modifiers sold as items, capture rate, identified customers and consents, unattributed events, the store's settings, receipt days and daily restarts | Lines read laterally from the non-voided invoices; consents by `app.has_active_consent()`; receipt numbering over the 120 days up to the last day, null with fewer than 20 numbered days |
| `data_quality_payments(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per method: account, id, name, effective `kind`, `pos_kind`, `override_kind`, `in_catalogue`, `invoices`, `amount` | The accounts' catalogue methods and every id paid in `agg_payment_daily`; an id without a catalogue row cannot be overridden; ordered by amount (unpaid last), name, id, then the account of the first store asked (migration `data_quality_payments_account_order`) |

`hour_weekday_heatmap()` (phase 8) joins the theme contract unchanged; its callers keep it to 400 days. On the local two-store tenant, timed as its owner under RLS in a read-only transaction on 2026-11-14: `range_totals` over three 365-day ranges 9 to 15 ms, `opening_productivity` over 365 days 7 ms, `hour_profile` over 365 days 19 to 24 ms, and every one of them, `hour_weekday_heatmap` and `store_coverage` under 21 ms at 400 days. The same way, after migration `theme_menu_functions`: `menu_engineering` over one store and 28 days 4 ms and over both stores and 91 days 13 ms, `item_margins` over 365 and 400 days 20 to 22 ms, `item_attach` over 28 and 91 days 22 to 63 ms, and `item_summary` with its comparison over 365 and 400 days 66 to 78 ms. After migration `theme_basket_and_cash_functions`: `basket_pairs` over 28 and 91 days 26 and 78 ms, `modifier_summary` over 365 days with its comparison 5 ms, `shift_review` over 92 days 16 ms.

**Discounts (10b).** `app.discount_rows(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` turns the non-voided invoices of the stores into one row per discount: an item line with a discount (key: the promotion id, else its name, else `unnamed`; base: the line's gross), an invoice's bill plus extra discount (key: the bill discount name; base: gross minus item discounts) and an invoice's voucher (key: the voucher name, else its code; base: gross minus item, bill and extra discounts), with `discount_rate` = discount ÷ base to 6 digits, null on a zero base. Grouped by kind and key it equals `promotion_summary`, which migration `promotion_and_shift_functions` rebuilt with a 400-day limit and nothing else.

**Promotion lift.** For one promotion over at most 92 days: the test stores used it in the window, the control stores did not in the `pre_weeks` before it nor in it, and the promotion days are the window dates on which a test store used it. Gross revenue (`app.daily_sales()`) is the outcome, so the promotion's own discount is not inside it. The reference of a date is, for `baseline`, the median gross of the test stores on the same weekday in the 8 weeks before, leaving out holidays, Tết and the promotion's own days (at least 3 dates); for `prior_weeks` and `sister_store`, the mean of that weekday over the `pre_weeks` before the first promotion day without those days (at least 2 dates). `baseline` and `prior_weeks` take the window's other days (for `prior_weeks` from the first promotion day on) with a reference as control days and, with at least 7 of them, add their mean difference to the reference (the trend adjustment); `sister_store` adds the control stores' change against their own pre-period mean instead. Gross lift is the test gross minus the counterfactual on measured promotion days; net lift subtracts the discount of those days.

**Shift adjustments.** An adjustment event goes to the shift whose id its invoice carries (a re-sync edit noticed days later included), else to the shift of its store whose window, opening to closing or one day without a close, holds its time. Voids count from events, because a voided sale is usually absent from the POS sales report. The void rate is voids over orders plus voids and the removed-line rate removed lines over item lines; each is scored against the store's shifts of the 56 days before with at least 3 of them, on unrounded means and deviations, and only a rate above the threshold flags the shift.

**Member share by shift (phase 16a).** The phone-capture KPI of Ca & két tiền (FR-LOY-01). Each shift of a visible store whose opening falls on a business date of the range (the store's day-start rule, as `agg_shift_cash` and `app.shift_number`) gives a row, numbered `shift_ordinal` by opening time and then external id among the store's shifts of that date, zeros when it has no invoice. A non-voided invoice counts in the shift its `shift_external_id` names, so one sold after midnight stays with the shift opened the evening before; an invoice naming no shift of its store counts in one row per store and business date of the invoice with a null `shift_ordinal`, `opened_at` and `closed_at` ("Không rõ ca"). `member_invoices` counts `has_customer_phone`, `identified_invoices` `has_customer_identity`, and each share is the count over `invoices` to six digits, null without an invoice. The function is `security invoker` and reads `invoices` and `shifts` under their policies, so the caller's revenue scope applies. It never reads `customer_phone` or `customer_id` themselves: the two stored flags are generated by the database from them, which keeps the count working if a later change gives those columns a column grant that data roles lack, as `customers.phone` has (#253). No column carries a phone, a customer id, a cashier name or the POS shift label. On the local real tenant, called as `postgres` over 92 days on 2026-11-19: 138 shift rows, 3,538 invoices, 0 with a phone, in 9 to 19 ms.

On the local two-store tenant, timed warm as its owner under RLS in a read-only transaction on 2026-11-16: `promotion_summary` over 365 days 123 ms and over 400 days 151 ms, `promotion_days` over 365 days 84 ms, `promotion_catalogue` all-time 212 ms, `promotion_compliance` over 365 days 86 ms, `item_discount_days` year to date 76 ms, `shift_adjustments` over 92 days 86 ms and `promotion_lift` over 92 days 103 to 106 ms; `data_quality_items` over 365 days 19 to 24 ms, `data_quality_summary` over 365 days 149 to 153 ms and over 400 days 157 ms, `data_quality_payments` 2 ms.

**Effective category and role.** An override in `item_category_overrides` replaces the POS item type of an item key on its POS account. `menu_engineering` and `item_attach` resolve the type and role per store, from the store's (or the invoice's) POS account, so an item whose type two accounts map differently appears once per role, as in `menu_matrix`; the type, name and source shown are those of the first requested store in that role. `item_margins` and `item_summary` give one row per item: the highest POS type id seen in the range, the override of the POS account of the first requested store that sold the item, and the role of that account (`item_margins`, through `app.effective_item_types()`; another requested store's account when that one maps no role) or, as in phase 9, of the first requested store whose account maps the type (`item_summary`). Overrides are read when a query runs, so a change shows in the next statement; the aggregates keep the POS type until the refresh applies overrides.

**Cost by business date.** Each store-day is costed with the `item_costs` row in force on that business date: a row for the store wins over a tenant-wide row, whichever started later, and a row applies from its `effective_from` until the next row of the same scope (`app.item_cost_periods()`). `menu_engineering` gives an item no cost when any of its store-days in the window has none; `item_margins` counts the costed store-days apart and reports their coverage.

### Data pack and export functions (phase 11)

Migration `pack_and_export_functions` adds four public functions for the AI data pack and one for the proof of an export, with two helpers in `app`. They follow the conventions of the report functions above: `stable`, `security invoker`, an empty `search_path`, one signature each, `numeric` for every number, `22023` naming the limit, `collate "C"` for text ties; execution is revoked from `public` and `anon` and granted to `authenticated` and `service_role`, and each carries a `comment on function`. `store_profiles`, `metric_windows` and `holiday_calendar` join the theme registry of `@fnb/reports` and have demo ports held to the SQL by the parity suite; `pack_fence` and `export_control_totals` are called directly.

| Function | Returns | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `store_profiles(store_ids)` | per visible store in the order asked: `store_id`, `tenant_id`, `label`, `name`, `timezone`, `business_day_start_minutes`, `opened_on`, `opening_hours`, `seat_count`, `e_invoice_status`, the tenant's `currency_code` and `country_code` | `label` is `S<n>`, the rank among the tenant's stores the caller can see by `first_seen_at` then `id`, inactive stores keeping their place; no address, store code or POS id |
| `metric_windows(store_ids, metric_ids, from_dates, to_dates)` | per range and id: `range_index` (from 1), `from_date`, `to_date`, `metric_id`, `value` | `metric_value()` of the stores together; 1 to 25 ids without null, 1 to 12 ranges with as many ends as starts, each at most 400 days; an id `metric_value()` does not compute raises its own `22023`; no rows for an empty store list; ordered by range, then id as asked (at most 300 rows) |
| `holiday_calendar(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per date, name and kind: `holiday_date`, `name`, `kind` | the global rows (`tenant_id` null) of the visible stores' tenant countries and those tenants' own rows of the same country, as `period_series()` reads them; distinct; ordered by date, name (`collate "C"`), kind; at most 400 days |
| `pack_fence(store_ids)` | `text`, a lowercase hex SHA-256 | over the visible stores and their tenants: the count and latest `finished_at` of `analytics_refreshes`, the latest `synced_at` of invoices dated from `app.analytics_live_from() - 3`, the latest `checked_at` of `sync_checks`, each tenant's `analytics_inputs_version` and `analytics_inputs_changed_at`, the count and latest `updated_at` of `item_costs`, `targets` and the tenants' own `holidays`, the latest `updated_at` of `report_settings`, and an MD5 per store of its name, zone, day start, opening hours, seats, opening date, e-invoicing and `active` |
| `export_control_totals(store_ids, from_date, to_date)` | per visible store in the order asked: `invoices`, `voided_invoices`, `refund_invoices` (since migration `refund_business_day`), `sales_net_amount`, `invoice_fingerprint`, `lines`, `line_net_amount`, `line_fingerprint`, `payments`, `payment_amount`, `payment_fingerprint`, `shifts`, `shift_sales_amount`, `shift_fingerprint` | below; at most 92 days |

`app.minor_units(amount, scale)` writes `amount × 10^scale` as an integer in text (`12.34` at scale 2 is `1234`, `-0.5` is `-50`); `app.fingerprint(lines)` is the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the text in UTF-8, the empty text for null (`e3b0c442…b855`). Both are `immutable`, revoked from `public` and executable by `authenticated` and `service_role`, because the invoker function calls them as the caller.

**Export control totals.** Each fingerprint is `app.fingerprint` of canonical lines joined by a line feed, one per row, fields joined by `|`, money at scale 2 and quantities at scale 3 through `app.minor_units`:

| Table | Rows | Line | Order |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `invoices` | the store's invoices whose `business_date` is in the range, voided included | `id`, `status`, `net_amount`, `gross_amount`, `paid_amount`, `business_date` | `id` |
| `invoice_lines` | the lines of those invoices (by the invoice's business date) | `invoice_id`, `external_id`, `line_kind`, `quantity`, `net_amount` | `invoice_id`, then `external_id` `collate "C"` |
| `invoice_payments` | the store's payments whose own `business_date` is in the range | `invoice_id`, `sequence_no`, `amount` | `invoice_id`, `sequence_no` |
| `shifts` | the store's shifts whose business date, `((opened_at at time zone <store zone>) - <day start>)::date` as in `agg_shift_cash`, is in the range | `id`, `sales_amount`, `cash_variance_amount` (empty when null, so positions never shift) | `id` |

`sales_net_amount` sums the non-voided invoices, refund documents included, and `refund_invoices` counts the refund documents (status `refund`, ADR-071), so `invoices - voided_invoices - refund_invoices` is what the daily summary counts; `line_net_amount`, `payment_amount` and `shift_sales_amount` sum every selected row. A store with no rows gets zero counts, zero sums and the empty fingerprint; so does the publisher, which reads no fact. `41_export_totals.sql` pins the fingerprints of a tiny fixture to literal hex that the exporter's tests share.

### Publisher and aggregates

The `publisher` role reads zero rows of every aggregate table, the refresh log and the live views, exactly as it reads zero rows of the fact tables: aggregates reveal revenue, and ADR-013 wants a leaked publisher credential to read no sale. The AI data pack of phase 11 is therefore produced by an owner or manager session in the browser, or by the `fnb` CLI acting as an owner or manager member inside one read-only snapshot (never the service role), and the publisher only publishes the resulting plan. The publisher reads store profiles and the holiday calendar, as it reads `stores` and `holidays`; `metric_windows` gives it null values and `export_control_totals` zeros.

## Notifications and alerts (phase 12)

Roadmap phase 12. Digests and alerts reach the owner and managers through one Postgres outbox: `outbound_messages` is the queue and the one-year log, every send decision is taken in SQL when a dispatcher claims a message, and the channel credentials stay outside the database (rows name an environment variable, never its value). Migrations `notify_consent_purpose` (the consent label), `notifications` (schema, RLS, grants, seeds), `notification_outbox` (the functions), `notification_claim_passes_and_consent_floor` (the claim's further batches and the member consent floor), `notification_alerts_and_digests` (episodes, the runner and job mappings, the `sync_alerts` import, channel health, the digest functions and the second form of the tick's rules hook) and `notification_alert_resolution_rules` (the cool-down after a recovery notice, the previous-day cover by a credential outage, resolutions without a notice when a condition stops being watched) build what follows; the schedule functions and the browser RPCs arrive in the last step of the phase and extend the tick through `app.notify_tick_rules`.

### `alert_rule_catalog`

Global, read-only list of the 16 rules with their defaults; a tenant works with no override at all. Every authenticated role reads it.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `rule_key` | text | no | Primary key, `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{2,40}$` |
| `category` | text | no | `digest`, `business`, `data_quality`, `sync`, `operations` or `channel` |
| `delivery` | text | no | `digest` (a line of the daily digest) or `immediate` (an alert episode) |
| `default_enabled` | boolean | no | Whether the rule is on without an override |
| `default_severity` | alert_severity | no | Severity of its messages |
| `default_roles` | tenant_role[] | no | Roles that receive it, 1 to 4, never `publisher` |
| `default_params` | jsonb | no | Thresholds and switches, an object |
| `params_schema` | jsonb | no | Per parameter `{"type": "number" \| "integer" \| "boolean" \| "time" \| "enum", "min", "max", "values"}` |
| `default_cooldown` | interval | no | 0 to 7 days between notices of one dedupe key |
| `remind_every` | interval | yes | Reminder cadence of an open episode, at least an hour |
| `notify_on_resolve` | boolean | no | Whether a resolution sends a notice |
| `store_scoped` | boolean | no | Whether a store override is accepted |
| `sort_order` | integer | no | Unique display order |

Seeded rules, in order: `daily_digest`, `revenue_below_baseline`, `ticket_drop`, `drawer_variance`, `unverified_day`, `zero_sales_day` (digest lines; `daily_digest` holds `deadline_local` 06:30, `settle_grace_minutes` 30, `skip_closed_days` and `corrections`), then the immediate rules `zero_sales_open` (24 h cool-down), `sync_failed` (6 h), `auth_failed` (critical, 1 h, resolution notice), `sync_stale` (critical, threshold 90 minutes, 2 h cool-down, reminder every 24 h, resolution notice), `sync_dispatch_failed` (6 h), `nightly_missing` (30 hours, 24 h, resolution notice), `job_failed` (12 h), `channel_failed` (24 h), `digest_failed` (24 h) and, from migration `zalo_inbound_events` (#207), `zalo_webhook_unverified` (category `channel`, warning, owners, `max_per_day` 10 from 0 to 1,000, 24 h): an OA whose webhook calls of one tenant-local day failed the signature check more than `max_per_day` times. Digest rules go to owners and managers, the sync and operations rules to owners. Every default passes its own schema.

`app.alert_rule_params_valid(p_rule_key, p_params)` (stable, executable by `authenticated` and `service_role`) checks that every key of `p_params` is a parameter of the rule's schema and that its value has the type and range; a `time` is `HH:MM`.

### `alert_rules`

Overrides of the catalogue per tenant (`store_id` null) or per store; one row per (`tenant_id`, `store_id`, `rule_key`) with nulls not distinct. A null column inherits; `params` merge key by key over the catalogue defaults, and a store row overrides the tenant row for that store. Columns: `id`, `tenant_id`, `store_id`, `rule_key` (references the catalogue), `enabled`, `severity`, `roles` (1 to 4, never `publisher`), `params` (object, default `{}`), `cooldown` (0 to 7 days), `created_by` (default `auth.uid()`, frozen by trigger), `created_at`, `updated_at`. Composite foreign key (`tenant_id`, `store_id`) to `stores`.

The trigger `alert_rules_validate` (`app.validate_alert_rule()`, before insert or update, run as the writer) raises `22023` when the parameters fail the schema, naming the refused keys, or when a store row names a rule that is not `store_scoped`. Writes from the browser are limited to owners with an unlimited scope (see "Who can write what"): the rules include the signals that watch the managers themselves, so a manager reads them and mutes rules only on their own destinations.

`app.effective_alert_rule(p_tenant_id, p_store_id, p_rule_key)` returns `(rule_key, delivery, enabled, severity, roles, params, cooldown, remind_every, notify_on_resolve, source)`: each column from the store row over the tenant row over the catalogue, and `source` the most specific row that exists (`default`, `tenant` or `store`); no row for an unknown key.

### `channel_policies`

Send rules per (`channel_kind`, `message_class`): 27 global rows (`tenant_id` null) and tenant overrides, one row per (`tenant_id`, `channel_kind`, `message_class`) with nulls not distinct.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id`, `tenant_id` | uuid | no, yes | Key; null tenant for a global row |
| `channel_kind`, `message_class` | enums | no | The pair the row governs |
| `allowed` | boolean | no | False refuses the pair |
| `window_start`, `window_end` | time | yes | Allowed hours in the reader's zone, both or neither; `24:00` ends the day; an end at or before the start crosses midnight |
| `max_per_day` | smallint | yes | Cap per destination, 1 to 1,000 |
| `cap_window` | text | no | `local_day` (default) or `rolling_24h` |
| `max_per_month` | smallint | yes | Cap per destination per local month |
| `max_per_week` | smallint | yes | Cap per recipient in a rolling 7 days, 1 to 100 (migration `weekly_promotion_cap`, GA-065); a message sent exactly 7 days before no longer counts; held to 1 or 2 on a Zalo promotion row that may send |
| `critical_bypass` | boolean | no | A critical operational alert ignores the window and the daily cap |
| `label`, `label_position` | text | yes | `[QC]` or `[AD]`; `subject`, `text` or `template` |
| `opt_out_text` | text | yes | Refusal text, 10 to 300 characters |
| `sender_info_required` | boolean | no | Sender block before the refusal (advertising e-mail) |
| `consent_purpose`, `requires_consent` | consent_purpose, boolean | yes, no | Purpose checked for member and customer destinations |
| `interaction_window` | interval | yes | Zalo OA consultation window |
| `api_send_allowed` | boolean | no | False for broadcasts sent from the OA manager only |
| `source_note` | text | no | The rule's basis, shown beside the default |
| `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_at` | | | Attribution, as on the manual-input tables |

CHECK constraints bind every writer, the service role included, so a tenant can tighten a policy and never store one below the floor: operational rows use no purpose or `notifications`, transactional rows `loyalty`, advertising rows `marketing`; `channel_policies_member_consent` requires for an allowed operational row of every kind whose destination belongs to a member (all but `webhook` and `zalo_gmf`) `requires_consent` and the purpose `notifications`, so no override can switch off the consent check of the send decision; `channel_policies_advertising_floor` requires for an allowed advertising row the consent, 1 to 3 messages in a rolling 24 hours, a window inside 07:00–22:00, no critical bypass, an opt-out text and a label or an approved template (Decree 91/2020 Art. 11, 13, 15, 16, 18, 20); `channel_policies_advertising_email` adds the `[QC]` label in the subject, the sender information and a `{hotline}` refusal for e-mail; `channel_policies_internal_kinds` refuses customer classes on `zalo_bot`, `telegram`, `webhook` and `zalo_gmf`; `channel_policies_advertising_weekly` holds an allowed advertising row of `zalo_oa` or `zalo_zbs` to 1 or 2 messages a week, the promise of C12's opt-in text, and the trigger `channel_policies_weekly_default` (`app.channel_policy_weekly_default()`) writes 2 into such a row written without the number (the global Zalo promotion rows hold 2). Both floor constraints treat a missing value as a violation. The seeded operational defaults are 06:00–24:00 and 20 a local day per destination under `notifications` for the Zalo Bot, Telegram and e-mail, no window and 200 a day without consent for a webhook; the customer classes are seeded and floor-checked now and refused by the send decision until phases 17 to 19.

`app.effective_channel_policy(p_tenant_id, p_kind, p_class)` returns the tenant row when it exists, else the global row, else null (the decision then cancels as `no_policy`).

### `notification_channels`

Destinations: one row per place a message can go.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id`, `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Key; `unique (tenant_id, id)` |
| `kind` | channel_kind | no | Where it goes |
| `provider` | text | no | `zalo_bot`, `telegram`, `brevo`, `resend`, `hmac_webhook`, or the unbuilt kinds; tied to `kind` by CHECKs |
| `label` | text | no | 1 to 60 characters |
| `user_id` | uuid | yes | The member it belongs to; required except for `webhook` and `zalo_gmf`; composite foreign key (`tenant_id`, `user_id`) to `tenant_members` with cascade, so removing a member removes their destinations |
| `address` | text | yes | Bot chat id learnt from the member's own message; null for e-mail (sent to the login address) and webhooks; required for an active or failing bot destination |
| `address_hint` | text | yes | What screens show, at most 40 characters |
| `secret_name` | text | no | Environment variable of the credential; `app.notify_secret_name_valid(kind, secret_name)` requires `NOTIFY_` and the kind's prefix |
| `config` | jsonb | no | Non-secret settings (`from_email`, `from_name`, a webhook's `url`), under 4 kB; a webhook URL must be `https://` without user information, query or fragment |
| `timezone` | text | yes | Reader's zone; null means the tenant's |
| `status` | text | no | `pending`, `active` (needs `linked_at`), `failing` or `disabled` (needs `disabled_reason`) |
| `enabled` | boolean | no | Default true |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | yes | Narrowing of the member's scope, 1 to 50 stores of the tenant |
| `muted_rules` | text[] | no | Catalogue keys this destination does not want |
| `consent_record_id` | uuid | yes | The member's `notifications` consent (set null on delete) |
| `linked_at`, `last_ok_at`, `last_error_at` | timestamptz | yes | Link and health |
| `last_error_code` | text | yes | Short code `^[a-z0-9_]{1,40}$` |
| `consecutive_failures` | integer | no | Default 0 |
| `last_interaction_at` | timestamptz | yes | For a `zalo_oa` destination, when its OA user last followed the OA, messaged it or tapped a menu or button that sends a message, as `oa-webhook` reports (#207); only moves forward; the send decision reads it against the interaction window of the `zalo_oa` policies; not selectable by `authenticated` |
| `disabled_reason` | text | yes | `blocked_by_user` (also set when the OA user unfollows, #207), `member_request`, `owner_request` or `consent_withdrawn` |
| `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_at` | | | Attribution |

Unique indexes: one destination per (`tenant_id`, `user_id`, `kind`, `secret_name`) and per (`tenant_id`, `kind`, `secret_name`, `address`). Triggers: `notification_channels_validate` (`22023` for an unknown IANA zone, a store outside the tenant or a muted key outside the catalogue), `notification_channels_cancel_open_messages` (before delete: its `queued` and `held` messages become `cancelled` with `decision = 'destination_removed'`; a message in `sending` keeps sending without a destination), `set_updated_at` and `keep_created_by`. `authenticated` holds column-level `SELECT` without `address`, `secret_name` and `config`, so a browser query must name its columns (`select *` fails with `42501`); only service-side code and `security definer` functions write the table.

### `alert_events`

Alert episodes: one open row per (`tenant_id`, `dedupe_key`) (partial unique index `alert_events_one_open_key`), with `rule_key`, the subject (`store_id`, `pos_account_id`, `channel_id`, each a composite foreign key to its tenant's row with cascade), `business_date`, `severity`, `state` (`open` or `resolved`, with `resolved_at` and `resolution` set together), `opened_at`, `last_seen_at`, `occurrences`, `folded_occurrences`, `last_notified_at` (the stored cool-down), `notifications`, `suppressed_reason` (`cool_down`, `no_destination`, `root_cause_open`), `source` (`incremental`, `nightly`, `cli`, `scheduler`, `notify`, `actions`, or `webhook` for the OA webhook's alert, #207) and count-only `facts` (`app.facts_are_count_only`). Episodes are opened by `app.raise_alert` and resolved by `app.reconcile_alerts` (see "Episodes and alerts" below).

### `digests`

One daily digest per (`tenant_id`, `business_date`, `scope_key`), where `scope_key` is the md5 of the scope's sorted store ids: `store_ids`, `all_stores`, `state` (`built` with its model, version, fingerprint, trigger, data state and `built_at`, or `failed` with `attempts` and `next_attempt_at`), `revision` (0, or 1 after one correction), `built_as`, `revised_at`, `last_error_code`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. The model holds aggregates and store, item and shift names only. Digests are found due by `app.due_digests` and stored by `app.complete_digest` and `app.fail_digest` (see "Digest functions" below).

### `outbound_messages` as the outbox

Phase 12 made `sent_at` nullable without a default and added `created_at`, `kind` (`digest`, `digest_correction`, `alert`, `alert_reminder`, `alert_resolved`, `test`, `link_code`, `link_notice`, `link_confirmation`, or null for log rows), `message_class`, `channel_id`, `store_id`, `digest_id` (composite foreign keys with `on delete set null` of the column), `alert_event_id` and `resend_of` (`on delete set null`), `business_date`, `severity`, `dedupe_key` (unique per tenant), `params` (count-only), `title` (at most 200) and `body` (at most 8,000), `body_sha256`, `decision`, `hold_reason`, `not_before`, `expires_at`, `attempts` (0 to 10), `claimed_at`, `lease_until`, `send_started_at`, `last_error_code`. A queue row needs a class and a dedupe key, and a destination while it waits; a `sending` row needs its claim and lease, a `sent` or `delivered` row its `sent_at`. For a queue row `channel` is the destination's kind, `recipient_ref` is `channel:<id>` (never an address), the subject is the member, `purpose` the policy's, and `template_ref` names the template (`alert.<rule>.v1`, `test.v1`, `link_notice.v1`, …).

The trigger `outbound_messages_guard` (`app.guard_outbound_message()`, before update) enforces the state machine on rows with a kind and raises `55000` on anything else:

```
queued  -> held | sending | cancelled
held    -> queued | sending | cancelled
sending -> sent | queued | failed | uncertain | cancelled (only once the destination vanished)
sent    -> delivered | failed
uncertain -> sent | failed
```

`id`, `tenant_id`, `kind` and `created_at` never change. A row in `sent`, `delivered`, `failed`, `cancelled` or `uncertain` is frozen except: `title`, `body`, `params`, `dedupe_key` and `subject_id` may become null (body purge, redaction), `recipient_ref` may become `redacted`, the foreign keys (`channel_id`, `store_id`, `digest_id`, `alert_event_id`, `consent_record_id`, `resend_of`) may become null when their row is deleted, and `status`, `sent_at`, `provider_message_id` and `last_error_code` change only with an allowed transition.

Indexes: the partial unique dedupe key, due messages by `coalesce(not_before, created_at)` while `queued` or `held`, leases while `sending`, per-destination activity by `coalesce(sent_at, claimed_at)` for the caps, (`tenant_id`, `created_at desc`), and the digest, episode and resend references.

The read policy `outbound_messages_select_for_managers_or_recipient` replaced the phase 2 one: owners and managers with an unlimited scope read every row of the tenant, a store-limited owner or manager reads the rows of their stores, and every member reads the messages to their own destinations (`app.current_user_channel_ids()`).

### Tables in `app`

| Table | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| `app.notification_link_codes` | One pending code or acceptance per destination, stored only as a hash: `sha256(secret_name:CODE)` for a bot code (`bot_link`, 15 minutes), `sha256(channel_id:code)` for an e-mail code (`email_confirm`, 15 minutes, at most 5 attempts), `sha256(secret_name:chat id)` for an acceptance waiting 24 hours (`bot_consent`, keeping the code's issue time); `notice_sent_at` records when the notice reached the member |
| `app.bot_poll_state` | Per bot secret name: the Telegram offset, the bot's display name, the polling lease (`poller`, `poll_lease_until`), and for a Zalo Bot in webhook mode (#207) `webhook_secret_sha256`, the SHA-256 of the secret token `bot-webhook` checks (unique; the token itself is never stored), with `webhook_set_at`, both set together and only for `zalo_bot`; `app.set_bot_webhook` sets them and `app.take_poll_lease` then refuses the bot; no update content |
| `app.notify_settings` | Single row: the schedule (`enabled` false until enabled, `function_url`, `request_timeout_ms`, `ticket_lifetime`), `app_base_url`, `retention_days` (default 400, 366 to 1,100), `body_retention_days` (default 90, 31 to 400), the last tick and its count-only summary, the last dispatch and the `NOTIFY_` names (never values) each runtime reported, unclaimed-ticket counters, `last_housekeeping_at` |
| `app.notify_tickets` | ADR-028 single-use tickets for the `notify` Edge Function (`schedule`, `kick`, `continue`, `manual`), with the `pg_net` request id once posted |
| `app.notification_link_starts` | The rate limit of `start_bot_link` (#229): per member (`tenant_id`, `user_id`, a composite foreign key to `tenant_members` with cascade) the start of the current one-hour window and the codes started in it, 1 to 5; nothing else about the member |

All five are revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`.

### Functions in `app`

Every function sets an empty `search_path`, is revoked from `public` and executable by `postgres` only, except the check helpers `channel_kind_available`, `notify_secret_name_valid`, `facts_are_count_only`, the parameter check `alert_rule_params_valid` and `current_user_channel_ids`, which `authenticated` and `service_role` execute because constraints, the invoker trigger and a policy call them. Every function that scans all tenants takes `p_tenant_id uuid default null` last, so a test never touches another tenant.

| Function | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `channel_kind_available(kind)` | True for `zalo_bot`, `email`, `telegram`, `webhook` |
| `notify_secret_name_valid(kind, name)` | `NOTIFY_` plus the kind's prefix: `NOTIFY_ZALO_BOT_TOKEN`, `NOTIFY_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `NOTIFY_EMAIL_API_KEY`, `NOTIFY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, each with an optional `_SUFFIX` |
| `facts_are_count_only(jsonb)` | At most 24 snake_case keys holding numbers, booleans, nulls or short lower-case tokens, so no error text, name, address or URL reaches an episode or message parameters |
| `current_user_channel_ids()` | The caller's own destinations; `security definer` |
| `tenant_has_destinations(tenant)` | An active or failing, enabled destination exists |
| `enqueue_message(tenant, channel, kind, template_ref, dedupe_key, params, title, body, severity, store, business_date, digest, alert_event, expires_at, at) returns uuid` | The one insert path: a queued operational message created at `at`, its subject, purpose and consent from the destination and policy, expiring after its kind's default (alerts 24 h, digests 36 h, tests, notices and confirmations 1 h, link codes 15 minutes) when `expires_at` is null; a repeated dedupe key returns the first id |
| `next_window_start(start, end, zone, at) returns timestamptz` | `at` inside the window, else the next opening in the zone |
| `message_decision(message, at) returns table (decision, release_at, reason)` | First match: cancel `expired`; cancel `resolved_before_send` (an alert of a resolved, not instant, episode); cancel `destination_inactive` (missing, disabled, switched off, or pending for anything but `link_code` and `link_notice`); cancel `unavailable`; cancel `class_not_enabled`; cancel `no_policy`; cancel `not_allowed` or `manual_only`; cancel `no_consent` (member destinations, link code and notice excepted); cancel `unsubscribed` (muted, or the rule no longer enabled for the tenant or store); send test and link messages; hold `outside_hours` until the next opening in the reader's zone; hold `daily_cap` (messages `sending`, `sent`, `delivered` or `uncertain` since local midnight, or in the last 24 hours) until the window opening after the next period or after the oldest counted message turns 24 hours; hold `monthly_cap`; else send. `critical_bypass` lets a critical alert past the window and the daily cap |
| `claim_next_message(worker, secret_names, lease default 2 minutes, at default now(), tenant)` | Locks due rows with `skip locked` in batches of 20, re-takes the decision under a per-destination advisory lock, holds or cancels on the way, and returns at most one message as `sending` with what the adapter needs (address or the member's login e-mail, config, names, zone, rule); a batch that only held rows until later or cancelled them is followed by the next, at most 10 batches a call, so a due message behind 20 held or cancelled ones is claimed in the same call |
| `begin_send(message, claimed_at, title, body, body_sha256, at) returns table (ok, title, body)` | Stores the render on the first attempt only and returns the stored text; marks `send_started_at`; `ok` false for a lost claim |
