| `finish_send(message, claimed_at, outcome, scope, code, provider_message_id, retry_after_seconds, idempotent, at) returns text` | `sent`; `retry` to `queued` after the back-off, `failed` after five attempts; `failed`; `uncertain`, or `queued` again on an idempotent provider; a late `sent` turns `uncertain` into `sent`; a vanished destination turns a retry into `cancelled`; updates the destination's health, and a destination or channel failure marks an active destination `failing` (`disabled` with `blocked_by_user`) |
| `notify_backoff(attempt, retry_after_seconds, seed) returns interval` | 1, 5, 30, 120, 360 minutes plus 0–20 % jitter from the message id, never under retry-after |
| `recover_message_leases(at, tenant) returns integer` | Expired leases: never started to `queued`; started on `resend` or `hmac_webhook` to `queued`; started elsewhere to `uncertain`; `cancelled` when the destination vanished and no duplicate can arise |
| `expire_messages(at, tenant) returns integer` | Cancels waiting rows past `expires_at` as `expired`, or `no_dispatcher_secret` when no runtime reported the destination's secret name in 48 hours; clears the text of expired link codes |
| `record_dispatch(at, runtime, secret_names, counts)` | Stores the dispatch time, the `NOTIFY_` names per runtime and count-only counts |
| `issue_link_code(channel, purpose, at) returns text` | `FNB-` plus 8 characters (bot) or 6 digits (e-mail), returned once, stored as a hash |
| `complete_channel_link(kind, secret_name, code, address, hint, at) returns uuid` | A matching code stores the chat id on the pending destination, replaces the code by a 24-hour acceptance and enqueues one `link_notice` |
| `accept_channel_link(kind, secret_name, address, at) returns uuid` | After the notice was sent, the member's reply activates the destination, records the consent (`method = 'bot_reply'`, `notice_channel = 'bot_chat'`) and enqueues one `link_confirmation` |
| `confirm_email_link(channel, code, at) returns boolean` | The handed-back code activates the destination with `method = 'email_code'`; the fifth wrong code deletes it; the code leaves the log |
| `ensure_notifications_consent(tenant, user, channel, evidence, at) returns uuid` | The active row or a new one (`policy_version = 'ops-notifications-v1'`), keeping only the evidence keys `method`, `notice_version`, `notice_channel`, `channel_id`, `code_issued_at`, `notice_sent_at`, `accepted_at`; `security definer` |
| `withdraw_notifications(tenant, user, at) returns integer` | Revokes the consent, cancels the member's open messages, deletes destinations still pending a link and disables the others as `consent_withdrawn` |
| `take_poll_lease(secret_name, kind, worker, lease, at)`, `release_poll_lease(secret_name, worker)` | One `getUpdates` consumer per bot token |
| `redact_outbound_for_subject(tenant, subject_type, subject) returns integer` | Clears title, body, params and subject, sets `recipient_ref = 'redacted'`, cancels waiting rows; skips rows being sent |
| `notify_housekeeping(at, tenant) returns jsonb` | See retention below |
| `issue_notify_ticket(reason default 'manual')`, `post_notify_ticket(reason)`, `claim_notify_ticket(ticket)`, `kick_notify(reason default 'kick') returns boolean` | Tickets; a kick posts only when the schedule is enabled and no ticket was issued in the last 30 seconds, and returns false otherwise |
| `notify_tick(at default now(), post default true, tenant) returns jsonb` | Drops expired tickets (counting posted ones as unclaimed), recovers leases, expires messages, returns early when posting while disabled, runs `notify_tick_rules`, housekeeping every 20 hours, counts due messages and pending bot links, posts a `schedule` ticket when work is due and none was issued in 4 minutes, and records a count-only summary; a tenant filter leaves the shared state alone |
| `notify_tick_rules(at, tenant) returns jsonb` | The hook later steps redefine with `create or replace`; its first form returned `{}`, its second form (migration `notification_alerts_and_digests`) runs `reconcile_alerts`, `import_sync_alerts` and `evaluate_channel_health` in that order and counts `due_digests`, returning `{reconciled, imported, channel_failed, due_digests}`; the tick posts a ticket when `due_digests` is above 0 |

The trigger `tenant_members_redact_notifications` (`app.redact_member_notifications()`, after delete, `security definer`) redacts a removed member's rows at once (PDPL Art. 25(2)(c)); their destinations go with the composite foreign key, the consent ledger stays.

### Member notification functions (#229)

Migration `member_notification_settings` gives a member the staff app's "Nhận thông báo" over their own notifications. The five functions in `public` are `security definer` with an empty `search_path`, executable by `authenticated` only, check the caller with `auth.uid()` and act on the caller's own rows; [`api.md`](api.md#35-notifications) section 3.5 gives their arguments, answers and errors. No owner or manager acts for another member through them, and none returns an address, a chat id, a secret name or a configuration value.

| Function | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `public.my_notifications(tenant) returns jsonb` | The caller's `notifications` consent (`active`, `withdrawn` or `none`), their destinations (the 20 readable columns' meaning, plus the link stage `code` or `acceptance` and the expiry of a pending one), the catalogue rules their role receives or they muted, the notice `ops-notifications-v1` with the tenant's name, and the Bot's display name |
| `public.start_bot_link(tenant, notice_version) returns jsonb` | After the app showed the notice: reuses the caller's pending Zalo Bot destination, re-opens one they stopped, or creates a pending one on the Bot of `app.member_bot_secret_name`; issues a 15-minute single-use code through `app.issue_link_code` (returned once, stored as a hash); at most 5 an hour (`app.notification_link_starts`); records no consent: `app.complete_channel_link` and `app.accept_channel_link` do, on the member's "ĐỒNG Ý" in the Bot chat |
| `public.set_my_muted_rules(channel, rules) returns text[]` | Replaces the `muted_rules` of the caller's own destination with catalogue keys, without duplicates, in catalogue order |
| `public.stop_my_notifications(tenant) returns integer` | `app.withdraw_notifications` for the caller: consent revoked, waiting messages cancelled (`no_consent`), pending destinations deleted, the others disabled as `consent_withdrawn`; `staff_ops`, the membership, the roster row and the Zalo pairing untouched |
| `public.my_follow_oa(tenant) returns text` | The OA id for the SDK's `followOA`: the OA of the brand whose Zalo App the caller last signed in through, else the one OA of the brands meeting their store scope, else null; `oas` stays readable by owners and managers only |
| `app.notification_member_role(tenant, user) returns tenant_role` | The member's role, `42501` for anyone who is not a member; revoked from every API role |
| `app.bot_link_notice_text(tenant) returns text` | The notice `ops-notifications-v1` for a Zalo Bot, word for word `NOTICE_TEMPLATE` of `@fnb/notify` with the tenant's name, NFC; revoked from every API role |
| `app.member_bot_secret_name(tenant, user) returns text` | The Bot a member links to: their own Zalo Bot destination's (pending first), else the one most linked members of the tenant use, else `NOTIFY_ZALO_BOT_TOKEN`; a name, never a token; revoked from every API role |

### Episodes and alerts

Migrations `notification_alerts_and_digests` and `notification_alert_resolution_rules` (which replaces `raise_alert`, `record_runner_notification` and `reconcile_alerts` with `create or replace`, same signatures). Immediate rules become episodes in `alert_events` under the dedupe key `<rule>:<subject>`; digest rules never do (they are lines of the digest). Every function below is executable by `postgres` only and takes a tenant filter where it scans.

| Function | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `raise_alert(tenant, rule_key, subject_key, source, occurred_at, store, account, channel, business_date, facts, severity, instant) returns table (event_id, outcome, messages)` | One reading of an immediate rule, serialised by an advisory lock on the key: `ignored_disabled` when the effective rule is off; `folded` into the account's `auth_failed` episode when `root_cause_event` finds one; `repeated` on the open episode (occurrences, last seen, merged facts), or `reminded` once `remind_every` has passed since the last notice; otherwise `opened`: silent inside the cool-down of the key's last notice (`suppressed_reason = 'cool_down'`, keeping that notice time) unless an `alert_resolved` message of the key was queued, held, sending, sent, delivered or uncertain after that notice (the owner was or will be told it recovered, so its return is announced), else fanned out (`no_destination` when nothing receives it); `instant` resolves it at once (`resolution = 'instant'`). `22023` for an unknown or digest rule, a subject that is not lower-case tokens, or facts that are not count-only |
| `root_cause_event(tenant, rule_key, store, account, at) returns uuid` | For `sync_stale`, `sync_failed`, `sync_dispatch_failed` and `zero_sales_open`: the account's (or the store's account's) `auth_failed` episode that is open or resolved less than 30 minutes before `at` |
| `alert_destinations(tenant, store, account, rule_key, exclude_channel) returns table (channel_id)` | Active or failing, enabled destinations not muting the rule: a member's when their role is in the effective rule's roles and both the member's and the destination's store scope cover the episode (the store; one active store of the account; an unlimited member for a tenant episode), a webhook when its `store_ids` cover it; `exclude_channel` leaves out a failing destination and adds its member's other destinations (`channel_failed`) |
| `enqueue_alert_messages(event, kind, at) returns integer` | One `alert`, `alert_reminder` or `alert_resolved` message per destination: dedupe key `alert:<event>:<channel>:open`, `:remind:<n>` or `:resolved`, template `alert.<rule>.v1`, `alert.<rule>.reminder.v1` or `alert.<rule>.resolved.v1`, expiring after 24 hours; a resolution only where the opening message was `sent` or `delivered`; calls `kick_notify('kick')` when it queued anything |
| `record_runner_notification(kind, severity, tenant, account, store, run, facts, occurred_at, source) returns table (event_id, outcome, messages)` | The phase 7 Notifier mapping (`OutboxNotifier`), see below; `22023` for a store or account outside the tenant, a store of another account, a run of another tenant, an unknown kind or a malformed previous-day date; the runner's label, message and severity are not stored |
| `reconcile_alerts(at, tenant) returns integer` | Resolves open episodes whose condition cleared or is no longer watched (`superseded`, never with a notice), sends the resolution notice of `auth_failed`, `sync_stale` and `nightly_missing` (rules with `notify_on_resolve`) for `recovered` and `cleared` resolutions, and sends the reminder of an episode still open once `remind_every` passed, unless a root cause is open; returns the episodes resolved or reminded |
| `import_sync_alerts(at, tenant) returns integer` | Phase 7's open `app.sync_alerts` rows of tenants with destinations: `sync_stale` over the tenant's `threshold_minutes` becomes a reading of `sync_stale:store:<id>` (the runners' key) unless a successful sync ended after the row's reading or the gap at `at` is within the threshold; `dispatch_failed` with at least `min_occurrences` becomes `sync_dispatch_failed:store:<id>`; a reading the open episode already saw is skipped; sets `notified_at` (first notice kept) on imported rows; never reads `error` |
| `evaluate_channel_health(at, tenant) returns integer` | `channel_failed:channel:<id>` once per failure: for a `failing` destination, or one `disabled` as `blocked_by_user`, without an open episode and without one opened since its `last_error_at` |
| `record_job_event(workflow_key, conclusion, run_id, run_attempt, started_at, at, tenant) returns table (tenant_id, outcome, messages)` | For each tenant (or the one given) with a destination receiving `job_failed`: for `sync`, while an `auth_failed` episode is open or a `sync_failed` or `sync_stale` episode was opened or seen since `started_at`, an instant episode with `suppressed_reason = 'root_cause_open'` and no message (outcome `root_cause_open`); otherwise an instant `job_failed:workflow:<key>` episode (critical for `migrate`) under the 12-hour cool-down. Workflow keys `sync`, `backup`, `migrate`, `deploy_web`, `deploy_functions`, `notify`, `workflows_active`, `other`; conclusions `failure`, `timed_out`, `startup_failure`; the run id is not stored |

Runner notifications map as follows:

| Notification | Episode |
| --- | --- |
| `auth_failed` | `auth_failed:account:<account>`, no store |
| `run_failed` of an incremental run (kind from `sync_runs` by the run id, else `facts.kind`, else a store with `facts.stage`) | `sync_failed:store:<store>` once the store's latest `min_consecutive_incremental` concluded incremental runs all failed; outcome `below_threshold` before |
| `run_failed` of a window run | `sync_failed:account:<account>:<kind>` |
| `sync_stale`, scope `now` | `sync_stale:store:<store>` when `minutes_without_sync` is over the tenant's `threshold_minutes`, else `below_threshold` |
| `sync_stale`, scope `previous_day` | instant `sync_stale:store_day:<store>:<date>`, or `covered` when a `sync_stale:store:<store>` episode, or an `auth_failed` episode of the store's POS account, overlapped that business day |

Resolutions applied by `reconcile_alerts`:

| Rule | Resolved when | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `auth_failed` | the account is `active` or `error` again; the account was `paused` instead (`superseded`, no notice) | `recovered`, notice |
| `sync_failed` | a succeeded sales run of the store (store key), or of the account and kind (account key), started after the opening | `recovered` |
| `sync_stale` | no open `sync_alerts` row newer than the store's last successful sync remains, `sync_gap_minutes` is null or within the threshold and a successful sync ended after the opening; `superseded` (no notice) while `incremental_sync_watched` is false (store inactive or opted out, account paused) or when the gap is back within the threshold without such a sync (the watch restarted); `schedule_disabled` (no notice) when the incremental schedule was switched off after the opening | `recovered`, notice |
| `sync_dispatch_failed` | its `sync_alerts` row resolved | `cleared` |
| `zero_sales_open` | a non-voided invoice of the business date, or the end of that business day | `cleared`, `day_ended` |
| `nightly_missing` | a succeeded nightly run finished after the opening | `recovered`, notice |
| `digest_failed` | the digest is built | `cleared` |
| `channel_failed` | a later `sent` or `delivered` message on the destination | `recovered` |

Message parameters (`outbound_messages.params`, count-only) hold the episode's facts plus `rule_key`, `severity`, `store_id`, `account_id`, `business_date`, `occurrences` (the episode's readings) and `opened_at` as Unix seconds, and `resolved_at` on a resolution. Facts by rule: `auth_failed` `store_count`; `sync_failed` `kind`, `stage`, `connector_calls` and, for a store, `consecutive_failures`; `sync_stale` `minutes_without_sync`, `threshold_minutes`, `scope` and, for the previous day, `business_date`; `sync_dispatch_failed` `sync_alert_occurrences` (unclaimed tickets) and `http_status`; `job_failed` `workflow`, `conclusion`, `run_attempt`; `channel_failed` `channel_kind` and `error_code`; `digest_failed` `attempts`, `scope_key`, `store_count`.

### Digest functions

| Function | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `store_day_close_at(store, date) returns timestamptz` | The end of the last opening span opening within the business day; the end of the business day for unknown hours; its start on a scheduled closed day |
| `store_day_shift_state(store, date) returns table (open_shifts, last_closed_at)` | The shifts opened within the business day (`shifts_store_opened_at_idx`): how many are open and the last close |
| `store_day_settled_at(store, date, grace default 30 minutes) returns timestamptz` | The end of the first succeeded sales run (not master data) covering the store and the date that started at least `grace` after the later of the close and the last shift close; null while a shift of the day is open; a scheduled closed day settles at its start |
| `due_digests(at, tenant) returns table (tenant_id, business_date, scope_key, store_ids, all_stores, trigger, due_at, run_as, channel_ids, store_facts)` | Destinations that receive `daily_digest` (active or failing, enabled, a member whose role the rule names, not muted, never a webhook), grouped by scope (the member's visible active stores narrowed by the destination's `store_ids`, sorted) for today and yesterday of the scope's earliest store, never before the day before the earliest link, without a destination that already has a digest or correction for that date; `settled` when every store settled by `at` (`due_at` the latest settle), `deadline` when every unsettled store passed the later of its business-day end and `deadline_local` (06:30) the next day (`due_at` that deadline); skipped when built, when a failed build waits or failed five times, without `sync_checks` history up to the date, or when every store is scheduled closed and `skip_closed_days`; only the newest due date per scope and never one older than a built or waiting date; `store_facts` lists per store `store_id`, `opening_minutes` (null for unknown hours), `close_at`, `last_shift_closed_at`, `open_shifts`, `settled_at` and `day_ended` |
| `complete_digest(tenant, business_date, scope_key, store_ids, all_stores, trigger, data_state, model, model_version, fingerprint, built_as, at) returns uuid` | Under the advisory lock `fnb-notify:digest:<tenant>:<scope_key>:<date>`: inserts the built digest or turns a failed row into it and returns its id, or returns null when it was already built |
| `digests_to_revise(at, tenant) returns table(digest_id, tenant_id, business_date, scope_key)` | The built digests whose figures may have changed: a successful nightly, reconciliation or backfill run of the tenant covering the date finished after the build or last revision and by `at`, within the last three local days; incremental and master-data runs never count; closed to every API role (G-70) |
| `revise_digest(digest, model, fingerprint, entries, at) returns integer` | Stores a rebuilt model: an unchanged fingerprint only records `revised_at`; a changed one replaces model and fingerprint and, the first time and only when `entries` carries a correction for a destination, queues one `digest_correction` per destination and sets `revision` to 1; later changes are replaced silently; returns the corrections queued; closed to every API role (G-70) |
| `enqueue_digest_message(digest, channel, revision, kind, title, body, at) returns uuid` | The pre-rendered `digest` (revision 0) or `digest_correction` (revision 1) for one destination of the tenant: dedupe key `digest:<digest>:<channel>:r<revision>`, the store of a one-store scope, expiring after 36 hours (24 for a correction) |
| `fail_digest(tenant, business_date, scope_key, store_ids, error_code, at) returns uuid` | A failed build: `attempts + 1`, the next attempt after 5, 15, 60 and 120 minutes, `last_error_code` (`build_failed` unless a short token); the fifth failure raises `digest_failed:digest:<scope_key>:<date>` and stops the retries; null once the digest is built |
| `digest_scope_check(tenant, scope_key, store_ids)` | `22023` unless the stores are 1 to 50 distinct stores of the tenant and the key is the md5 of their sorted ids joined by commas |

The partial index `outbound_messages_channel_digest_date_idx` (`channel_id`, `business_date`) on digest and correction messages serves the per-destination check of `due_digests`.

### Retention

| Data | Kept |
| --- | --- |
| Queue rows in `sent`, `delivered`, `failed`, `cancelled` | `retention_days` after `coalesce(sent_at, created_at)`, deleted in batches of 5,000; `queued`, `held`, `sending` and `uncertain` rows and rows without a kind are never purged |
| `title`, `body` | `body_retention_days`, then null; hash, template and parameters stay |
| Resolved episodes, digests | `retention_days` after `resolved_at` or `business_date` |
| Link codes | until a day after expiry |
| Pending bot destinations with a chat id but no acceptance | deleted, chat id included, once the 24-hour acceptance lapsed |
| A former member's rows | redacted at removal; housekeeping redacts any row that was being sent at the time |
| `cron.job_run_details` | 7 days (unscoped runs only) |
| `oa_events` (#207) | 90 days after `received_at`; the day counts in `app.oa_event_days` stay |

## Zalo configuration (phase 13)

Roadmap phase 13, built before the owner's Zalo accounts exist (#8). Migration `zalo_configuration` holds each brand's Zalo App, Official Account, Mini Apps, message templates and staff groups as rows, so a new brand is a configuration insert with no code change (FR-STF-01, FR-POR-12). Every Zalo identifier lives in these rows and never in the repository, and every Zalo secret is referenced by its name, never stored. The tables are empty until the owner enters the real ids with `fnb zalo config set` (#205), which writes them as `postgres` from flags or a JSON file kept outside the repository; the brand settings screen belongs to phase 20a. Migrations `zalo_sign_in` (#205) and `zalo_inbound_events` (#207) add the binding codes, the Mini App sign-in, the access-token hook and the OA webhook's event log, described after the tables.

Rules shared by the six configuration tables (`brands`, `zalo_apps`, `oas`, `mini_apps`, `zbs_templates`, `gmf_groups`):

- Every row carries `tenant_id` and references its parent through the pair (`tenant_id`, parent id), so a row can never hang off another tenant's brand or OA, even when the service role writes it. Deleting a brand removes its Zalo App, OA, Mini Apps, templates, staff groups, identities and tokens, and leaves the tenant's other brands.
- `created_by` defaults to `auth.uid()`, must equal the caller on an insert from the browser and is frozen by `app.keep_created_by()`; `updated_by` is set to the caller by `app.set_updated_by()` on every insert and update, and `updated_at` by `app.set_updated_at()`.
- An id that Zalo assigns (`external_id`) is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`.
- A secret column holds the name of an Edge Function secret, never its value. `app.zalo_secret_name_valid(name, prefix)` accepts a name that matches `^ZALO_[A-Z0-9_]{1,60}$`, starts with its column's prefix and adds something after it, and refuses any segment between underscores that is shaped like a value: longer than 16 characters, or longer than 8 with a digit in it. A pasted secret, token, JWT, URL or numeric id therefore fails the CHECK (`23514`).

### `brands`

One row per brand of a tenant. Every Zalo row hangs off it.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key; also unique together with `tenant_id` |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant (cascade delete) |
| `brand_key` | text | no | The neutral key used in secret names, flags and logs: `brand-1` to `brand-999`; unique in the tenant |
| `display_name` | text | no | 1 to 80 characters without control characters |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | no | The stores the brand covers: 1 to 50, no null. The trigger `app.validate_zalo_store_ids()` refuses a store listed twice (`22023`) and a store of another tenant (`23503`), the array form of the tenant-consistent foreign key |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | The author and the last writer (`on delete set null`) |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation and last change |

### `zalo_apps`

The brand's Zalo App (ADR-017: one per brand, holding both Mini Apps). Besides the columns below, every configuration table has `id`, `tenant_id` and the four attribution columns of `brands`.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `brand_id` | uuid | no | The brand; unique, so a brand has one Zalo App |
| `external_id` | text | no | The Zalo App id from the Zalo developer console; unique in the tenant |
| `secret_name` | text | no | The function secret holding the App secret: `ZALO_APP_SECRET_<BRAND>`, such as `ZALO_APP_SECRET_BRAND_1`; the OA prefix is refused here |
| `status` | text | no | `active` (default) or `disabled`, which tells the sign-in function (#205) to refuse every Mini App sign-in of the brand |

### `oas`

The brand's Official Account (ADR-017: one per brand).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `brand_id` | uuid | no | The brand; unique, so a brand has one OA |
| `external_id` | text | no | The OA id; unique in the tenant |
| `package_tier` | text | no | `co_ban` (Cơ bản, the default), `tieu_chuan` (Tiêu chuẩn), `tang_truong` (Tăng trưởng) or `toan_dien` (Toàn diện); the Open API needs `tang_truong` or `toan_dien` |
| `package_expires_on` | date | yes | When the package ends |
| `verified` | boolean | no | Whether the OA is verified; default false |
| `secret_name` | text | no | The function secret holding the OA secret key that signs webhooks: `ZALO_OA_SECRET_<BRAND>`; the App prefix is refused here |
| `webhook_mode` | text | no | `observe` (default): a signature mismatch is recorded and the event accepted; `enforce`: the event is refused (#207) |

### `mini_apps`

The brand's staff and customer Mini Apps, with the inputs of each one's `app-config.json`.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `brand_id` | uuid | no | The brand; unique together with `kind`, so a brand has one Mini App of each kind |
| `kind` | text | no | `staff` or `customer` |
| `external_id` | text | no | The Mini App id; unique in the tenant |
| `browsable` | boolean | no | False for the staff app (hidden, opened by link or QR code), true for the customer app; a CHECK ties it to `kind` |
| `title` | text | no | The Mini App title, checked by `app.mini_app_title_valid()` against Zalo's naming rules (ADR-022): 3 to 50 characters, no leading or trailing space and no control character, not written in capitals only, no "Zalo" anywhere and no "App" or "Apps" as a word (so "Mini App" is refused while a word that only contains the letters passes) |
| `header_title_vi`, `header_title_en` | text | no | The header title in Vietnamese and in English, 1 to 50 characters |
| `header_color_light`, `header_color_dark` | text | no | The header colour of each theme, `#RRGGBB` |
| `text_color_light`, `text_color_dark` | text | no | The colour of the header title and icons in each theme: `black` or `white`, the only two Zalo allows |

`@fnb/zalo-mini` turns such a row into `app-config.json` and also asks for a contrast of at least 4.5:1 between each header colour and its text colour ([packages/zalo-mini/README.md](../packages/zalo-mini/README.md)). The command that reads these rows from the database for a deploy is still to be built.

### `zbs_templates`

The ZBS template messages of an OA. Rows arrive in phase 19a (#35).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `oa_id` | uuid | no | The OA; (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`) references `oas` |
| `external_id` | text | no | The template id; unique per OA |
| `tag` | text | no | `transaction`, `customer_care` or `promotion` (Zalo's tags 1, 2 and 3) |
| `purpose` | text | no | What the template is for, a lowercase key (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,40}$`) |
| `approval_state` | text | no | `draft` (default), `pending_review`, `approved`, `rejected` or `disabled` |

### `gmf_groups`

The OA's staff groups.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `oa_id` | uuid | no | The OA; (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`) references `oas` |
| `external_id` | text | no | The group id; unique per OA |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | no | The stores the group serves, with the checks of `brands.store_ids` |
| `purpose` | text | no | `staff`, the only value |

### `zalo_identities`

A Zalo user as one Zalo App sees them, bound to an Auth user. `app.zalo_sign_in` writes it when a binding code is used, reached by `zalo-session` through `public.zalo_session_sign_in` as `service_role` (#205), and `app.zalo_pair` when a pairing code or a binding code is used in the pairing step (#213, through `public.zalo_session_pair`); `app.deactivate_staff` and `app.revoke_staff_ops_consent` delete a person's identities in the tenant (phase 14a). A later code for the same Zalo user id moves it to the code's member, and a later sign-in updates `display_name` when Zalo reports another one (a name over 100 characters or with a control character is ignored). No API role may write it. It holds no phone, avatar, location or other Zalo-derived field (NFR-PRV-07), and pgTAP checks that no Zalo table has such a column.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant (cascade delete) |
| `zalo_app_id` | uuid | no | The Zalo App; (`tenant_id`, `zalo_app_id`) references `zalo_apps` |
| `zalo_user_id` | text | no | The user id that Zalo gives this app, 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`; unique per Zalo App, so the same person under another brand's app is another identity |
| `user_id` | uuid | no | The Auth user (cascade delete); indexed |
| `display_name` | text | yes | The Zalo display name, at most 100 characters |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation and last change |

### `zalo_tokens`

A rotating token cache, service role only: RLS on, no policy and no privilege for `anon` or `authenticated`, like `pos_account_tokens`. The refresh tokens are single-use and change at every refresh, so they live here and not in function secrets (ADR-006 allows a service-role-only token cache). The token jobs (#206) are still to be built.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `brand_id` | uuid | no | The brand, through (`tenant_id`, `brand_id`); unique together with `kind` |
| `kind` | text | no | `oa`: the OA token pair (access about 25 hours, refresh 3 months); `developer`: the Mini App developer token pair of the deploys (access about 60 minutes, refresh one month) |
| `access_token`, `refresh_token` | text | no | 1 to 4,096 characters each |
| `access_expires_at` | timestamptz | no | When the access token stops working |
| `refresh_expires_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the refresh token stops working |
| `refreshed_at` | timestamptz | no | Default `now()` |
| `version` | bigint | no | Starts at 1 and moves by exactly one on every update (`app.bump_zalo_token_version()`, which also sets `updated_at`), whatever the update says, so a refresh that read an older version can tell its row was rewritten; read under the single-row lock of #206 |
| `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Last change |

### `app.zalo_binding_codes`

One-time codes that bind a Zalo identity to an existing member (#205). `fnb zalo bind` issues one through `app.issue_zalo_binding_code` and prints it once. Since phase 14a the roster's pairing codes (`app.staff_pairing_codes`, below) pair staff; a binding code still works, in `app.zalo_sign_in` and in the pairing step of `app.zalo_pair`, and `pnpm zalo:dev` uses one to bind its mock Zalo user ([development.md](development.md)).

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `zalo_app_id` | uuid | no | The Zalo App, through (`tenant_id`, `zalo_app_id`) to `zalo_apps` (cascade) |
| `user_id` | uuid | no | The member, through (`tenant_id`, `user_id`) to `tenant_members` (cascade); one live code per member and Zalo App |
| `code_sha256` | text | no | `app.zalo_binding_code_hash(zalo_app_id, code)`, the SHA-256 of the app id and the six digits; unique per Zalo App; no column holds the code |
| `issued_at`, `expires_at` | timestamptz | no | Valid at most 10 minutes |
| `failed_attempts` | smallint | no | Wrong codes sent to the app while this code was live, 0 to 5 |

A wrong code counts against every live code of the Zalo App. At five wrong attempts the next wrong one deletes the codes, so five are tolerated and the sixth burns them; an expired code is deleted when the app is next asked. No API role, `service_role` included, may read the table.

### Sign-in functions and the access-token hook

Migration `zalo_sign_in` (#205). Every function sets an empty `search_path`.

| Function | Security | Executable by | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `app.issue_zalo_binding_code(tenant_id, user_id, zalo_app_id, at default now())` | definer, volatile | `postgres` only | Issues a member's six-digit code for an active Zalo App of the tenant and returns it once, replacing the member's previous code and dropping expired ones; refuses a publisher, a login that is not a member, an app of another tenant and a disabled app (`22023`) |
| `app.zalo_resolve_app(brand_key, tenant_id default null)` | definer, stable | `service_role` | The Zalo App of a brand key, optionally within one tenant: tenant, tenant slug, app id, the name of its App secret and its status; at most two rows, so a key shared by two tenants shows as ambiguous |
| `app.zalo_sign_in(zalo_app_id, zalo_user_id, display_name, binding_code, at)` | definer, volatile | `service_role` | `bound` with the member's profile for a Zalo user id already bound to a current member of the app's tenant; otherwise a valid code binds the identity once and answers `bound`; anything else answers `unpaired` without saying why. `22023` for an unknown or disabled app, a malformed user id or a code that is not six digits. Since migration `staff_pairing` (#213) it runs the consent check `app.staff_ops_gate` before it answers `bound`: a login on the tenant's staff roster whose `staff_ops` consent is missing or outdated gets `notice_required` with the published staff notice instead, and `unpaired` carries that notice too |
| `app.zalo_member_profile(zalo_app_id, user_id)` | definer, stable | `postgres` only | The profile a Mini App shows: role, store scope and the names of the brand's stores within that scope; null when the login is no longer a member |
| `public.zalo_session_app(p_brand_key, p_tenant_id default null)`, `public.zalo_session_sign_in(p_zalo_app_id, p_zalo_user_id, p_display_name default null, p_binding_code default null)` | invoker | `service_role` only | The two RPCs of `zalo-session` and `zalo-phone` over the two functions above, at the current time; `authenticated` and `anon` get `42501`. Phase 14a adds a third, `public.zalo_session_pair`, for the pairing step ("Staff roster, consent and pairing (phase 14a)") |
| `app.custom_access_token_claims(event jsonb)` | definer, stable | `supabase_auth_admin` only | The access-token hook, registered under `[auth.hook.custom_access_token]` in `supabase/config.toml`: keeps every claim and sets `memberships` to one `{tenant_id, role, store_ids}` per tenant of the login, in tenant order (`store_ids` null for every store, an empty list without a membership or a user id); RLS still reads `tenant_members`. The migration grants `supabase_auth_admin` `USAGE` on `app` and the hook only |

### `oa_events` and `app.oa_event_days`

Migration `zalo_inbound_events` (#207). `public.oa_events` is the OA webhook's event log, kept 90 days by the notify housekeeping. It holds no text: no message, no attachment and no other field of the event.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `oa_id` | uuid | no | The OA, through (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`) to `oas` (cascade) |
| `event_id` | text | no | SHA-256 hex of the app id, OA id, event name, user id, timestamp and message id; unique per OA, so a second delivery changes nothing |
| `event_name` | text | no | Zalo's event name, `^[a-z][a-z0-9_.]{0,63}$` |
| `oa_user_id` | text | yes | The OA user the event concerns (the follower, the sender, or the recipient of an `oa_send_` event), never the per-app user id of `zalo_identities` |
| `occurred_at`, `received_at` | timestamptz | no | The event's time, never later than its arrival |
| `signature` | text | no | `raw` or `compact` (which serialisation of the body matched `X-ZEvent-Signature`), or `mismatch` or `missing`, kept only while the OA is in `observe` mode |
| `processed_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the follow-up ran |
| `customer_step`, `customer_step_at` | text, timestamptz | yes | The outcome of `app.oa_customer_step` for the event and when it ran (phase 19a); set once, so every customer step runs once per event |

RLS is on with no policy, and `anon` and `authenticated` hold no privilege on it. `app.oa_event_days` keeps the count-only facts per OA, tenant-local day and outcome (`raw`, `compact`, `mismatch`, `missing`, or `refused` for a call refused in `enforce` mode), with the same composite key to `oas`; it outlives the events and no API role reads it. Owners read it through `public.oa_event_counts(p_tenant_id, p_from, p_to)` (`security definer`, at most 400 days, `42501` for anyone but an owner of the tenant).

Webhook functions in `app`, executable by `postgres` only (the role `oa-webhook` and `bot-webhook` connect with):

| Function | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `oa_webhook_target(app_external_id, oa_external_id)` | The OA rows an event names: OA, tenant, secret name and webhook mode; more than one row means the ids are configured in several tenants, which the function refuses to guess between |
| `receive_oa_event(oa_id, event_id, event_name, oa_user_id, occurred_at, signature, at)` | The decision against the OA's current `webhook_mode`: a matching signature is stored; a mismatch or missing signature is stored in `observe` mode and refused in `enforce` mode, where only the day's `refused` count moves; a stored event id is a duplicate; returns `recorded`, `duplicate` or `refused`, the new row and, for an unverified call, the day's unverified count. Unverified calls of one OA and day take an advisory lock, so their count is exact |
| `apply_oa_event(event_row, at)` | The follow-up, once per event: keeps the follow state of `oa_users` in step for a verified event (`app.track_oa_user`, phase 19a); a follow or any `user_send_` event (a message, or a menu or button tap that Zalo delivers as the user's message; `oa_event_is_interaction`) moves `last_interaction_at` of the tenant's `zalo_oa` destinations of that OA user forward; an `unfollow` disables them as `blocked_by_user`; anything else changes nothing |
| `raise_oa_webhook_alert(oa_id, unverified, at)` | Raises `zalo_webhook_unverified` once the day's unverified calls (`oa_unverified_on`) reach `max_per_day` + 1, as an instant episode under `oa:<OA row id>` with count-only facts and the source `webhook` |
| `set_bot_webhook(secret_name, secret_sha256, at)`, `bot_webhook_target(secret_sha256)` | Put a Zalo Bot in webhook mode after its `setWebhook` succeeded, and find the bot whose secret token hashes to a value |
| `stop_bot_chat(kind, secret_name, address, at)` | A member's "DỪNG" in a linked bot chat: for each member whose active or failing destination is that chat, `app.withdraw_notifications` (consent revoked, waiting messages cancelled, destinations disabled as `consent_withdrawn`); returns the destinations, so the caller confirms once |
| `record_bot_reply(channel_ids, template, status, error_code, provider_message_id, at)` | Logs a reply sent straight to a chat outside the outbox (the stop confirmation, `stop_confirmation.v1`) as one `outbound_messages` row per destination, with the opaque recipient `channel:<id>` and never the text |

`app.take_poll_lease` now refuses a bot in webhook mode, and `app.notify_housekeeping` also deletes `oa_events` older than 90 days and reports `oa_events_deleted`.

**Open until #210.** The exact bytes and header form Zalo signs, the event names of menu and button taps and the Zalo Bot's webhook body and token format are taken from Zalo's documents and checked against fakes only; the device check of #210 confirms them on the owner's accounts, and the owner then switches the OA from `observe` to `enforce` ([`operations.md`](operations.md)).

### OA menus, OA users and the no-app enrolment (phase 19a)

Migrations `oa_menus_and_users` and `oa_form_enrolment` (#276, #277). They give each brand's OA a chat menu and a welcome, and let a customer join from the chat through Zalo's `request_user_info` form, the second way to join besides the Mini App (FR-LOY-25). The database makes no Zalo call: `oa-webhook` and `fnb zalo oa-menu apply` call the OA Open API ([api.md, section 5](api.md#oa-webhook)).

`public.oa_menus` has one row per OA and so per brand (`unique (oa_id)`), through (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`) to `oas` (cascade):

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `buttons` | jsonb | no | 1 to 5 buttons `{action, label, target}` under `app.oa_menu_buttons_valid`: `action` is `join`, `points`, `vouchers` or `open_app`, each once; `target` is `mini_app` (a deep link `https://zalo.me/s/<customer Mini App id>/<path>?src=oa`, with the path `tham-gia`, `the-thanh-vien`, `vi-voucher` or none) or `query` (the hidden keyword `#join`, for `join` only); a button has no field of its own, so no other link is possible |
| `welcome_enabled` | boolean | no | Whether a follow earns the welcome; default true |
| `welcome_text`, `joined_text`, `member_text` | text | no | The welcome a follow earns; the reply after an enrolment through the form, which says how to opt in to promotions from the Mini App; the reply to the join keyword of an OA user already enrolled. Each is 1 to 2,000 characters under `app.oa_text_valid` |
| `form_title`, `form_subtitle` | text | no | The texts of the `request_user_info` form, 1 to 100 and 1 to 500 characters |
| `form_image_url` | text | yes | The https image Zalo requires in the form; needed when `join` is a keyword (`oa_menus_join_form_image`) |
| `applied_buttons`, `applied_sha256`, `applied_at` | jsonb, text, timestamptz | yes | The Zalo buttons the last `fnb zalo oa-menu apply` pushed, their digest and time, all three or none; written only by `app.record_oa_menu_applied` |
| `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_by`, `updated_at` | | | As on the configuration tables |

`app.oa_text_valid(text, max)` refuses a text with leading or trailing space, a control character other than a line break, a link (`http`, `www.`, `zalo.me`) or a phone number (8 digits or more once spaces, dots, brackets, `+` and `-` are dropped). Managers read the menu and owners with an unlimited scope write it through RLS, as on `oas`. `authenticated` holds `SELECT` and `DELETE` on the table, and `INSERT` and `UPDATE` only on the eight configured columns, so the applied state cannot be written through the API.

`public.oa_users` has one row per OA and OA user id seen in a verified event, through (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`) to `oas` (cascade). It holds the OA user id only, never a name, phone, address or message (NFR-PRV-07). RLS is on with no policy, and no API role holds a privilege on it.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `oa_user_id` | text | no | The user id the OA's events carry, scoped to the OA and unique per OA; not the per-app id of the Mini App |
| `customer_id`, `enrolled_at` | uuid, timestamptz | yes | The customer this OA user enrolled as through the form, both or neither. It references (`tenant_id`, `customer_id`) of `customers` with `on delete cascade`, so a data-subject deletion takes the row along; at most one OA user per customer and OA. It is the OA counterpart of `memberships.zalo_user_id` of the Mini App; the tie the outbox uses lives in `app.customer_oa_contacts` (`app.link_customer_oa_user`, migration `customer_messages`) |
| `following`, `followed_at`, `unfollowed_at` | boolean, timestamptz | | The follow state, moved by event time, so a late older event does not undo a newer one |
| `welcomed_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the welcome of the current follow was claimed: one welcome per follow, at most one a day |
| `form_sent_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the last `request_user_info` form was claimed: at most one every two minutes |

Functions in `app`, executable by `postgres` only (the role `oa-webhook` and the CLI connect with). The exceptions are the two pure checks, which `authenticated` and `service_role` may call:

| Function | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `oa_text_valid(text, max)`, `oa_menu_buttons_valid(buttons)` | The CHECK helpers above |
| `oa_last_interaction(oa_id, oa_user_id)` | The latest verified `user_send_` event of the OA user, from `oa_events`: the start of the 48-hour consultation window. A follow alone does not open it; it earns the welcome |
| `track_oa_user(event)` | Keeps the follow state of `oa_users` in step with one verified follow or unfollow; called by `apply_oa_event` |
| `oa_customer_step(event_row, input, at)` | The customer side of one stored event. It runs once per event (`oa_events.customer_step`) and acts only on a verified signature (`unverified` otherwise). `input` is what `oa-webhook` read from the body and keeps in memory only: `{kind: none}`, `{kind: keyword, action: join}` or `{kind: form, phone, name}`. A follow earns the welcome when the OA has a menu with the welcome on, the user follows, the event is under 48 hours old and no welcome was claimed this follow or in the last 24 hours (`welcome`; otherwise `no_welcome`, `not_following`, `stale` or `already_welcomed`). The join keyword inside the window answers `member` to an OA user enrolled as a member and otherwise sends the form (`form`; otherwise `no_menu`, `outside_window`, `no_form` or `form_recent`). A form answer enrols through `oa_form_enrol` and answers the joined text inside the window (`joined`), nothing outside it (`joined_quiet`), or `refused_<reason>`. Every other event is `ignored`. Returns the outcome, the OA user row, the recipient, the customer and, when a consultation message is due, the reply: its kind and texts, the menu buttons and the customer Mini App id |
| `oa_form_enrol(event_row, phone, name, at)` | The enrolment from a verified `user_submit_info` event. It refuses `unverified`, `stale` (an event 48 hours old or older, so a signed answer replayed after its id left the 90-day log enrols nobody; migration `oa_form_stale_events`), `phone_invalid` (no E.164 reading), `no_programme`, `policy_missing` (no published privacy notice) and `phone_in_use` (the phone's customer enrolled as, or is tied to, another OA user of this OA), writing nothing. Otherwise, under the OA's advisory lock, it finds or creates the customer through `app.loyalty_customer_for_phone` (the form's name is kept only when the customer has none), enrols it with channel `oa_form` under the OA's brand through `app.loyalty_join` (the welcome stamps once, none for a staff phone; a Mini App membership keeps its channel and Zalo user id), records the `loyalty` consent (`oa_record_loyalty_consent`), sets `oa_users.customer_id` and ties the OA user for the outbox through `app.link_customer_oa_user`. It never records a `marketing` consent, because the form does not ask for one. The form's birth date, gender and address are never passed in |
| `oa_record_loyalty_consent(tenant_id, customer_id, policy_version, evidence, at)` | Keeps an active `loyalty` record of the same policy version whatever its channel; otherwise revokes the active one and inserts a record with channel `oa_form`. The evidence is `{channel: oa_form, oa_id, event_id, submitted_at, received_at, accepted_at, form_sent_at, form_title, form_subtitle, shared_fields, phone_source: user_submitted, marketing: not_asked}`, never the phone or the name |
| `set_oa_menu(tenant_id, brand_key, buttons, welcome_enabled, welcome_text, joined_text, member_text, form_title, form_subtitle, form_image_url)` | `fnb zalo oa-menu set`: creates or replaces the brand's menu row; answers `created`, `updated` or `unchanged`, and `22023` when the brand has no OA |
| `oa_menu_source(tenant_id, brand_key)` | What `fnb zalo oa-menu apply` reads: the OA, its package, the configured and the applied buttons, and the customer Mini App id (`oa_customer_mini_app`) |
| `record_oa_menu_applied(menu_id, buttons, sha256, at)` | Keeps the buttons Zalo accepted, with their digest and time |
| `oa_access_token(oa_id, at)` | The brand's stored OA access token while it is valid for more than a minute and the package includes the Open API (`tang_truong`, `toan_dien`); null otherwise, and nothing is sent |
| `record_oa_reply(oa_user_row, customer_id, kind, status, error_code, provider_message_id, at)` | Logs one consultation message that `oa-webhook` sent outside the outbox (`welcome`, `form`, `member`, `joined`) as an `outbound_messages` row with `kind` null, channel `zalo_oa`, the opaque recipient `oa_user:<id>`, the customer as subject when there is one, purpose `loyalty`, the template `oa_<kind>.v1` and the outcome (`cancelled` while sending is off), never the text. The data-subject deletion redacts it with the customer's other messages |

### Access and functions

- `authenticated` reads the six configuration tables and `zalo_identities` through RLS and holds `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on the six configuration tables only; it holds no privilege on `zalo_tokens` or `oa_events`, and `anon` none on any of the nine tables. The policies are in "Row-level security" below.
- `app.zalo_secret_name_valid(text, text)` and `app.mini_app_title_valid(text)` are immutable checks called by the CHECK constraints; `authenticated` and `service_role` may execute them, `anon` may not. The trigger functions `app.validate_zalo_store_ids()` and `app.bump_zalo_token_version()` are executable by no API role. All four run as the caller with an empty `search_path`.

## Staff roster, consent and pairing (phase 14a)

Roadmap phase 14a, built before the owner's Zalo accounts exist. Four migrations: `staff_roster` (#211), `operations_rls` (#211, #216), `staff_notice_consent` (#212) and `staff_pairing` (#213). They hold who may use the staff Mini App, what staff read of revenue, the access rules every operations table of phases 14b and 15 will carry, the staff notice with its consent, and the one-time codes that pair a person's Zalo account with their roster row. Every function sets an empty `search_path`; a function listed as `name(…)` with "(`app.` and `public.`)" is a `security definer` function in `app` with an invoker RPC of the same name and arguments in `public`.

### `staff_roster`

One row per person of a tenant who may use the staff Mini App, whatever the brand. No API role writes it: `authenticated` holds column-level `SELECT` without `phone` and no write privilege, and the rows change only through the four roster functions, the pairing step and the triggers below.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key; also unique together with `tenant_id` |
| `tenant_id` | uuid | no | Tenant (cascade delete) |
| `display_name` | text | no | 1 to 100 characters, trimmed, without control characters |
| `role` | tenant_role | no | `staff` or `manager` (a CHECK): the role the person's membership gets when paired |
| `store_ids` | uuid[] | yes | 1 to 50 stores of the tenant, no null; null for every store, which only a member with an unlimited scope may grant. The trigger `app.validate_zalo_store_ids()` refuses a store listed twice (`22023`) or of another tenant (`23503`) |
| `status` | text | no | `invited` (the default: on the roster, not paired), `active` (paired, with a `tenant_members` row of the roster role and stores; needs `paired_at`) or `inactive` (deactivated, no membership) |
| `user_id` | uuid | yes | The Auth user bound at the first pairing (`on delete set null`), kept after a deactivation so a later pairing reuses it; unique per tenant, so one row per login and tenant whatever the brand. The person's Zalo identities, one per Zalo App, are the `zalo_identities` rows of this user |
| `paired_at` | timestamptz | yes | When the person last paired a Zalo account |
| `phone` | text | yes | International form (`^\+[1-9][0-9]{7,14}$`), only once the person linked it themselves (`link_my_phone`) and only while the row is active; no API role may select it |
| `phone_hint` | text | yes | Generated: `…` and the last three digits, what the screens show |
| `phone_linked_at` | timestamptz | yes | Set exactly when `phone` is |
| `deactivated_at` | timestamptz | yes | Set exactly while the status is `inactive` |
| `created_by`, `updated_by` | uuid | yes | The author (frozen by `app.keep_created_by()`) and the last writer (`app.set_updated_by()`), `on delete set null` |
| `created_at`, `updated_at` | timestamptz | no | Row creation and last change (`app.set_updated_at()`) |

Index: `user_id` where it is not null. The read policy `staff_roster_select_for_managers_or_self` lets an owner or manager of the tenant read a row when their scope holds every store of it (`app.can_manage_staff(tenant_id, store_ids)`; a row for every store needs an unlimited scope), and a person read their own row (`user_id = auth.uid()` in a tenant where they hold a data role). Staff read no one else's row; viewers, the publisher and `anon` read none.

| Function | What it does | Refused with |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `add_staff(p_tenant_id, p_display_name, p_role, p_store_ids)` (`app.` and `public.`) | Adds an `invited` row as the caller and returns its id | `42501`: not an owner or manager of the tenant, or a store (or every store) outside the caller's scope; `22023`: a role other than `staff` or `manager`, a name that is empty, over 100 characters or holds a control character |
| `update_staff(p_roster_id, p_display_name, p_role, p_store_ids)` (`app.` and `public.`) | Replaces the name, role and stores; an active row's membership takes the new role and stores at once | `42501`: a row whose stores are not all in the caller's scope (an unknown row gets the same answer), or new stores outside it; `42501` with the hint `own_entry` or `last_owner` when the role or the stores of one's own entry or of the tenant's only owner would change (the name may); `22023` as above |
| `deactivate_staff(p_roster_id)` (`app.` and `public.`) | Status `inactive` and the phone dropped; the staff or manager membership removed (its destinations and binding codes go by the foreign keys, its notification rows are redacted by trigger), the person's Zalo identities in the tenant deleted and every Auth session of the login revoked, so no refresh token is left; the live pairing code goes by trigger; the consent ledger stays; a second call changes nothing | `42501` as for `update_staff`; `42501` with the hint `own_entry` for one's own entry and `last_owner` for the tenant's only owner (`app.roster_lock_guard`, migration `staff_lock_guards`) |
| `reactivate_staff(p_roster_id)` (`app.` and `public.`) | Back to `invited` with `paired_at` cleared: the person regains access only by pairing again; a row that is not inactive is left as it is | `42501` as for `update_staff` |

All four are executable by `authenticated` only, not by `anon` or `service_role`. `app.can_manage_staff(tenant_id, store_ids)` (definer, stable, executable by `authenticated`) is true when the caller is an owner or manager of the tenant whose scope holds every given store; a null list needs an unlimited scope. A store-limited manager therefore cannot grant another store, every store or the owner role, and cannot change a person who also works in another store.

Triggers:

- `staff_roster_set_updated_at`, `staff_roster_keep_created_by`, `staff_roster_set_updated_by` and `staff_roster_validate_store_ids` on the table.
- `tenant_members_deactivate_staff`, after delete on `tenant_members` (`app.deactivate_removed_staff()`): a membership removed by any path makes the active roster row of that login `inactive` and drops its phone, so the roster never shows access the person no longer has; nothing happens while the whole tenant is being deleted.
- `staff_roster_drop_pairing_codes`, after an update of `status` to `inactive` (`app.drop_pairing_codes_of_inactive_staff()`): the row's live pairing code is deleted.

The seed adds three `invited` rows to the demo tenant: two staff and one manager, one store each.

### Revenue for staff

Migration `operations_rls` adds `tenants.staff_revenue_visibility` (see [`tenants`](#tenants)) and sends the read policies of the 16 revenue tables through one helper: `invoices`, `invoice_lines`, `invoice_payments`, `invoice_events`, `shifts`, `sync_checks` and the ten `agg_*` tables now read `store_id = any ((select app.current_user_revenue_store_ids())::uuid[])`. The report functions and `metric_value` are `security invoker`, so they follow the level for staff too; owners, managers and viewers read exactly as before. `sync_runs` is not one of the 16, so every data role still reads the runs of its stores, whatever the level; the staff app's freshness stamp reads it.

| Function | Security | Executable by | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `app.current_user_revenue_store_ids()` | definer, stable | `authenticated` | The stores whose revenue rows the caller reads: the stores in scope of each data role, without those of a `staff` membership unless the tenant's level is `all` |
| `set_staff_revenue_visibility(p_tenant_id, p_visibility)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, volatile | `authenticated` | Sets the level as an owner with an unlimited scope and returns it; `42501` for anyone else, `22023` for a level other than `none`, `store_totals` or `all`; the profile stamps record who changed it |
| `today_store_totals()` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, stable | `authenticated` | Today's net revenue and invoice count of each store in the caller's scope, by each store's own business date, voided invoices left out as Hôm nay counts them: `store_id`, `business_date`, `net_amount`, `invoices`. A `staff` membership gets rows only at `store_totals` or `all`; the publisher gets none. It is `security definer` so that it is the only revenue a staff member at `store_totals` reads; the staff app's home calls it |

### Operations access: helpers and the policy template

The foundation of the operations tables of phases 14b and 15 (#216): four helpers, `security definer` and `stable`, executable by `authenticated` and not by `anon`.

| Helper | Returns |
| --- | --- |
| `app.ops_can_read(tenant_id, store_id)` | True for an owner, manager or staff member of the tenant with the store in scope (a null store needs an unlimited scope); never for viewers and the publisher |
| `app.ops_can_write_own(tenant_id, store_id)` | The same roles and scope: the caller may write their own rows of the store; policies pair it with `created_by = auth.uid()` |
| `app.ops_can_manage(tenant_id, store_id)` | True for an owner or manager with the store in scope: may manage every row of the store |
| `app.current_user_ops_store_ids()` | The stores for which `ops_can_read` is true, as one array that a read policy evaluates once per statement |

Every operations table carries the composite foreign key to `stores`, a `created_by` that defaults to `auth.uid()` and is frozen by `app.keep_created_by()`, and exactly these four policies, for `authenticated` only:

| Policy | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Read in scope | `store_id = any ((select app.current_user_ops_store_ids())::uuid[])` |
| Insert own | `app.ops_can_write_own(tenant_id, store_id) and created_by = (select auth.uid())` |
| Update own or managed | using and with check: `app.ops_can_manage(tenant_id, store_id) or (created_by = (select auth.uid()) and app.ops_can_write_own(tenant_id, store_id))` |
| Delete managed | `app.ops_can_manage(tenant_id, store_id)` |

So staff read their stores' rows, insert and update their own and delete nothing; owners and managers update and delete in their stores; viewers, the publisher and other tenants read and write nothing. No operations table exists yet: `47_operations_rls.sql` builds one inside its rolled-back transaction to hold the template, and fails when any table in `public` whose policies call these helpers lacks one of the four or has another.

### The staff notice and `staff_ops` consent

Migration `staff_notice_consent` (#212). The staff notice is the tenant's published Vietnamese `legal_documents` row of kind `staff_notice`, written and published on Cài đặt → Pháp lý. A person reads it before pairing, and accepting it records their `staff_ops` consent: channel `mini_app`, `policy_version` the notice's version and `evidence` with `method` (`accept_button`), `notice_version`, `notice_channel`, `notice_shown_at` and `accepted_at`, never a Zalo id, phone or device detail. A newer published version makes earlier acceptances outdated, worked out at read time, so publishing changes no consent row. A withdrawal ends the staff session at its next check and leaves the `notifications` consent alone (Law 91/2025 Art. 9). The seed publishes the placeholder `staff-privacy-notice-2026-01` for the demo tenant; it is not a legal text.

| Function | Security | Executable by | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `app.published_staff_notice(tenant_id)` | definer, stable | no API role | `{version, title, body_markdown, published_at}` of the published Vietnamese staff notice, or null |
| `app.staff_ops_consent_state(tenant_id, user_id)` | definer, stable | no API role | `missing` (no active row), `outdated` (the active row names a version other than the published notice) or `current` (it names the published version, or no notice is published) |
| `app.staff_notice_acceptance_valid(tenant_id, version, shown_at, at)` | definer, stable | no API role | The acceptance names the published notice and a showing time between its publication and the acceptance, five minutes of clock skew allowed on each side |
| `app.record_staff_ops_consent(tenant_id, user_id, version, shown_at, at)` | definer, volatile | no API role | Records the acceptance under an advisory lock per person: the same version already active is kept, an outdated one is revoked at the new acceptance; `22023` when the acceptance is not valid |
| `app.revoke_staff_ops_consent(tenant_id, user_id, at)` | definer, volatile | no API role | Revokes the active row, deletes the person's Zalo identities in the tenant and ends every Auth session of the login; the `notifications` consent, the membership and the roster row stay; returns whether a consent was active |
| `accept_staff_notice(p_tenant_id, p_notice_version, p_notice_shown_at)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, volatile | `authenticated` | The signed-in person's own acceptance, for a newer version after pairing; only for a paired person (an active roster row with a membership), else `42501` |
| `withdraw_staff_ops_consent(p_tenant_id)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, volatile | `authenticated` | The signed-in person withdraws their own `staff_ops` consent, so the staff app needs a new pairing; `42501` for a login that is not on the tenant's roster |
| `my_staff_notice(p_tenant_id)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, stable | `authenticated` | `{consent, notice}` for a member of the tenant: `current`, `outdated`, `missing`, or `not_required` for a member who is not on the roster; `42501` for anyone else |

Two restrictive policies, `consent_records_staff_ops_not_inserted_from_browser` (insert) and `consent_records_staff_ops_not_updated_from_browser` (update), keep every browser role, owners and managers included, from inserting a `staff_ops` row or changing one: only the person, through the functions above, records or withdraws their own.

### `app.staff_pairing_codes`

Migration `staff_pairing` (#213). One-time 6-digit codes that an owner or manager issues for a roster row. No API role, `service_role` included, may read the table.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | uuid | no | Primary key |
| `tenant_id`, `roster_id` | uuid | no | The roster row, through (`tenant_id`, `roster_id`) to `staff_roster` (cascade); unique on `roster_id`, so one live code per row |
| `code_sha256` | text | no | `app.staff_pairing_code_hash(tenant_id, code)`, the SHA-256 of the tenant id and the six digits; unique per tenant; no column holds the code |
| `issued_by` | uuid | yes | The issuer (`on delete set null`) |
| `issued_at`, `expires_at` | timestamptz | no | Valid at most 10 minutes |
| `failed_attempts` | smallint | no | Wrong codes sent in the tenant's staff apps while this code was live, 0 to 5 |

A code works in every brand's staff app of the tenant. A wrong code counts against every live code of the tenant: at five the next wrong one deletes them, so five are tolerated and the sixth burns them. An expired code is deleted at the tenant's next pairing request or code.

### `app.staff_pairing_attempts`

Wrong pairing codes per Zalo identity, keyed by the SHA-256 of the Zalo App id and the Zalo user id, so no Zalo user id is kept for a person who never paired. No API role may read it.

| Column | Type | Null | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `zalo_app_id` | uuid | no | The Zalo App (cascade); part of the primary key |
| `identity_sha256` | text | no | SHA-256 hex of the app id and the Zalo user id; part of the primary key |
| `failed_attempts` | smallint | no | 1 to 5 |
| `last_failed_at` | timestamptz | no | The latest wrong code |
| `locked_until` | timestamptz | yes | Set at the fifth wrong code, 15 minutes after it; only with five attempts |

A row is deleted at the app's next pairing request once it is stale (15 minutes after the last wrong code, or once the wait is over), and when the identity pairs.

### Pairing functions

| Function | Security | Executable by | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `issue_pairing_code(p_roster_id)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, volatile | `authenticated` | Issues the row's code and returns it once, for an owner or manager whose scope holds every store of the row; a new code replaces the row's previous one, and the tenant's expired codes go. `42501`: not such a caller, or an unknown row (the same answer); `22023`: an inactive row, or a row whose login is an owner, viewer or publisher of the tenant |
| `app.zalo_pair(zalo_app_id, zalo_user_id, display_name, code, notice_version, notice_shown_at, new_user_id, at)` | definer, volatile | `service_role` | The database half of `zalo-session`'s pairing step, below |
| `public.zalo_session_pair(p_zalo_app_id, p_zalo_user_id, p_display_name, p_code, p_notice_version, p_notice_shown_at, p_new_user_id)` | invoker | `service_role` only | The RPC of `zalo-session` over `app.zalo_pair` at the current time; `authenticated` and `anon` get `42501` |
| `link_my_phone(p_tenant_id, p_phone)` (`app.` and `public.`) | definer, volatile | `authenticated` | The signed-in person stores the phone number that `zalo-phone` decoded for them on their own active roster row and gets its hint back; null removes it; `22023` for a number not in international form, `42501` without an active roster row of their own in the tenant. Optional: no sign-in waits for it |
| `app.staff_ops_gate(tenant_id, user_id, version, shown_at, at)` | definer, volatile | no API role | The consent check of a sign-in: null when the login is not on the tenant's roster, when its `staff_ops` consent is current, or when the request carries a valid acceptance (which is then recorded); otherwise the `notice_required` answer with the notice |
| `app.zalo_unpaired(tenant_id, reason, retry_after)` | definer, stable | no API role | The `unpaired` answer: the published staff notice when there is one and, for a pairing request, the reason |
| `app.staff_login_is_fresh(user_id)` | definer, stable | no API role | The Auth user exists and has no membership, roster row or Zalo identity in any tenant |
| `app.staff_pairing_code_hash(tenant_id, code)` | invoker, immutable | no API role | The stored form of a pairing code |

`app.zalo_pair` decides in this order:

1. `22023` for an unknown or disabled Zalo App, a malformed user id, a code that is not six digits or a notice version that is not shaped like a legal-document version.
2. A Zalo user id already bound to a current member signs in through `staff_ops_gate`, which records the acceptance the request carries when the consent is missing or outdated, or answers `notice_required`.
3. Without a code: `unpaired`.
4. An identity that waits: `unpaired` with reason `wait` and `retry_after`, the seconds left.
5. An acceptance that does not name the published notice, or no acceptance: `unpaired` with reason `notice_outdated`. No code is checked before a valid acceptance, so nobody is paired without a current `staff_ops` consent.
6. A pairing code of a row that is not inactive and whose login is not an owner, viewer or publisher of the tenant pairs: a row without a login answers `login_needed` until `zalo-session` passes a fresh login (`new_user_id`, checked by `staff_login_is_fresh`, else `22023`), which the row keeps; then the `staff_ops` consent is recorded, the membership created or updated with the roster's role and stores, the Zalo identity bound, the row set `active` with `paired_at`, the code and the identity's wrong-code row deleted, and the answer is `bound`. The code of an inactive or conflicting row is deleted and counts as wrong.
7. A phase 13 binding code of the app binds as `app.zalo_sign_in` does, then passes the consent check.
8. Anything else is a wrong code. It counts against every live pairing code of the tenant and every live binding code of the app (the sixth burns them) and against the identity, which waits 15 minutes after its fifth wrong code; the answer is `unpaired` with reason `code_refused`, or `wait` with `retry_after` 900 at the fifth. Wrong, used, expired, deactivated and conflicting codes get the same answer, which never says whether a roster row exists.

For the login of a first pairing, `zalo-session` creates an Auth user through the admin API with the address `staff-<uuid>@zalo-staff.invalid` (a reserved domain that can never receive mail), a confirmed e-mail and no password, and deletes it again when the pairing does not use it ([decisions.md](decisions.md), ADR-037 as amended).

## Loyalty configuration (phase 16)

Roadmap phase 16, built before the owner's paperwork (#14, #25, #8) so the rules are configuration from the start. Two migrations: `loyalty_programmes` (#239) and `coupon_rails` (#242, the schema part; the CRM setup, the callback and pool imports are #243 to #246). Every parameter is a row: no function holds a mechanic value, so changing a row changes the next earn without a deploy (FR-LOY-03). The zod schemas `loyaltyProgrammeSchema`, `loyaltyParametersSchema` (with `LOYALTY_PARAMETER_RANGES`), `couponRailSchema` and `couponRailRewardSchema` of `@fnb/core` hold the same ranges and rules for the Settings page.

### Enums

| Enum | Labels | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `loyalty_programme_status` | `draft`, `active`, `paused`, `ended` | `loyalty_programmes.status` |
| `loyalty_notification_decision` | `pending`, `notified`, `below_threshold`, `not_required` | `loyalty_programmes.notification_decision` |
| `coupon_rail` | `crm_code_pool`, `crm_callback`, `pos_payment_discount` | `coupon_rails.rail` |
| `einvoice_display` | `discount`, `zero_price_line` | `coupon_rail_rewards.einvoice_display` |
| `coupon_code_state` | `available`, `assigned`, `used`, `quarantined`, `expired` | `coupon_codes.state` |

### `loyalty_programmes`

One programme of a tenant; at most one is `active` per tenant (a partial unique index). Read by the data roles of the tenant and by a customer session of the tenant when not a draft; written by owners and managers with an unlimited scope. The trigger `app.guard_loyalty_programme()` trims the name, the basis and the excluded sources, stamps `notification_decided_at` when the decision leaves `pending` (and clears it on `pending`), and refuses with `22023` every value outside its range (`app.loyalty_programme_problem`) and `status = 'active'` while the decision is `pending`, for every writer including the service role and the database owner (FR-CMP-15).

| Column | Type | Default | Range or rule |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `id`, `tenant_id` | uuid | | unique (`tenant_id`, `id`) |
| `name` | text | | 1 to 120 characters, no control character |
| `starts_on`, `ends_on` | date | | `ends_on` null (open-ended) or on or after `starts_on` |
| `reward_budget_amount` | numeric(14,2) | 0 | 0 or more; drives the 100 M₫ notification question |
| `status` | loyalty_programme_status | `draft` | `active` needs a decision other than `pending` |
| `stamps_per_card` | smallint | 8 | 2 to 20 |
| `stamp_min_bill_amount` | numeric(14,2) | 50,000 | 0 to 10,000,000: the net amount that earns a stamp |
| `welcome_stamps` | smallint | 2 | 0 to one less than `stamps_per_card` |
| `point_unit_amount`, `points_per_unit` | numeric(14,2), smallint | 10,000, 1 | 1,000 to 1,000,000; 0 to 100: points per started unit of the net amount |
| `stamp_validity_months`, `points_expiry_months` | smallint | 6, 12 | 1 to 36; 1 to 60 (points: rolling, from each earn) |
| `earning_invoices_per_phone_per_day` | smallint | 3 | 1 to 20 |
| `checkin_window_before_minutes`, `checkin_window_after_minutes` | smallint | 2, 15 | 0 to 30; 1 to 120: the path B window around the recording |
| `checkin_code_ttl_seconds`, `checkin_pending_hours` | smallint | 120, 24 | 30 to 600; 1 to 168: a code's life, and how long a recorded check-in waits for its invoice |
| `receipt_claim_window_hours`, `receipt_claims_per_phone_per_day` | smallint | 72, 3 | 1 to 720; 1 to 20 |
| `excluded_order_sources` | text[] | `{}` | at most 50 names of 1 to 100 characters, matched case-insensitively against `invoices.source_external_id` or `source_name` |
| `point_value_amount` | numeric(14,2) | 500 | 0 to 1,000,000: the value of a point at issue and in the liability |
| `expected_breakage_rate` | numeric(4,3) | 0.200 | 0 to 0.9: used until six months of expiry data exist |
| `notification_decision` | loyalty_notification_decision | `pending` | `not_required` only with `notification_basis` (Decree 239/2026 is unconfirmed; #282 checks again at launch) |
| `notification_decided_at` | timestamptz | | set by the trigger |
| `notification_basis` | text | | 1 to 500 characters |
| `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_by`, `updated_at` | | | the usual stamps |

### `loyalty_staff_phones`

The staff-phone exclusion list of a tenant (#14): an invoice or a claim of one of these phones never earns, and a member with such a phone gets no welcome stamps. Since migration `staff_phone_earn` the same holds for a phone linked to an active `staff_roster` row of the tenant, on every path (`app.loyalty_is_staff_phone`). `phone` is normalised to E.164 by `app.normalize_loyalty_staff_phone()` through `app.loyalty_normalize_phone` (`22023` when unreadable; unique per tenant), with an optional `label` of 1 to 100 characters. Read and written by owners and managers of the tenant only (whatever their store scope); staff, viewers, the publisher, a customer session and `anon` read nothing.

### `coupon_rails`

The loyalty rail of each store (FR-LOY-04): `rail`, the CRM programmes by external id (`crm_programme_external_ids`, at most 20), an optional `callback_ip_allowlist` (`cidr[]`, at most 20), `active_from` and `active_until`. The row with `active_until` null is the active one (a partial unique index per store). Switching a rail inserts a row: `app.guard_coupon_rail()` closes the previous row at the new `active_from` (refusing a start before it) and refuses a change of store, rail or start or the reopening of a closed row (`22023`), so the history stays. Read by the data roles, stores in scope; written by owners and managers with an unlimited scope.

### `coupon_rail_rewards`

The POS side of a reward (`reward_key`, `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`) at a store, unique per store and reward: `pos_discount_name` (a Fabi payment discount), `pos_free_item_external_id` (a 0-price promotional line) or `crm_programme_external_id` (at least one, a CHECK), and `einvoice_display` (`discount` or `zero_price_line`, the latter needing the free item), with `active`. FR-CMP-11 is kept for every writer by two triggers: `app.guard_coupon()` refuses an active `coupons` row while a store of its scope (its `store_ids`, or every active store of the tenant) has no active mapping, and `app.guard_coupon_rail_reward()` refuses deactivating, deleting or moving a mapping that an active reward needs (`22023`); since migration `coupon_guard_cascades` the cascade of a deleted tenant or store passes (the parent row is already gone), as `app.keep_loyalty_ledger()` lets a deleted tenant's rows go. Same access as `coupon_rails`.

### `coupon_codes`

Single-use POS codes per store and reward (a CRM export pool, imported by #244): `code` (unique per store), `state` with `assigned_at`, `used_at`, `used_invoice_id` and `quarantined_at` required by their states, `expires_at`, `imported_at`. Owners and managers read the codes of their stores; no API role writes them (the service role and the phase 17b functions do).

### The e-invoice flag

`loyalty_rail_flags(store_ids)` returns the active rail of each store with its `e_invoice_status` and `flagged` when the status is not `configured` (FR-CMP-10), as the caller (`security invoker`), for Chất lượng dữ liệu.

## Loyalty ledger (phase 17)

Roadmap phase 17a. Migration `loyalty_ledger` (#247) holds the tables; `loyalty_earn` (#248), `loyalty_matching` (#249) and `loyalty_jobs` (#250) the functions, the refusals, the queue and the liability. The ledger is the system of record (ADR-016); the synced invoice is the only proof of purchase.

### Enums

| Enum | Labels | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `membership_status` | `active`, `suspended`, `closed` | `memberships.status` |
| `loyalty_entry_kind` | `earn`, `confirm`, `clawback`, `redeem`, `adjust`, `expire` | `loyalty_ledger.entry_kind` |
| `stamp_state` | `active`, `redeemed`, `expired`, `clawed_back` | `stamps.state` |
| `checkin_status` | `issued`, `recorded`, `matched`, `queued`, `expired`, `rejected` | `pending_checkins.status` |
| `receipt_claim_status` | `pending`, `matched`, `queued`, `rejected`, `expired` | `receipt_claims.status` |
| `redemption_state` | `held`, `confirmed`, `released`, `cancelled` | `redemptions.state` |
| `loyalty_queue_state` | `open`, `resolved`, `dismissed` | `loyalty_queue.state` |

### Tables

| Table | Holds | Key rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `memberships` | a customer's membership of a programme: `customer_id` (the phone stays on `customers`), `joined_at`, `channel` (`mini_app`, `oa_form`, `till`, `staff`, `import`), `status`, `joined_store_id`, and only the per-brand `zalo_user_id` of `brand_id` (NFR-PRV-07) | unique (`programme_id`, `customer_id`); no balance column |
| `loyalty_ledger` | every movement: `entry_kind`, `stamp_delta`, `point_delta`, `value_amount` (points × `point_value_amount` at issue), `invoice_id` and `business_date`, `matched_path` (`A` to `D`), `pending`, `source_entry_id`, `reason`, `approved_by`, `expires_at` (the points' expiry of a crediting row), `occurred_at` | an invoice for every earn, confirm and clawback; a path on every earn and only there; a source for every confirm and clawback; a reason for every adjust and clawback; signs per kind; one earn per invoice, one confirm and one clawback per entry (unique indexes) |
