| `stamps` | one row per stamp a ledger row credited, with `earned_at` (the sale time), `expires_at` and `state`, closed by the clawback, redemption or expiry row | a working table: the balance comes from the ledger only |
| `pending_checkins` | path B: `code` (6 digits, unique per store while `issued`), `expires_at`, `recorded_at`, `recorded_by`, `amount` typed by staff, `status`, `matched_invoice_id`, `matched_entry_id`, `reason` | one check-in per matched invoice |
| `receipt_claims` | path C: `store_id`, `business_date`, `receipt_no`, `amount`, `status`, `expires_at`, `invoice_id`, `entry_id`, `reason` | one claim per invoice for ever; one open (`pending`, `matched`, `queued`) claim per store, business date and receipt number, because receipt numbers reset daily at one shop and yearly at the other (ADR-024) |
| `coupons` | the reward catalogue (completed in #269): `reward_key`, `name`, `cost_stamps`, `cost_points`, `min_bill_amount`, `valid_days`, `hold_hours`, `store_ids`, `active` | active only with a mapping at every store in scope (above) |
| `redemptions` | a hold of a coupon by a member: `code` (unique per store while `held`), `coupon_code_id`, `state`, `hold_expires_at`, `redeem_entry_id`, `confirmed_invoice_id` | confirmed by the synced invoice carrying the code (path D) |
| `loyalty_refusals` | why an invoice did not earn for a membership: `reason` (`below_minimum`, `daily_cap`, `excluded_phone`, `excluded_source`, `already_earned`, `invoice_not_completed`, `programme_inactive`, `membership_inactive`), `matched_path`, store and business date, for the shift anomaly report | unique per invoice, membership and reason; a refused invoice is not tried again on path A |
| `loyalty_queue` | a check-in or claim with 2 to 20 candidate invoices within tolerance (`two_candidates`, `duplicate_receipt`), its `state` and resolution (`resolved_by`, `chosen_invoice_id`, `resolution_reason`, `entry_id`) | one item per check-in or claim |
| `loyalty_liability_daily` | per programme and local day: members, outstanding points and stamps (positive confirmed balances), `point_value_amount`, `expected_breakage_rate` with `breakage_source` (`configured` or `observed`), `liability_amount`, the issued, redeemed, expired and clawed-back points and the deferred revenue released by redemptions of the closed previous local day (from its first moment to the first moment of the row's day, whatever the hour of the run; the stock columns are those of the moment of the run) and `breakage_by_cohort` (issued and expired points per issue month; an expiry is traced to its credits first in first out, what cannot be traced is reported under the run month less the expiry period) (ADR-048) | unique per programme and day; a re-run replaces the row |

Every table carries `tenant_id` and composite foreign keys to the tenant's stores, memberships, invoices and ledger rows (`invoices` and `customers` gained `unique (tenant_id, id)` for this, and `invoices` two partial indexes for paths C and D: (`store_id`, `business_date`, `receipt_no`) and (`store_id`, `voucher_code`)).

### Append-only rule

`app.keep_loyalty_ledger()` refuses every update and delete of a ledger row and `app.refuse_loyalty_ledger_truncate()` every truncate, for every role, the service role and the database owner included; setting the flag `app.loyalty_erasure` does not help the service role. Corrections are new rows that reference the original (`source_entry_id`). Two deletions pass: `app.erase_loyalty_membership(membership)`, the data-subject deletion (it runs as the owner, sets the transaction-local flag and removes the membership's queue items, refusals, stamps, redemptions, claims, check-ins, ledger rows and the membership, returning the counts; `app.dsr_erase_customer` calls it for each membership of a customer and removes the rest of the subject, see "Data-subject deletion"), and the cascade of a deleted tenant.

### Access

Owners, managers and viewers read memberships, stamps and the liability of their tenants and the ledger, check-ins, claims, redemptions and refusals of the stores in their scope (ledger rows without a store with the tenant); owners and managers read the queue of their stores. Staff read none of it: they reach loyalty rows through the functions of phase 17b. The publisher and `anon` read nothing. A customer session reads only its own rows: `app.current_customer_id()` accepts a JWT whose `app_metadata` holds `kind` `customer`, `tenant_id` and `customer_id` when that customer belongs to that tenant and the caller is that customer's own login in `app.customer_logins`, of an active Zalo App, without a `tenant_members` row (migration `customer_claims_bound_to_login`), and `app.current_customer_membership_ids()` lists its memberships; such a session also reads its own `customers` row, its tenant's programmes that are not drafts and its tenant's active `coupons`. No API role writes these tables; `coupons` is written by owners and managers with an unlimited scope.

### Loyalty functions (phase 17)

Every function sets an empty `search_path`. The `app` functions are `security definer` and executable by the service role only (the jobs connect as the owner), except the balance (`security invoker`) and the queue resolution.

| Function | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `app.loyalty_normalize_phone(text)` | E.164 or null: spaces, dots, dashes and brackets dropped; `00` read as `+`; a national number with a leading 0 and the bare 84 prefix read as Vietnamese. `normalizeLoyaltyPhone` of `@fnb/core` is the same rule |
| `app.loyalty_customer_for_phone(tenant, phone, at)` | the tenant's `customers` row of a phone in any form, created with the E.164 phone when missing |
| `app.loyalty_active_programme(tenant, on)` | the active programme covering a day |
| `app.loyalty_join(tenant, customer, channel, store, brand, zalo_user_id, at)` | the membership id in the active programme; the first join credits `welcome_stamps` (an `adjust` row, reason `welcome`, with their stamps rows), except for a staff phone (`app.loyalty_is_staff_phone`: the list or an active roster row); idempotent |
| `app.loyalty_earn(invoice, membership, path, at, pending, approved_by)` | one earn per invoice: a stamp when the net amount reaches `stamp_min_bill_amount`, `points_per_unit` per started `point_unit_amount`; refuses in this order `already_earned`, `membership_inactive`, `invoice_not_completed`, `programme_inactive` (paused, or the invoice outside the period), `excluded_source`, `excluded_phone` (the member's or the invoice's phone is a staff phone, `app.loyalty_is_staff_phone`: on `loyalty_staff_phones` or linked to an active `staff_roster` row, on every path A to D since migration `staff_phone_earn`), `daily_cap` (earns not clawed back on the invoice's business date: a clawed-back earn does not count toward the cap from the moment its clawback is written, so a void frees its place; the `@fnb/core` replay states the same through `voidedAt`, G-42), and notes `below_minimum` when no stamp is earned (points may still be). Returns (`entry_id`, `stamps`, `points`, `refusal`) and stores each refusal |
| `app.loyalty_confirm(entry, invoice, at)` | confirms a pending earn or redemption hold with a `confirm` row naming the invoice; idempotent; a reversed entry is refused |
| `app.loyalty_clawback(invoice, reason, at)` | reverses every earn of the invoice not yet reversed and closes its active stamps; a redeemed stamp stays redeemed, so the balance may go negative; the reason defaults to `invoice_voided` or `invoice_edited`; a completed invoice needs one |
| `app.loyalty_expiring_points(membership, as_of)`, `app.loyalty_expire(tenant, as_of)` | stamps past their `expires_at` and points first in first out (credits past their expiry, net of their clawbacks, less everything already spent, held, adjusted away or expired), one `expire` row per membership; counts only |
| `app.loyalty_balance(membership, at)` and `public.loyalty_balance(membership)` | spendable and pending stamps and points, card progress, next stamp expiry and the points value, as the caller; credits whose expiry has passed at `at` are left out whatever expire rows exist (a null `at` is the plain ledger sum, `public.loyalty_balance` passes `now()`) (ADR-047) |
| `app.loyalty_credit_expires_at(sold_at, months, tenant)` | the local midnight that ends the local date of the sale plus the months, clamped to the last day of a shorter month; used by every writer of an expiry (ADR-047) |
| `app.loyalty_write_clawback(...)` | the one writer of a clawback row: a pending earn whole, a confirmed earn up to the member's spendable balance of each kind, an expired earn nothing; the part not taken goes on the reason as "short by n stamp(s) and n point(s)" and in the nightly answer as `written_off_stamps` and `written_off_points` (ADR-046) |
| `app.loyalty_expire_membership(membership, at)` | the due expiry of one member, run by `app.loyalty_expire` and before a hold or a manual debit is accepted, so points never expire behind a debit (ADR-047) |
| `app.loyalty_issue_checkin_code(membership, store, at)` | path B: a fresh 6-digit code valid `checkin_code_ttl_seconds`, replacing the member's live code at that store |
| `app.loyalty_record_checkin(store, code, recorded_by, amount, at)` | path B: staff record a live code (`22023` when unknown or expired); matched at once when the invoice is synced, otherwise a fast-lane run is requested |
| `app.loyalty_match_checkin(checkin, at)` | path B: completed invoices of the store sold from `checkin_window_before_minutes` before to `checkin_window_after_minutes` after the recording, not earned, matched or claimed, ranked by the gap to the typed amount and then by time; two candidates equally close go to the queue |
| `app.loyalty_submit_claim(membership, store, business_date, receipt_no, amount, at)` | path C: records the claim; a refusal is stored as a `rejected` claim with `membership_inactive`, `outside_window` (a business date older than `receipt_claim_window_hours` or in the future), `daily_cap` (`receipt_claims_per_phone_per_day` submissions on the store's business day) or `already_claimed` (an open claim of the receipt, by this or another phone); matched at once when the invoice is synced, otherwise a fast-lane run is requested |
| `app.loyalty_match_claim(claim, at)` | path C: the invoice of the store, business date and receipt number (leading zeros ignored) with the exact net amount; `amount_mismatch`, `already_claimed`, `already_earned`, `invoice_not_completed` and earn refusals reject it; two such invoices go to the queue; past `expires_at` without an invoice it expires (`no_matching_invoice`) |
| `app.reconcile_loyalty(tenant, from, to, at)` | the step after every sync (at most 63 days; nothing without an active programme): path A for invoices of the range carrying a member's customer or phone (sold no earlier than the claim window before joining), path B for recorded check-ins, path C for pending claims, path D for held redemptions whose code (case-insensitive) is on an invoice of the range (confirming the redemption, its hold and its pool code, then earning), then expiry of stale codes, check-ins (`checkin_pending_hours`) and claims; idempotent; returns counts |
| `app.resolve_loyalty_queue_item(item, choice, reason)` and `public.resolve_loyalty_queue_item` | an owner or manager of the store earns the chosen candidate (`approved_by` the caller) or dismisses the item |
| `app.request_fast_lane(store, reason, at)` | records a fast-lane request in `app.loyalty_fast_lane_runs`: joins the store's run not yet claimed, or opens one due at once, or 5 minutes after the previous run's start when that is later; calls nothing |
| `app.claim_fast_lane_runs(at, limit)`, `app.finish_fast_lane_run(run, outcome, at)` | hand each due run out once with its 60-minute window and record its outcome; requests unclaimed for an hour close as `expired`; `postgres` only |
| `app.loyalty_liability(programme, as_of)` | stores the day's `loyalty_liability_daily` row: outstanding points × `point_value_amount` × (1 − breakage); breakage is `expected_breakage_rate` until the programme's first expiry is six months old, then expired ÷ (redeemed + expired) points of the last 12 months; a pending earn that is not confirmed at that moment, and the clawback of it, count for nothing (ADR-048, migration `20261122100000`, `pgtap:237_liability_pending_clawback`) |
| `app.loyalty_nightly(tenant, as_of)` | clawback of earns whose invoice the re-pull marked `voided` or `edited`, `loyalty_expire`, then the liability of each active or paused programme; counts only |
| `app.loyalty_tenants()` | the tenants with an active or paused programme |
| `app.erase_loyalty_membership(membership)` | the loyalty part of the data-subject deletion above, called by `app.dsr_erase_customer` |

### Where the steps run

`@fnb/runner` ends every incremental run (the `sync-incremental` Edge Function and `fnb sync incremental`) with `app.reconcile_loyalty` over the run's window, and `fnb sync window` does the same after each window run; `--kind nightly` then runs `app.loyalty_nightly` for the account's tenant. `fnb loyalty nightly` reconciles the last 14 business days and runs the nightly step on demand, `fnb loyalty reconcile` reconciles a range. A failure of a loyalty step is reported and never fails the sync (NFR-AVL-04). The fast lane calls the POS only from `fnb loyalty fast-lane` with `LOYALTY_FAST_LANE=on`; it is off by default and nothing schedules it.

## Customer session and staff loyalty tools (phases 17b and 18a)

Migrations `customer_session` (#261), `customer_wallet` (#264) and `staff_loyalty` (#255) put the customer Mini App and the staff app's loyalty tools on the ledger above; `customer_app_answers` replaces `app.customer_join`, `app.customer_programme` and `app.customer_wallet` with the answers below (a join without an open programme refused as `no_programme`, the programme's status, a released reward hold read as reversed). The RPCs are in [api.md](api.md) sections 3.20 and 3.21.

### Tables and columns

| Object | Holds | Key rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `app.customer_logins` | the Auth login of a customer of the customer Mini App: `tenant_id`, `zalo_app_id`, `zalo_user_id`, `user_id` and, once joined, `customer_id` | unique (`zalo_app_id`, `zalo_user_id`) and unique `user_id`; never a login with a `tenant_members` row or a staff `zalo_identities` row; `customer_id` set null when the customer row goes; no other Zalo data (NFR-PRV-07); no API role reads it |
| `app.loyalty_requests` | a dispute or adjustment recorded in the staff app: `store_id`, `membership_id`, `kind` (`adjustment`, `dispute`), `stamp_delta` (−20 to 20), `point_delta` (−100,000 to 100,000), `reason` (1 to 200 characters), `status` (`pending`, `applied`, `approved`, `refused`), `requested_by`, `decided_by`, `decided_at`, `decision_reason`, `entry_id` (the ledger `adjust` row) | an adjustment moves something; a dispute never takes; decided exactly when not pending; a refusal has its reason; removed with the membership; no API role reads it |
| `tenants.loyalty_adjust_max_stamps`, `tenants.loyalty_adjust_max_points` | the largest adjustment (either sign) a staff member applies at once: 1 stamp (0 to 20) and 0 points (0 to 100,000) by default | written through `set_loyalty_adjust_limits` |

### The customer session

A customer session is a login of `app.customer_logins` with a JWT whose `app_metadata` is `{"kind": "customer", "tenant_id", "customer_id"}`. `zalo-session` sets it through the Auth admin API after `zalo_customer_sign_in` and `customer_join`, and the custom access-token hook keeps it. `app.customer_session()` returns the login of the caller, or refuses with `42501`. The login must be of an active Zalo App, with the claimed tenant and no `tenant_members` row. `app.customer_session_customer()` also needs a joined customer equal to `app.current_customer_id()`, the same claim RLS reads. `app.customer_session_membership()` gives that customer's membership of the active programme, else of the newest programme that is not a draft. A store is named by its link key, `app.customer_store_code(code, external_id)`: the code, else the external id, 1 to 64 of `A-Z a-z 0-9 . _ -`. `app.customer_app_stores(app)` and `app.customer_app_store(app, key)` keep to the active stores of the app's brand.

### Functions

Every function sets an empty `search_path`. The `public` RPCs of the customer and the staff are `security definer` and take no time argument; the `app` functions behind them take `p_at` and no client role executes them.

| Function | Does |
| --- | --- |
| `app.zalo_customer_sign_in(app, zalo_user_id, display_name, new_user_id)` | `login_needed`, or binds a new customer login once (first binding wins), then answers the login, its tenant, the customer once joined and the name |
| `app.customer_join(...)` | refuses `consent_required`, `policy_outdated`, `unknown_store`, `no_programme` (no active programme covers the tenant's today), `phone_in_use`, writing nothing; finds or creates the customer by E.164 phone, `app.loyalty_join` with the brand and per-brand Zalo user id, `loyalty` consent and, only when ticked, `marketing` consent through `app.customer_record_consent` (an active record of the same policy version is kept; another version is revoked and replaced), each with its evidence |
| `app.customer_home()` | programme facts with its `status` (`app.customer_programme`; null without a programme that is not a draft), OA, legal texts, the brand's stores with the receipt numbering each follows (`app.receipt_numbers_reset(tran_no_reset_period, receipt_numbers_reset)`: the owner's setting unless `unknown`, else the POS's), no menu yet; no amount or price |
| `app.customer_wallet(at)` | card, one stamp card, points with the next expiry day's points, history, claims and the claims of the day, from the ledger; a redeem row is `pending` while held, `confirmed` once its confirm row exists and `reversed` once a `redemption_released` adjust row names it, and that adjust row has no matched path; and `deletion_request` (`app.customer_deletion_request(tenant, customer, at)`: the open request, else the latest, else null) |
| `app.customer_member_code(store, at)` | `app.loyalty_issue_checkin_code` at a store of the brand, reusing a code issued in the last 30 seconds; QR payload `fnb-member:1:<link key>:<code>` |
| `app.customer_submit_claim(store, receipt_no, amount, business_date, at)` | `app.loyalty_submit_claim` after the contract's input checks |
| `app.customer_stop_marketing(at)`, `app.customer_request_deletion(at)` | revoke the active `marketing` consent; record or answer the open `deletion` request in `data_subject_requests`, which an owner carries out with `dsr_delete`, answered by `app.customer_deletion_answer(request)` as `{request_id, status, requested_at, first_response_due_at, completion_due_at}` |
| `app.loyalty_is_staff_phone(tenant, phone)` | a phone on `loyalty_staff_phones` or linked to an active `staff_roster` row; such a phone gets no welcome stamps and earns on no path (`excluded_phone`, named `staff_phone` in the staff app) |
| `app.loyalty_member_for_phone(tenant, phone)` | the membership in the active programme of the customer with the phone, without creating a customer |
| `app.staff_record_checkin(store, phone, code, amount, at)` | "Ghi nhận khách": phone (a code is issued for the member) or code or QR payload, then `app.loyalty_record_checkin`; refusals answered with the staff app's names (`app.staff_checkin_refusal`) and `checkin.refusal.<refusal>` |
| `app.staff_member_lookup(store, phone, membership, at)` | by phone or membership: masked member card with the balance, today's visits at the store and the adjustment limits, or `found` false |
| `app.staff_request(store, kind, membership, reason, stamps, points, at)`, `app.staff_decide_loyalty_request(request, decision, reason, stamps, points, at)` | requests within the tenant's limits applied at once, others pending for an owner or manager of the store other than the requester, who may approve other amounts; an applied or approved change is `app.loyalty_apply_adjustment` |
| `app.staff_loyalty_queue(store)`, `app.staff_my_loyalty_requests()` | the manager queue (pending requests the caller may decide and open `loyalty_queue` match items with their candidates) and the caller's own requests, as JSON arrays |
| `app.loyalty_member_summary(membership, at)`, `app.loyalty_masked_phone(phone)`, `app.loyalty_requester_name(tenant, user)` | the short member card of a check-in, the masked phone, the requester's roster or Zalo name |
| `app.loyalty_apply_adjustment(membership, store, stamps, points, reason, approved_by, at)` | the ledger `adjust` row with reason and approver; stamps rows added, or the oldest active closed as `clawed_back` |
| `app.set_loyalty_adjust_limits(tenant, stamps, points)` | an owner with every store sets the two tenant limits |
| `app.loyalty_text_key(prefix, reason)` | a refusal's message key in camel case, as `redemption.refused.codeExpired`, used by the redemption functions |

## Rate limits on code and phone guessing (#317)

Migration `code_guessing_limits`. `app.code_attempt_policies` holds one row per limited endpoint: whether every call (`calls`) or only a miss (`misses`) counts, the caller's maximum, the store's maximum (the tenant's for the web phone lookup), the window and the lockout. Defaults: `staff_verify_redemption` 5 / 20 unknown codes, `staff_member_lookup` and `loyalty_member_lookup` 10 / 30 phones without a member, `customer_redeem` 10 / 100 calls, each within 15 minutes with a 15-minute lockout. `app.code_attempts` counts per endpoint, tenant and subject (`caller` user id, `store` or `tenant`) in a fixed window with `locked_until`; no code, phone or answer is kept. `app.code_attempt_gate` takes a transaction advisory lock per endpoint and store (or tenant), so parallel guesses are counted one after the other, and returns the end of a live lock; `app.code_attempt_record` counts an attempt, locks the subject that reaches its maximum and raises the instant alert `code_guessing_lockout` (owners and managers, count-only facts, one-hour cool-down). Neither table nor function is reachable by `anon` or `authenticated`; the service role reads both tables and may update the policies.

## Rewards and redemptions (phase 18b)

Migrations `coupon_catalogue` (#269), `redemption_flow` (#271) and `redemption_reconciliation` (#273) complete the reward catalogue, add the redemption lifecycle on the ledger and reconcile redemptions with the synced invoices. The RPCs are in [api.md](api.md) section 3.22.

### Enums

| Enum | Labels | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `coupon_reward_kind` | `free_item`, `percent_off`, `amount_off`, `free_upgrade` | `coupons.reward_kind`; no cash-like kind (payout, stored value, top-up) exists, so none can be stored (FR-CMP-13) |
| `coupon_status` | `draft`, `approved`, `active`, `retired` | `coupons.status` |

### The reward catalogue (`coupons`)

New columns: `reward_kind` (default `free_item`), `reward_percent` (`percent_off` only, above 0 and at most 100), `reward_amount` (`amount_off` only), `reward_item_external_ids` (the POS items given or discounted, 1 to 50; null for the bill), `excluded_item_external_ids` (at most 100; never also a reward item), `description` (1 to 500 characters), `valid_from` and `valid_until` (fixed business days; `valid_days` still bounds each hold after issue), `per_member_limit` (1 to 100 held or confirmed redemptions per membership; null for none), `status`, `approved_by` and `approved_at`. `active` stays and always equals `status = 'active'` (a CHECK), so the policies and triggers of phase 16 keep working.

`app.guard_coupon_terms()` runs before `app.guard_coupon()` for every writer, the service role and the database owner included:

- the discount cap (FR-CMP-12): a `percent_off` above the cap, and an `amount_off` above the cap times `min_bill_amount` or without a minimum bill, give `22023`. The cap is `app.promotion_discount_cap(country, from, to)`: the lowest `maxDiscountRate` of the dated `discount_cap` rules of `promotionRules` in `@fnb/core` in force on any day of the reward's validity (50 % for Vietnam under Decree 81/2018 to 30 June 2025 and Circular 39/2025/TT-BCT after), null for a country without rules; a change of the rules in `@fnb/core` needs a migration here. `promotionDiscountCap(country, from, to)` of `@fnb/core` is the same rule: the unit test `discount-cap.test.ts` holds the function's values rows to the dated rules and builds the fixture `discount-cap.json` (every rule's first and last day, the days around them and open ranges), which pgTAP `92_discount_cap_mirror.sql` embeds and compares with the SQL. `free_item` and `free_upgrade` are gifts and not capped while the legal read (#44) is open;
- the status: a new reward is a draft (or approved or active), a retired one stays retired, and an old writer that sets only `active` moves the status (`active`, or `approved` when deactivated). A writer that clears or rewrites `approved_by` itself gets the approver back; only the foreign key's `ON DELETE SET NULL` after the approving Auth user was deleted passes (inside a trigger, nothing else changed, the user gone; migration `coupon_guard_cascades`), so the reward keeps its status and `approved_at`. Leaving draft records the approval: `approved_by` the owner, who needs every store in scope (`42501` for anyone else, so a manager drafts and activates an approved reward but never approves), or `approved_at` only for the service role and the database owner. When a manager changes the terms (kind, percentage, amount, items, costs, minimum bill, validity, hold, limit) of an approved or active reward it returns to draft; an owner's change is approved at once;
- the rail: an active reward needs an active `coupon_rails` row at every store of its scope (`22023`), besides the `coupon_rail_rewards` mapping `app.guard_coupon()` already requires.

`public.coupon_discount_days(coupon, year)` gives the discount days of the reward's items from `item_discount_days`, as information only, with `app.promotion_discount_days_limit(country, on)`: 120 until 25 June 2026, then null while Decree 239/2026 is reported to repeal the limit.

### Columns added for the lifecycle

| Object | Added | Rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `redemptions` | `rail`, `pos_discount_name`, `pos_free_item_external_id` (copied from the store's rail and mapping at hold time), `verified_at`, `verified_by`, `release_reason` (`hold_expired`), `release_entry_id` | a held code is unique in the tenant, whatever its case (`redemptions_live_tenant_code_key`); an invoice confirms one redemption at most (`redemptions_confirmed_invoice_key`); no amount column, so `redemptions` holds no revenue figure |
| `loyalty_queue` | `redemption_id`, `invoice_id`; `membership_id` may be null on path `D` | path `D` items (`verified_without_invoice`, `discount_without_redemption`, `amount_mismatch`, `confirmed_invoice_voided`) have no check-in, claim or candidate invoice and name a redemption or an invoice; one item per redemption and reason, and per invoice and reason |
| `alert_rule_catalog` | `redemption_mismatch` (sort 195): business, immediate, `warning`, owners and managers, store-scoped, no cool-down, parameters `grace_hours` (1 to 168, 24) and `amount_tolerance` (0 to 100,000 dong, 1,000) | |

### Lifecycle

| Step | Function | Does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hold | `app.redeem_coupon(membership, coupon, store, at)` | refusals in order: `membership_inactive`, `store_unavailable`, `programme_inactive`, `coupon_unavailable` (not active, another programme, outside its dates), `store_unavailable` (outside the reward's stores), `rail_not_ready` (no active rail, no active mapping, or a fallback mapping without a discount name or free item), `already_held`, `member_limit_reached`, `insufficient_stamps` (ledger balance or spendable stamps, `app.redemption_spendable_stamps`), `insufficient_points`, `no_code_available` (an empty pool); then a pending `redeem` row (reason `redemption`, the points valued at `point_value_amount`), the oldest spendable stamps closed as `redeemed`, and a `held` redemption with its code: a pool code (assigned), or `app.redemption_new_code(tenant)`, 8 characters never given before in the tenant; the hold ends at `hold_hours`, at most `valid_days`, never past `valid_until` |
| verify | `app.staff_verify_redemption(store, code, at)` | marks a held, unexpired code of the given store (in the caller's scope) verified, with the rail instruction; the code may be the QR payload `fnb-redeem:1:<code>`; the refusals `unknown`, `wrong_store`, `used`, `expired` and `released` of api.md 3.22 are answered, not raised |
| confirm | `app.redemption_confirming_invoice(redemption)`, `app.confirm_redemption(redemption, invoice, at)` | the invoice that confirms a held redemption (the code rails: the code as voucher code at the store; the fallback rail: after verification, the discount entry name, any case and spacing, or the 0-price line of the free item, on an invoice of the store sold from `checkin_window_before_minutes` before to `checkin_window_after_minutes` after the verification, closest first; never an invoice that confirms another redemption); confirming sets `confirmed`, writes the hold's `confirm` row, marks the pool code `used` and earns the invoice on path `D` |
| release | `app.release_redemption(redemption, reason, at)`, `app.release_redemption_holds(tenant, as_of)` | at the end of the hold, a hold whose invoice is synced is confirmed; otherwise an `adjust` row (reason `redemption_released`, the hold as `source_entry_id`, the opposite deltas) gives the stamps and points back, the stamps the hold closed are active again with their own expiry, the redemption is `released` and its pool code `quarantined` |
| reconcile | `app.reconcile_redemptions(tenant, from, to, at)` | confirms the verified fallback holds whose invoice is synced, resolves path `D` items whose redemption or invoice is now confirmed, lists new mismatches (a verified code without a confirming invoice `grace_hours` later; a completed invoice of the range with a mapped discount entry name, a pool code or a redemption code and no redemption it confirms; a confirmed `percent_off` or `amount_off` whose invoice discount, the payment discount on the fallback rail or the voucher amount on the code rails, differs from `app.redemption_expected_discount` by more than `amount_tolerance`, or whose bill is under the minimum), then per store opens or refreshes the episode `redemption_mismatch:store:<id>` with the facts `open_items`, `verified_without_invoice`, `discount_without_redemption`, `amount_mismatch`, and resolves it as `cleared` when no item is open |

### A voided confirmed redemption (migration `void_after_confirmation`)

Migration `20261119960000_void_after_confirmation.sql` gives the `cancelled` value of `redemption_state` its one transition: `confirmed` to `cancelled`, when the re-pull marks the confirming invoice `voided` (G-03).

- `app.cancel_voided_redemptions(tenant, at)` (service role only, called first by `app.reconcile_redemptions` after the rail recognition) cancels every confirmed redemption of the tenant whose `confirmed_invoice_id` invoice is voided. For each one, under a row lock: an `adjust` ledger row (reason `redemption_released`, the hold as `source_entry_id`, the opposite deltas) gives the stamps and points back and the stamps the hold closed are active again; `state` becomes `cancelled` with `released_at` and `release_entry_id`; the pool code goes to `quarantined`; and a path `D` queue item with the reason `confirmed_invoice_voided` is added for the managers, once per redemption. It returns the number cancelled; a second run cancels nothing.
- `loyalty_queue_reason_check` now accepts `confirmed_invoice_voided` on path `D`.
- The alert `redemption_mismatch:store:<id>` counts the new reason: its facts gain `confirmed_invoice_voided` (left out when 0) beside `open_items`, `verified_without_invoice`, `discount_without_redemption` and `amount_mismatch`, and `open_items` includes it. `app.reconcile_redemptions` skips a `cancelled` redemption in the verified-without-invoice check.
- An edited invoice (a changed amount, not a void) is not handled; G-44 in [test-gaps.md](test-gaps.md).

A released hold is netted out by `app.loyalty_expiring_points` (the returned points keep the expiry of the credits they came from) and by `app.loyalty_liability` (neither issued nor redeemed), both replaced by `redemption_flow` with that rule only.

### Where the steps run

`app.reconcile_loyalty` is now a wrapper: it calls `app.reconcile_loyalty_paths` (the phase 17 function, renamed) and then `app.reconcile_redemptions`, adding the counts `discount_confirmed`, `discount_earned`, `mismatches_found` and `mismatch_alerts`. `app.loyalty_nightly` likewise calls `app.release_redemption_holds` first and then `app.loyalty_nightly_steps` (renamed), adding `released_holds`, `late_confirmed_holds` and `quarantined_codes`. Every caller of phase 17 (the runner after each sync, the nightly job, `fnb loyalty reconcile`, `fnb loyalty sale`) runs the redemption steps without change. A later migration that replaces either function must keep the wrapper. `fnb loyalty sale` takes `--discount-name`, `--voucher-code` and `--discount-amount`, so a local trial on `loyalty-sim` puts the reward on the simulated invoice.

### Access

The `app` functions of this section are executable by the service role (the hold, confirmation, release and reconciliation functions) or by no API role (the customer and staff functions behind the RPCs, the code generator and the spendable-stamp list). Staff read no `redemptions` row directly; they verify codes through the RPC, which keeps to their stores. `redemption_summary` is a `security invoker` report function.

## Staff confirmation mode and bills (POS-independent loyalty, 2026-11-21)

Migrations `loyalty_staff_confirmation` (20261120500000), `bill_captures` (20261120510000) and `bill_anomalies` (20261120520000). Design: [deep-dives/bill-verification-plan.md](deep-dives/bill-verification-plan.md) (section 7 lists what was built) and ADR-043. RPCs: [api.md](api.md).

### `loyalty_store_settings`

One row per store, created with the store by the trigger `stores_create_loyalty_settings` (every existing store was given one in `pos_sync`). Read by owners, managers and viewers of the store (RLS through `current_user_loyalty_store_ids`); written only by `set_loyalty_store_settings` (owners of the store).

| Column | Default | Range | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `confirmation` | `pos_sync` | `pos_sync`, `staff` | what confirms an earn or a redemption at this store |
| `max_bill_amount` | 2,000,000 | above 0, at most 100,000,000 | the largest bill a staff-mode earn may carry |
| `bill_evidence_required_level` | 2 | 0 to 2 | 0: the staff reading alone confirms; 2: the customer's agreeing blind total is needed |
| `bill_manager_approval_above` | 1,000,000 | above 0, at most `max_bill_amount` | a larger bill always waits in the manager queue |
| `bill_amount_tolerance` | 1,000 | 0 to 100,000 | two totals agree within it |
| `bill_unconfirmed_hours` | 24 | 1 to 168 | how long a bill waits for the customer |
| `bill_silence_outcome` | `reverse` | `reverse`, `confirm` | what the deadline does to an unanswered bill |
| `bill_staff_shift_cap` | 25 | 1 to 1,000 | unreviewed (level 0 or 1) bills one staff member may record a business day; alert at 70 % |
| `staff_amount_per_day` | 30,000,000 | above 0, at most 1,000,000,000 | total of one staff member's bills a day |
| `store_earns_per_day` | 400 | 1 to 5,000 | bills a store may record a day |
| `staff_redemptions_per_day` | 50 | 1 to 1,000 | rewards one staff member may confirm a day |
| `reverse_window_minutes` | 480 | 15 to 1,440 | how long a manager may still undo a bill or void a reward (the shift) |
| `bill_audit_sample_pct` | 2 | 0 to 100 | share of confirmed bills put in a random audit |
| `self_claim` | false | | whether customers may claim with the counter QR |
| `counter_code_ttl_seconds` | 90 | 30 to 600 | life of one counter QR (plus 120 s of grace to type the bill) |
| `auto_approve_max_amount`, `auto_approve_per_day` | 0, 0 | at most `max_bill_amount`; 0 to 500 | counter claims confirmed at once at level 0 |
| `bill_photo_retention_days`, `bill_reader_mode` | 90, `off` | 1 to 365; `off`, `mock` | phase 4 knobs, stored now |
| `shadow_reconcile` | true | | link bills to synced invoices when a POS sync exists |

### `bill_captures`

A paper bill at a staff-mode store (path E). `source` `staff_scan` (recorded by `captured_by` after a scan of the member's dynamic QR) or `counter_claim` (sent by the customer with `counter_code_id`; `captured_by` becomes the staff member who answers it). `bill_no`, `bill_key` (`app.bill_key`: trimmed, upper case, leading zeros dropped) and `business_date`: the unique index `bill_captures_dedupe_key` on store, business date and key covers every bill not rejected or expired. `total` is the first reading. `evidence_level` 0 to 3 (0 typed, 1 one reading, 2 two agreeing readings, 3 with clean bill checks). `state`: `awaiting_customer`, `awaiting_staff`, `queued` (with `queue_reason` `over_approval` or `disagreement`), `confirmed`, `reversed` (with `reversal_reason` `disputed`, `timed_out`, `manager_rejected`, `manager_reversed`, `audit_failed`), `rejected`, `expired`. `confirm_by` is the deadline. `invoice_id`, `shadow_state` (`matched`, `mismatch`, `no_invoice`) and `shadow_checked_at` come from the shadow check. `printed_at`, `lines`, `flags`, `photo_path`, `photo_hash` and `reading` are empty until the camera phases. RLS: loyalty readers of the store, and the recording staff member their own rows; no API writes.

### `bill_confirmations`, `bill_audits`, `loyalty_counter_codes`, `loyalty_audit_events`

- `bill_confirmations`: one reading per bill and source (`staff`, `customer`, later `reader` with its `reading`), with `entered_total` and `agrees`. Owners and managers of the store read it. `answered_state` (migration `customer_bill_answer_replay`, G-92; the customer's row only, a CHECK) keeps the outcome the customer was given for their reading (`awaiting_customer`, `awaiting_staff`, `queued` or `confirmed`), so the same answer sent again (a lost one) gets it back instead of a refusal; null on rows written before the migration, which still answer `already_answered`.
- `bill_audits`: the random audit of a confirmed bill, `open`, `passed` or `failed` (with a note). Owners and managers read it.
- `loyalty_counter_codes`: the rotating 12-character counter tokens; no API role reads them.
- `loyalty_audit_events`: the audit trail of the mode (settings changes, every bill outcome, audits, counter QR issues, staff redemption confirmations and voids, shadow flags) with actor, subject, membership and amount; details never hold a phone, code or name. Owners and managers of the store read it; the data-subject erasure blanks `membership_id`.

### Ledger and redemptions

- `loyalty_ledger.bill_id` (foreign key to `bill_captures`); `matched_path` gains `E`; an earn, confirm or clawback names an invoice or a bill (a confirm of a staff-confirmed redemption carries reason `staff_confirmed` instead); one earn per bill (`loyalty_ledger_one_earn_per_bill_key`). A bill's earn is written pending at recording and confirmed by a confirm row with reason `bill_confirmed`, or reversed by a clawback with reason `bill_<reason>`.
- `loyalty_refusals.reason` gains `staff_mode`: paths A to D do not earn at a staff-mode store, and no path earns an invoice a live bill is linked to.
- `redemptions` gains `confirmed_by`, `voided_by` and `void_reason`; a confirmed redemption names an invoice or `confirmed_by`.
- `app.code_attempt_policies` gains `staff_submit_bill` and `customer_counter_claim` (5 misses per caller, 20 per store and 50 per tenant in 15 minutes).
- Alert rule `loyalty_staff_anomaly` (operations, owners and managers, one hour cool-down): kinds `cap_warning`, `cap_reached`, `bill_conflict` (critical), `disputed`, `timed_out`, `reversal`, `audit_failed`, `shadow_mismatch`, count-only facts.
- pg_cron job `loyalty-bill-deadlines` runs `app.bill_expire_pending` every 10 minutes.

### Tests

pgTAP 220 (settings), 221 (decision table mode x action x role), 222 (bill state transitions), 223 (boundaries), 224 (security), 225 (staff redemption transitions), 226 (anomaly report, audits, shadow check, the store lock, erasure), 248 (an answer sent twice and the wallet's counter claims, G-92); `supabase/snippets/bill-parallel-caps.sh` runs eight bills in parallel sessions against a cap of three.

## Till-side rails (phase 16b)

Built locally (migrations `voucher_callback`, `coupon_code_pools` and `rail_recognition`, pgTAP `95` to `97`; #243 to #245). Every table here is in schema `app` and revoked from every API role: only the jobs, the `voucher-callback` Edge Function (as `postgres`) and the service role through the functions below reach them. None stores a code value, a phone or a member reference.

### Voucher callback (phase 16b)

Two columns on `coupon_rails`, both for a `crm_callback` row only (a CHECK each): `callback_store_ref`, the store as the POS names it in the callback (on iPOS, `Pos_Id`, assumed to be the store's `fb_store_id`; unique among the active rails of a tenant), and `callback_enabled` (default false: the callback answers `disabled` until the owner switches it on). Like every rail column they are set again on the new row after a switch.

| Table | Holds |
| --- | --- |
| `app.voucher_callback_paths` | the tenant's secret path segments as SHA-256 only, per `label` (one live row per label), with `revoked_at` |
| `app.voucher_callback_answers` | the binding answers: one row per store, POS sale reference and redemption that was given a discount (`discount_amount` above 0, `reward_name`, `answered_at`, `replays`, `last_replayed_at`); `redemption_id` becomes null when a data-subject deletion erases the redemption |
| `app.voucher_callback_log` | one row per decided call: `logged_at`, `store_id` (null for an unknown store), `outcome`, `discount_amount`, `latency_ms` |

`app.answer_voucher_callback(path_sha256, source_ip, store_ref, programme_ref, code, sale_ref, member_phone, lines, received_at, at)` decides one call in one round trip, from canonical values only (`lines` is `[{item, quantity, amount}]`, the amount being the net line value). Without a write it answers `unknown_path` (no live segment), `malformed` (values out of shape, `app.voucher_callback_lines_valid`) and `refused_address` (outside a non-empty allow-list). Otherwise it logs one of: `unknown_store`, `disabled`, `replayed` (the first answer of the same store, sale and code, with its discount), `wrong_programme` (a programme reference outside the rail's `crm_programme_external_ids`; the iPOS adapter passes none today), `unknown_code` (no redemption of the tenant on `crm_callback` with the code, case ignored), `wrong_store`, `used` (confirmed, or answered for another sale), `released`, `expired` (the hold or the reward's dates), `wrong_member` (a phone on the bill that is not the owner's), `below_minimum`, `not_applicable` or `accepted`. The discount of an accepted call is computed on the eligible lines (the reward items, or every line but the excluded items): `percent_off` rounded to the dong, `amount_off` up to its amount, one unit of the cheapest reward item for `free_item` and `free_upgrade` with reward items; never above the eligible total, and for the two price reductions never above the dated discount cap (`app.promotion_discount_cap`) of it. It stores the answer and sets the redemption's `verified_at` when empty, so the staff app answers `used` and the `verified_without_invoice` check applies. `app.redemption_confirming_invoice` also accepts, on `crm_callback`, the completed invoice of the store whose `external_id` is an answered sale reference; `app.reconcile_rails` confirms those holds after each sync. `app.issue_voucher_callback_path(tenant, label)` draws a segment (43 base64url characters), revokes the live one of the label and returns it once; `app.revoke_voucher_callback_path(tenant, label)` revokes it.

### Code pools (phase 16b)

`app.import_coupon_codes(tenant, store, reward_key, codes[], expires_at, at)` imports one vendor export (at most 1,000 codes) into `coupon_codes` as `available`, with the CRM programme of the store's mapping, which must exist: a code is 3 to 64 letters, digits, `-` or `_`; it counts `malformed`, `duplicates` (case ignored), `already_present` (the store's pool holds it, so a second import changes nothing) and `conflicting` (another pool or a redemption of the tenant uses it). `app.mark_used_pool_codes(tenant, from, to, at)` is the used-code pass: a pool code that a non-voided invoice of its store carries as voucher code becomes `used` with that invoice (completed first, then earliest) and its sale time, from any other state; it is idempotent. `app.coupon_code_status(tenant)` counts codes per store, reward and state; `app.coupon_codes_in_state(tenant, state)` lists them for `fnb loyalty codes export`. An index on `(store_id, upper(code))` serves the case-insensitive matches.

### Rail recognition (phase 16b)

`app.invoice_rail_evidence` holds, per non-voided invoice, what it carries of the rails: `member_phone`, `member_ref` (a linked customer), `pool_code`, `redemption_code`, `callback_sale`, `discount_entry` (the bill discount name is a mapped `pos_discount_name`, any case and spacing), `zero_price_line` (a 0-net line of a mapped free item) and `crm_promotion` (a line whose `promotion_external_id` is a mapped `crm_programme_external_id` or listed in the rail's `crm_programme_external_ids`). Each row has the store's rail at the time of sale (from the `coupon_rails` history), `on_rail` (the evidence belongs to that rail; identity always does), the `reward_key` when a mapping names it and the discount it carried: the invoice's line, bill and voucher discounts for a code, the bill discount for an entry, the 0-price lines' gross value, and the promoted lines' discounts plus a bill discount named after their promotion for a CRM promotion (on Fabi the voucher discount sits on the lines or in the extra discount, never in the voucher amount). `app.recognise_rail_events(tenant, from, to, at)` replaces the rows of the range on every run, so a rail switch or a mapping change is recognised by the next run without code; `app.reconcile_redemptions` calls it first and its `discount_without_redemption` check reads the evidence (`discount_entry`, `pool_code`, `redemption_code`) instead of matching names and codes itself.

### Where the steps run

`app.reconcile_loyalty` calls `app.reconcile_loyalty_paths`, `app.reconcile_redemptions` (which now starts with the recognition and adds `rail_evidence`) and then `app.reconcile_rails` (`callback_confirmed`, `callback_earned`, `pool_codes_used`). Like the other steps, nothing runs for a tenant without an active programme. All the functions of this section are executable by the service role only.

## Customer phones and the owner's member reads (#253)

Migrations `customer_phone_privacy`, `loyalty_member_reads` and `loyalty_shift_anomalies` keep a customer's full phone in the database (NFR-PRV): screens show it masked (`•••• ••• 234`, `app.loyalty_masked_phone`), and only an owner's phone lookup answers with it. The RPCs are in [api.md](api.md) section 3.23.

| Object | Does | Key rules |
| --- | --- | --- |
| column grant on `customers` | `authenticated` loses the table-wide `SELECT` and keeps `SELECT` on `id`, `tenant_id`, `external_id`, `name`, `first_seen_at` and `last_seen_at` | the policies are unchanged; reading or filtering on `phone`, or a PostgREST embed `customers(phone)`, gives `42501` to every API role, owners and customer sessions included; a column added later is not granted; the service role and the `security definer` functions keep the whole row |
| `public.phone_masked(memberships)` | the computed column `memberships.phone_masked` for PostgREST | `security definer`; looks the membership up again by id and answers only for a membership the caller reads, else null |
| `memberships_tenant_joined_idx` | index on `memberships` (`tenant_id`, `joined_at` desc, `id` desc) | the keyset order of the members list |
| `app.loyalty_store_balance(membership, store_ids)` | stamps, points, pending stamps and points and the last visit, computed as `app.loyalty_balance` over the ledger rows at `store_ids` and the rows without a store (every row when `store_ids` is null) | no API role executes it; called by the two readers below as the owner |
| `public.loyalty_members_page(store_ids, after, limit, search)` | the members of the tenants of the stores, newest first, masked, with the balance over the caller's loyalty stores among them | `security definer`; owners, managers and viewers only (`42501`); keyset paging by `page_key` (`joined_at` in UTC and the id), 1 to 1,000 rows; search by membership id or the last 1 to 3 digits; `22023` on bad arguments |
| `public.loyalty_member_lookup(phone)` | the memberships of the customer whose phone reads as `phone` in E.164 form, in every tenant where the caller reads loyalty | `security definer`; owners, managers and viewers only; `phone_full` only for an owner of the row's tenant; a GET through PostgREST (`request.method`) is refused with `PT405` |
| `public.loyalty_shift_anomalies(store_ids, from, to)` | per store, business date and shift (Ca {n} as `app.shift_number` numbers it), the earns, refusals by reason, manual adjustments and check-ins without an invoice | a `security invoker` report function: 0 to 50 stores, at most 92 days, `22023` otherwise; stores outside the caller's loyalty scope give nothing; never a cashier name or POS shift label |

**A store-limited caller's balance.** The members list and the lookup count the ledger rows at the stores in the caller's loyalty scope (narrowed to `p_store_ids` when given) and the rows without a store. An owner, or a manager or viewer of every store, sees the member's whole balance, equal to `app.loyalty_balance`. A manager or viewer limited to some stores sees the stamps and points earned, redeemed, adjusted, clawed back and expired at those stores, the welcome stamps given there or off-store, and off-store adjustments: possibly less than the whole balance, as `loyalty_ledger`'s policy would give that caller row by row. The member list itself is tenant-wide, as `memberships` is.

## Customer messaging (phase 19a)

Roadmap phase 19a, #278 to #280, built against a mock ZBS provider before the owner's ZBS account and approved templates exist. Migration `customer_messages` holds the schema, the one insert path and the triggers; the routing, claim and send functions follow in the next migration of the same step. Customer messages are rows of the phase 12 outbox (`outbound_messages`), kept a year like every other row (FR-CMP-06), but they have no `notification_channels` destination: the customer is the subject and the recipient is opaque.

### Contract for callers (OA webhook, OA enrolment)

Two functions in `app`, executable by `postgres` only (the role the Edge Functions and the jobs connect with):

| Function | Use it to | Answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `app.enqueue_customer_message(p_tenant_id uuid, p_membership_id uuid, p_kind text, p_event_key text, p_params jsonb default '{}', p_store_id uuid default null, p_expires_at timestamptz default null, p_at timestamptz default now()) returns uuid` | queue one message for one loyalty event of a member. `p_kind` is `points_confirmed`, `coupon_issued`, `voucher_expiring`, `expiry_reminder`, `birthday`, `win_back`, `takeaway_ready`, `order_cancelled` or `order_refunded`; `p_event_key` (`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_:.-]{0,119}$`, usually the ledger entry or redemption id) makes the event idempotent through the dedupe key `customer:<kind>:<event key>`; `p_params` must be count-only (`app.facts_are_count_only`: numbers, booleans, short lower-case tokens and ids, never a name, phone or text) | the message id (the first id again for a repeated event), or null with nothing written when the tenant's route for the kind is off, the membership is missing or not active, the customer is gone, the loyalty consent is not active, or for `birthday` and `win_back` the promotions flag or the marketing consent is missing; `22023` for an unknown kind, a malformed key or parameters that are not count-only |
| `app.link_customer_oa_user(p_oa_id uuid, p_oa_user_id text, p_customer_id uuid, p_at timestamptz default now()) returns text` | tie a customer to their OA user id (the follower id the OA sees, never the Mini App's per-app id) once the OA enrolment (`user_submit_info`) or another event names both; the routing then reads the customer's last interaction from `oa_events` (`app.oa_event_is_interaction`) to choose the free consultation message | `linked`, `moved` (the customer had another OA user id) or `unchanged`; an OA user id tied to another customer of the OA moves to this one; `22023` for a malformed id, an unknown OA or a customer of another tenant |

The loyalty events queue themselves: the trigger `loyalty_ledger_enqueue_points_confirmation` (`app.enqueue_points_confirmation()`) queues `points_confirmed` for an earn that is not pending, or for the confirm row of a pending earn, when it credited stamps or points; `redemptions_enqueue_coupon_issued` (`app.enqueue_coupon_issued()`) queues `coupon_issued` for a held redemption, expiring with the hold. Neither ever fails the loyalty write: a refused enqueue returns null and an unexpected error is turned into a warning. `app.enqueue_customer_reminders(p_at, p_tenant_id)` queues the scheduled kinds (below).

### Rows

A customer message is an `outbound_messages` row with one of the six kinds above or `takeaway_ready` (migration `takeaway_ready_message`, C17: a member's takeaway order turned ready, queued by the trigger `orders_takeaway_ready_message` (`app.enqueue_takeaway_ready()`, after update of `status` on `orders`) through `app.enqueue_customer_message` with the event key `order:<order id>`, `params` `{order_id, membership_id}`, expiring an hour after the order turned ready; transactional, its route `customer_message_settings.order_ready_route` (`template` by default), its ZBS template purpose `order_ready`, its parameters `order_no` and `pickup_time` resolved at send time), `order_cancelled` and `order_refunded` (migration `order_cancel_refund_messages`, C12, ADR-073: queued by the inbox's order trigger with the event keys `order:<order id>` and `order:<order id>:<event id>`, `params` `{order_id, amount, refunded_on}`; transactional, their route `customer_message_settings.order_cancel_route`, their ZBS template purposes `order_cancelled` and `order_refunded`, their parameters `order_no`, `store_name`, `amount` and `refund_date` resolved at send time; a cancellation waits five minutes in `not_before` and is cancelled as `superseded` when a refund comes first), and the constraint `outbound_messages_customer_shape` holds it to: `subject_type = 'customer'` and `subject_id` the customer; no `channel_id`; `channel` `mini_app` (the in-app notice, where every message starts), `zalo_oa` (a consultation message) or `zalo_zbs` (a template message); `recipient_ref` `membership:<id>` (or `redacted`); never a `title` or `body`. `message_class` is `transactional` for the four loyalty kinds and `advertising` for `birthday` and `win_back`; `purpose` and `consent_record_id` name the `loyalty` consent, or `marketing` for the advertising kinds; `template_ref` is `customer.<kind>.v1`; `params` hold the counts (`points`, `stamps`, `expires_on`, `inactive_days`), the references (`membership_id`, `entry_id`, `redemption_id`) and nothing personal: the customer's name, phone, OA user id, bill number, reward name and code are resolved only when the message is sent (NFR-PRV-02). `outbound_messages_queue_shape` lets such a row wait without a destination row. The data-subject deletion redacts these rows with `app.redact_outbound_for_subject` like any other.

### `app.customer_message_settings`

One row per tenant; no row means off. Written by the CLI as `postgres`, read by no API role.

| Column | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `enabled` | false | nothing is queued while false |
| `points_route`, `coupon_route`, `voucher_expiring_route`, `expiry_route` | `free`, `template`, `template`, `template` | how far each kind may go: `off` (no message), `in_app` (the wallet notice only), `free` (the free consultation message inside the window, else in-app), `template` (consultation inside the window, else a ZBS template) |
| `voucher_expiring_hours` | 24 | lead of the reminder before a held reward ends (1 to 168); only holds longer than the lead get it |
| `expiry_reminder_days` | 7 | lead of the reminder before points or stamps expire (1 to 60) |
| `promotions_enabled` | false | `birthday` and `win_back` (template only, with marketing consent) |
| `win_back_days` | 45 | days without an earn before a win-back (14 to 365) |
| `free_window_hours` | 48 | hours after the last OA interaction in which a consultation message is free (1 to 48) |
| `out_of_window_monthly_quota` | 0 | consultation messages per OA and month allowed after the free window and inside the API's 7 days (the package allowance); 0 sends a template instead |
| `zbs_enabled` | false | template messages may be sent; also needs an approved `zbs_templates` row |
| `zbs_daily_quota` | 1,000 | template messages per OA and tenant-local day |
| `order_ready_route` | `template` | how far `takeaway_ready` may go (C17) |
| `order_cancel_route` | `template` | how far `order_cancelled` and `order_refunded` may go (C12, "Đơn bị hủy, hoàn tiền") |
| `reminders_scanned_on` | | the tenant-local day of the last expiry and win-back scan |

### `app.customer_oa_contacts`

(`tenant_id`, `oa_id`, `customer_id`, `oa_user_id`, `linked_at`): one OA user id per customer and OA, one customer per OA user id; composite foreign keys to `oas` and `customers` with cascade, so a deleted customer loses the tie. Read by no API role.

### Scheduled kinds

`app.enqueue_customer_reminders(p_at default now(), p_tenant_id default null) returns jsonb` runs from the customer step of every notify cycle and returns `{voucher_expiring, expiry_reminder, win_back}`: on every call one `voucher_expiring` per held redemption ending within `voucher_expiring_hours`; once per tenant-local day one `expiry_reminder` per active member with loyalty consent whose points (`app.loyalty_expiring_points`) or stamps expire within `expiry_reminder_days`, keyed on the member and the day of the first expiry; and with `promotions_enabled` one `win_back` per member with marketing consent, joined and without an earn for `win_back_days`, keyed on the member and the month. `birthday` has no scheduled source: the programme collects no birthday, so it is queued only by an explicit call.

### Helpers

`app.customer_message_class(kind)` (immutable), `app.customer_message_route_setting(tenant, kind)`, `app.customer_message_oa(tenant, membership, store)` (the OA of the member's brand, else of the brand covering the store, else the tenant's only OA) and `app.customer_consent_record(tenant, customer, purpose)`; all executable by `postgres` only. The index `oa_events_oa_user_idx` (`oa_id`, `oa_user_id`, `occurred_at desc`) serves the window check; `outbound_messages_customer_activity_idx` and `outbound_messages_customer_due_idx` serve the caps and the claim.

### Routing: the send decision for customers

Migration `customer_message_routing` (#279, FR-NOT-39, FR-CMP-02, FR-CMP-16). `app.customer_message_route(p_message_id, p_at)` returns (`decision`, `route`, `release_at`, `reason`, `oa_id`, `template_id`, `window_kind`), first match wins:

1. cancel `expired`; `subject_removed` (the customer or the membership is gone, or the row was redacted); `membership_inactive`; `messaging_disabled` (the kind's route is `off`, which includes `birthday` and `win_back` without `promotions_enabled`); `no_consent` (no active `loyalty` consent, so a message waiting when the consent is withdrawn is never sent); `no_marketing_consent` (an advertising message, checked again here after the check at enqueue, so "Dừng nhận tin" stops what already waits);
2. `in_app` (reason `in_app_only`) when the route is `in_app`;
3. **consultation** for a transactional message when the customer has an OA user id on the speaking OA whose last interaction (`app.oa_last_interaction`: the latest verified `user_send_` event; a follow alone does not count) is within `free_window_hours` and the API's 7 days (`window_kind = 'free'`), or after that inside the 7 days while the OA's `out_of_window_monthly_quota` of the month lasts (`allowance`); the `zalo_oa` transactional policy must allow it, and outside its hours (07:00–22:00) the message is held (`outside_hours`) only while the free window stays open. A free consultation message therefore replaces the paid template for the same event. Advertising never goes as a consultation message;
4. `in_app` (`outside_window`) when the route is `free`;
5. **template** when `zbs_enabled`, an `approved` `zbs_templates` row of the OA serves the kind (`app.customer_template_purposes`: `points_confirmation`; `voucher`, then `coupon_issued`; `voucher`; `expiry_reminder`; `birthday`; `win_back`) and the customer has an OA user id or a phone; held outside the `zalo_zbs` policy's hours (`outside_hours`), at the customer's caps (`daily_cap`, `monthly_cap`; `app.customer_cap_release` counts the customer's own messages of the class on the channel, so Tag 3's 1 in a rolling 24 hours and 30 a month hold per user) and at the OA's `zbs_daily_quota` (`zbs_quota`); when such a hold would outlast the message it goes `in_app` with the hold's reason instead;
6. otherwise `in_app` with the reason: `templates_disabled`, `no_oa`, `no_template`, `no_recipient` or `not_allowed`.

### Dispatch functions

| Function | Behaviour |
| --- | --- |
| `app.claim_next_customer_message(worker, routes text[], lease default 2 minutes, at default now(), tenant)` | Locks due customer rows with `skip locked` in batches of 20 (at most 10 batches), takes the decision under a per-customer advisory lock, cancels and holds on the way, closes an `in_app` decision at once (`sending` then `sent` on `mini_app`, `cost_amount` 0, `params.route = 'in_app'`, the reason in `decision`), and returns at most one `consultation` or `template` message whose route `routes` names, now `sending` on `zalo_oa` or `zalo_zbs` with `template_ref` `consultation.<kind>.v1` or `zbs.<purpose>.v1` and `params` `route`, `oa_id`, `window` and `template_id`. The returned row carries what the adapter needs and the outbox never keeps: the template's external id, purpose and tag, `recipient_kind` (`uid` with the OA user id, else `phone` with the phone in international digits without a plus sign) and `parameters` (`app.customer_message_parameters`). A route the dispatcher lacks leaves the message waiting |
| `app.customer_message_parameters(message)` | `customer_name`, `points`, `stamps`, `bill_no` (the earn's receipt number), `store_name`, `reward_name`, `voucher_code`, `expires_at` (the hold's end as `HH:MM DD/MM/YYYY`, or the expiry day as `DD/MM/YYYY`, in the tenant's zone) and `inactive_days`, each only when known |
| `app.begin_customer_send(message, claimed_at, at) returns boolean` | Marks `send_started_at`; false for a lost claim; stores nothing else |
| `app.finish_customer_send(message, claimed_at, outcome, code, provider_message_id, retry_after_seconds, at) returns text` | `sent`; `retry` back to `queued` after `app.notify_backoff`, `failed` once five attempts are used; `reroute` (the OA refused the consultation message) back to `queued` at once with `params.consultation_refused`, so the next decision goes on to the template or in-app; `failed`; `uncertain` (a timeout or a lost connection), never resent; a late `sent` turns `uncertain` into `sent`; a row whose customer was deleted while it was being sent is redacted at once (`app.redact_outbound_for_subject`); returns the new status, `lost` or `missing` |

The phase 12 lease recovery (`app.recover_message_leases`) also covers these rows: a send that started and lost its lease becomes `uncertain`, one that never started is cancelled, so a crash never sends twice. Every function of this section is executable by `postgres` only. The dispatcher reads the OA access token with `app.oa_access_token` (migration `oa_menus_and_users`) and never stores or prints it.

### Report-rate monitoring

Migration `zbs_report_rate`. The catalogue rule `zbs_report_rate` (channel, immediate, `warning`, owners, 24-hour cool-down, sort 196; parameters `max_rate_percent` 0.1 to 2, default 1, and `min_sent` 1 to 100,000, default 50) watches the share of an OA's template messages that recipients report, because a report rate above 2 % lowers the OA's ZBS daily quota tier. `app.zbs_quality_days` (`tenant_id`, `oa_id`, `day`, `reported`, `recorded_at`; composite key to `oas`; no API role) keeps the reported count per OA and tenant-local day, written by `app.record_zbs_reports(oa, day, reported, at)`, which counts the day's sent templates of the OA in the outbox (`app.zbs_sent_on`) and, with at least `min_sent` of them, raises an instant episode `zbs_report_rate:oa:<OA row id>` when the rate is above `max_rate_percent`, with the count-only facts `sent`, `reported`, `rate_permille` and `max_rate_permille`; it answers the `raise_alert` outcome, `disabled`, `too_few_sent` or `below_threshold`. The count comes from the ZBS console or its quality answer; until the owner's ZBS account exists (#16) nothing calls it automatically.

## The native till (ADR-060 to ADR-065)

A store whose till mode is `native` takes orders here instead of in the POS ([deep-dives/native-till.md](deep-dives/native-till.md)). Built step by step from 2026-11-27.

### Settings, closures and presence (step 1, migration `native_catalogue`)

- `store_ordering_settings`: one row per store, `store_id` the key. `till_mode` (`ipos` default, `native`; only `set_store_till_mode`, an owner with every store), `ordering_enabled` (false), `order_types` (`{table}`; subset of `table`, `takeaway`), `payment_methods` (`{counter}`; subset of `counter`, `zalo_checkout`, `vietqr`, not writable by a client before the providers exist), `payment_window_minutes` 5–60 (15), `late_order_minutes` 1–120 (10), `prep_minutes` 0–120 (10), `last_order_minutes` 0–120 (0), `max_order_amount` 50,000–20,000,000 whole đồng (2,000,000), `max_waiting_orders` 3–200 (30), `staff_presence_required` (true), `paused_until`, `paused_reason` (≤ 80), `paused_by` (only `counter_set_pause`), attribution columns. The takeaway slots of C17 (migration `takeaway_slots`): `takeaway_slot_minutes` (5, 10, 15, 20, 30 or 60, a divisor of an hour; 15) and `takeaway_slot_limit` (1–100; 6), written by owners and managers of the store like the other settings.
- `ordering_closures`: `store_id`, `starts_on`, `ends_on` (at most 62 days a row), `reason` (1–80, one line).
- `app.ordering_presence` (`store_id`, `user_id`, `seen_at`): the last counter poll per device; no API role reads it.
- `app.ordering_state(store, type, at)` answers `accepting` or the first reason holding orders: `ordering_off`, `hours_unknown`, `closed_today`, `paused`, `closed_now`, `last_orders_passed`, `no_staff_online` (no poll in 180 seconds), `busy` (accepted orders not started reach `max_waiting_orders`).

### The owned menu (step 1)

Per brand: `menu_categories` (name 1–40, order, visible), `menu_items` (category of the same brand, name 1–60, description ≤ 200 on one line without a link or phone, `photo_path` `<tenant>/<brand>/<uuid>.webp|jpg|png` in the public bucket `menu-photos`, `labels` ⊆ {`best_seller`, `new`}, order, `visible`, `restricted` never visible, `counts_for_stamps`, `order_types`, import provenance), `menu_item_sizes` (label null or 1–20 unique per item, `price` 1 to 100,000,000 whole đồng, one default per item, `active`), `menu_option_groups` (name, `min_select` 0–20 ≤ `max_select` 1–20), `menu_options` (name unique per group, `price_delta` 0 to 1,000,000), `menu_item_option_groups` (item and group of one brand). Child tables carry `brand_id` with composite foreign keys, so a row can never join another brand's. Per store: `menu_store_items` (`offered`, `sold_out_until`, `sold_out_by`). `menu_price_changes` is the append-only price log written by triggers on every size price and option amount set or changed (`subject_name`, `old_price`, `new_price`, `changed_by`, `changed_at`; update, delete and truncate refused for every role, rows kept when the item goes, removed only with the brand or tenant). `menu_imports` records the one-time import per brand. `app.menu_versions` moves with every menu, availability, settings and closure change.

`item_option_groups`, `item_option_group_options` and `item_option_group_targets` are POS catalogue tables written by the master-data run ([sync.md](sync.md#pos-option-groups)); `import_menu_from_pos` reads them once.

`app.menu_rows(store, brand, at)` is the one reading of the menu at a store: a size is `shown` when its item is on sale in a visible category, not restricted and the size active, and `available` when it is also offered at the store and not sold out at the instant; the customer menu, the counter menu and (step 3) the order placement read it.

### Tables, QR codes and staff calls (step 2, migration `tables_and_calls`)

- `store_tables`: `store_id`, `label` (1–20, unique among the store's active tables whatever the case), `area` (≤ 40), `sort_order`, `active`, `qr_version`, `qr_token_sha256` (unique; no API role selects it), `qr_issued_at`. A trigger issues the hash of version 1 on insert and of every new version: `rotate_table_qr`, and reactivating a table, which never revives a printed card; a version never goes back and a table never changes store.
- `app.table_qr_keys` (`tenant_id`, a 32-byte `key`): one key per tenant, created at its first table, readable by no API role (the ADR-052 pattern). `app.table_qr_token(tenant, table, version)` is base64url of the first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA-256(key, `table:<id>:<version>`); `app.table_qr_hash` its SHA-256 in hex.
- `table_calls`: `store_id`, `table_id`, `customer_login_id` (null when the login is deleted), `created_at`, `acknowledged_at`, `acknowledged_by`; at most one open call per table (a partial unique index).
- `app.code_attempt_policies` gains `customer_table` (misses: 10 per caller, 50 per store in 15 minutes, 15-minute lock) and `customer_call_staff` (calls: 6 and 120).

### Orders (step 3, migration `native_orders`)

- Enums `order_status` (`awaiting_payment`, `accepted`, `preparing`, `ready`, `serving`, `completed`, `cancelled`) and `order_payment_status` (`unpaid`, `paid`, `refund_pending`, `refunded`, `partially_refunded`).
- `orders`: tenant, brand and store (composite keys), `source` (`app` or `counter`), `customer_login_id` (null when the login is deleted), `order_type` (`table`, `takeaway`), `table_id` and a `table_label` snapshot, `pickup_at`, `business_date`, `order_no` (unique per store and business date; null while awaiting an online payment), `status`, `payment_status`, `payment_method` (`counter`, `zalo_checkout`, `vietqr`), `version`, `change_seq` (from `app.order_change_seq`), `subtotal`, `discount_amount`, `total` (CHECK `total = subtotal - discount_amount`, whole đồng), `note` (≤ 200), `request_id` and `request_hash` (unique per customer login, or per staff member for counter orders), `menu_version`, the step times (`placed_at`, `payment_due_at`, `accepted_at`, `preparing_at`, `done_at`, `completed_at`, `paid_at`, `cancelled_at`), the cancellation (`cancelled_by`, `cancel_actor`, `cancel_reason`, `cancel_note`), `created_by` (the staff member of a counter order). CHECKs keep a number on every accepted order and none on an unpaid online attempt, the cancellation fields together, and a completed order paid.
- `order_lines`: `line_no` 1–30, `item_id` and `size_id` (null when the menu row goes), the snapshots `item_name`, `size_label`, `unit_price`, `options` (`[{group_id, group, option_id, name, price}]`), `options_amount`, `line_total` (CHECK `quantity × (unit_price + options_amount)`), `quantity` 1–20, `counts_for_stamps`, `note` (≤ 100).
- `order_events`: append-only for every role (a trigger refuses update, delete and truncate; only the tenant's deletion removes rows): `kind` (`status`, `payment`), `action`, `from_state`, `to_state`, `actor_kind` (`customer`, `staff`, `system`, `provider`), `actor_user_id`, `reason_code`, `reason_text`, `amount`, `occurred_at`.
- `app.order_counters` (`store_id`, `business_date`, `last_no`) gives numbers under a row lock; `customer_order_blocks` (`customer_login_id`, `blocked_until`, `reason` `suspected_prank`).
- `app.order_move` is the one writer of the lifecycle; `app.order_payment_succeeded` the one writer of a payment (no client role executes either); `app.order_price_lines` the one pricing of an order; `app.ordering_tick` the minute job (pg_cron `ordering-tick`, created off). `app.code_attempt_policies` gains `customer_place_order` (calls: 20 per customer, 200 per store in 15 minutes).

### Payments and refunds (step 4, migration `order_payments`)

- `order_payments`: `order_id`, `method` (`cash`, `card`, `zalo_checkout`, `vietqr`), `amount` (whole đồng), `status` (`pending`, `succeeded`, `failed`, `voided`), `provider` and `provider_ref` (unique per provider; none for the counter's methods), `idempotency_key` (unique per order), `recorded_by`, `requested_at`, `settled_at`, `failure_reason`, `voided_by`, `voided_at`, `void_reason`.
- `order_refunds`: `order_id`, `payment_id`, `method`, `amount`, `status` (`due`, `pending`, `succeeded`, `failed`), `reason`, `provider_ref`, `requested_by`, `requested_at`, `recorded_by`, `settled_at`.
- `app.order_money(order)` gives what was paid, refunded and is still owed (due, pending or failed refunds); `app.order_settle_payment_status` derives the order's payment status from it. `app.order_payment_succeeded` now requires a succeeded payment record of the amount. `app.order_move` opens a due refund when a paid order is cancelled and runs `app.order_after_completion` on completion; succeeded refunds run `app.order_after_refund` (steps 5 and 6 fill both hooks).

### Online payment (migration `online_payments`, ADR-067, ADR-068)

- `payment_providers`: `brand_id`, `store_id` (null for the whole brand; the Checkout SDK is per brand), `method` (`zalo_checkout`, `vietqr`), `adapter` (`zalo_checkout`, `sepay`, `mock`), `mode` (`off`, `sandbox`, `live`; a mock never live), `secret_name` (`ZALO_CHECKOUT_KEY_…` or `PAYMENT_BANK_…`, a CHECK refusing a pasted value), `settings` (public: a forced wallet; bank BIN, bank name, account number, account name, reference prefix), created and updated by and at; unique per brand, method and store. Written only by `checkout_set_provider`.
- `payment_provider_changes` (append-only for every role): `provider_id`, `changed_by`, `changed_at`, `before`, `after`.
- `payment_exceptions`: `store_id`, `provider_id`, `method`, `provider_ref` (unique per provider), `amount`, `expected_amount`, `reason` (`amount_differs`, `late`, `duplicate`, `unknown_reference`), `reference`, `payment_id`, `order_id`, `source` (`webhook`, `requery`), `received_at`, `status` (`open`, `refunded`, `dismissed`), `resolved_by`, `resolved_at`, `resolution_note`. No payer name or account.
- `app.payment_attempts` (closed to every API role), one per online `order_payments` row: `provider_id`, `adapter`, `mode`, `reference` (unique: a prefix and 8 characters of 2-9 and A-Z without I and O), `actor` (`customer`, `staff`), `started_by`, `expires_at`, `platform_order_id` (Zalo's order id once linked), the report to Zalo (`platform_reported_at`, attempts, error), `requery_count`, `last_requery_at`.
- `app.refund_attempts` (closed): per online refund the job sends, the provider's refund id, attempts, last error, next try. `app.checkout_job_settings` and `app.checkout_job_tickets` drive the minute job `checkout-tick`.
- A trigger on `store_ordering_settings` offers the online methods whose providers are on to a new settings row; `checkout_set_provider` keeps every store's `payment_methods` in step.

### The stamp card (step 5, migration `stamp_cards`)

- `stamp_card_settings` (primary key `brand_id`): `tenant_id`, `enabled`, `stamps_per_reward` (2–30, default 9), `reward_name`, created and updated by and at.
- `stamp_cards`: `brand_id`, `customer_login_id` (unique together; removed with the login), `stamps` (≥ 0), `total_earned`.
- `stamp_rewards`: `card_id`, `status` (`available`, `used`, `void`), `stamps` it took, `issued_at`, `issued_order_id`, `used_order_id` (unique: one reward per order), `used_at`, `voided_at`, `void_reason`.
- `stamp_entries` (append-only, also for the database owner, except when its card or tenant is deleted): `card_id`, `order_id`, `reward_id`, `kind` (`order_earned` once per order, `order_reversed`, `reward_issued`, `reward_used`, `reward_returned`, `reward_voided`), `delta`, `balance_after`, `actor_kind`, `actor_user_id`, `note` (a refund's shortfall), `occurred_at`.
- `app.stamp_entry` is the one writer of the ledger and the card's balance; `app.stamps_on_completion`, `app.stamps_on_refund` and `app.stamps_on_cancel` fill the hooks `app.order_after_completion`, `app.order_after_refund` and the new `app.order_after_cancel` (run by `app.order_move`). `app.customer_place_order` and `app.customer_check_cart` take `use_reward`; `orders.discount_amount` holds the reward and an order it makes free is paid at placement.

### Native orders in the sales facts (step 6, migration `native_sales_facts`)

- `app.native_pos_account(tenant)`: the tenant's POS account of connector `native` (account key `native`) with the payment methods `cash`, `card`, `zalo_checkout` and `vietqr`; no store points at it.
- `app.native_order_invoice(order, at)` writes the invoice of a completed order (external id `native-<order id>`, no receipt number, no phone), its item and modifier lines and its payments by method, idempotently; `app.native_order_refunded` rewrites it after a succeeded refund (`edited`, or `voided` when fully refunded) with an `invoice_events` row (source `native_refund`). Both run from the hooks `app.order_after_completion` and `app.order_after_refund`.
- Refunds of a later business day (migrations `invoice_status_refund` and `refund_business_day`, ADR-071): `app.refund_business_date(refund)` is the business day a succeeded refund counts on (cash: the business date of the recorder's shift at the store that held the instant; card and online: the store's business date at the instant; never before the order's; null unless succeeded). `app.native_order_invoice` takes off only the refunds of the order's own day, then calls `app.native_refund_documents(order, at)`, which writes, idempotently, one document per refund of a later day (external id `native-<order id>-refund-<refund id>`, status `refund`, the refund's day, gross 0, net and paid minus the refund, one `invoice_payments` row of minus the refund in its method, no line) and marks its day; `app.native_order_refunded` takes that path, without rewriting the order's invoice or logging an event, when the refund just settled counts on a later day. All three are revoked from every API role.
