- `app.native_analytics_pending` (`tenant_id`, `business_date`, `marked_at`): the dates whose native invoices changed; `app.native_analytics_refresh(at, limit)` refreshes those that left the live window (`app.analytics_live_from`) and removes the marks it saw; `app.ordering_tick` runs it for two dates a minute.

### The requests inbox (step 7, migration `store_requests`)

- `store_ordering_settings` gains `request_kinds` (`booking` by default; `workshop`, `hold`, `application`) and `request_answer_minutes` (5–1440, 30).
- `store_requests`: `store_id`, `kind`, `channel` (`website`, `app`), `customer_login_id` (the app's; deleted with the login), `client_request_id` (unique per channel), `client_key_sha256` (the website's), `request_hash`, `contact_name`, `contact_phone` (E.164), `party_size`, `requested_for`, `subject`, `note`, `status` (`new`, `confirmed`, `declined`, `cancelled`), `version`, `answered_by`, `answered_at`, `answer_message`, `cancelled_by_kind` (`customer`, `staff`), `cancelled_at`, `created_at`. Written only by `app.request_submit` (behind the app's and the website's entry points), `app.customer_cancel_request` and `app.manager_answer_request`; `app.ordering_tick` deletes a request 180 days after its date.
- Two limits in `app.code_attempt_policies`: `request_app` (5 calls a day per customer, 100 per store, an hour's lock) and `request_website` (3 an hour per client key, 100 per store).

### Shifts (step 8, migration `staff_shifts`)

- `store_ordering_settings` gains `counter_qr_version` (moved only by `manager_counter_qr`), `standard_day_hours` (1–16, 8) and `standard_month_days` (20–31, 28).
- `staff_shifts`: `store_id`, `user_id`, `business_date` (of the clock-in), `clock_in_at`, `opening_cash`, `clock_out_at`, `expected_cash`, `counted_cash`, `cash_difference` (counted less expected), `status` (`open`, `awaiting_confirmation` with a difference, `closed`), `closed_by`, `closed_at`, `close_note` (required to close with a difference), `version`; at most one shift per person that is not closed. Written only by the shift functions.
- `app.counter_qr_token(tenant, store, version)` signs `counter:` with the tenant's table QR key; `app.shift_cash_flow(shift, until)` is the cash a person recorded less the cash they handed back during a shift; `app.worked_days_rows` turns recorded times into worked days. A limit `staff_clock_in` in `app.code_attempt_policies` (5 misses of a person, 20 of a store in 15 minutes).

### The native till's hardening (migrations `staff_lock_guards` to `request_proposals_and_reads`, 2026-11-28)

- `app.roster_lock_guard(roster row)` (migration `staff_lock_guards`): `deactivate_staff` and `update_staff` (a change of role or stores) refuse one's own entry and the tenant's only owner (`42501`, hints `own_entry`, `last_owner`).
- `app.counter_shift_open(store, user, at)` and the trigger `order_payments_need_shift` (before insert on `order_payments`, `app.order_payments_need_shift()`; migration `counter_payment_needs_shift`): a cash or card payment recorded by a person needs that person's open shift at the store, begun by the payment; `counter_record_payment` answers `shift_required` first, the trigger refuses any other writer (`42501`, hint `shift_required`); rows without a recorder are not checked.
- `staff_shifts` gains `difference_reason` (`wrong_change`, `unrecorded_order`, `petty_cash`, `unknown`), `difference_note` (1–200) and `difference_reason_at`, all three set together and only on a shift that was counted with a difference (CHECK `staff_shifts_difference_reason_fields_check`); written only by `app.staff_clock_out(counted, at, reason, note)` (migration `shift_difference_reason`), cleared by a matching recount.
- `app.order_ready_estimate(order)` (migration `order_ready_estimate`): an order's `ready_estimate_at`, computed in `app.order_json` and `app.customer_orders` from `accepted_at` or `preparing_at`, `pickup_at` and the store's `prep_minutes`; nothing is stored.
- `app.stamp_member_codes` (migration `stamp_member_codes`; RLS on, no API role): per brand and customer login, `token_sha256`, `number_sha256`, `issued_at`, `expires_at` (10 minutes), `revoked_at` (by the next code); deleted with the login. `orders` gains `member_login_id` (the stamp card holder the counter attached to a counter order; CHECK only on a counter order without a customer; set null with the login), `member_attached_at` and `member_attached_by`; `order_events.kind` gains `member`. `app.stamps_on_completion` earns for `customer_login_id` or else `member_login_id`. Helpers `app.luhn_check_digit`, `app.stamp_code_hash`, `app.stamp_code_parse`, `app.stamp_code_mistyped`, `app.stamp_code_find`, `app.stamp_member_summary`; two limits in `app.code_attempt_policies`: `counter_member_lookup` (misses: 5 per person, 20 per store in 15 minutes) and `customer_stamp_code` (calls: 30 per customer in 15 minutes).
- `app.customer_login_erasures` (`tenant_id`, `login_hash` HMAC-SHA-256 of the Zalo App and user id under the tenant's key, `erased_at`, `counts`) and `app.customer_data_exports` (`customer_login_id`, `exported_at`, `counts`; deleted with the login), both RLS on and read by no API role (migration `customer_account_deletion`); `app.customer_delete_account` and `app.customer_export_data` write them.
- `app.request_time_open(store, at)` (migration `web_request_hours`): inside an opening span (`app.ordering_span_at`) and on no day an `ordering_closures` row covers, true for a store without hours; `app.request_submit` refuses a booking or a hold outside it (`time_outside_hours`).
- `store_requests` (migration `request_proposals_and_reads`): `status` gains `proposed` (CHECK: with `proposed_for`, `answered_at` and the message), and the columns `proposed_for`, `read_at` and `read_by` (the first owner or manager who opened it; CHECK `read_by` only with `read_at`); written by `app.manager_answer_request(id, status, message, version, at, proposed_for)` and `app.manager_request_read(id, at)`; `app.customer_cancel_request` also cancels a proposed request.

### E-invoices and company invoices (migration `einvoices`, ADR-069, ADR-070)

- `einvoice_providers`: `brand_id`, `store_id` (null for the whole brand; a store row overrides it), `adapter` (`mock` until a provider is chosen), `mode` (`off`, `sandbox`, `live`; the mock never live), `secret_name` (`EINVOICE_KEY_…`, a CHECK refusing a pasted value), `settings` (`seller_tax_code`, `template` `1` or `2`, `series` of a cash register `^[CK][0-9]{2}M[A-Z0-9]{2}$`, `vat_rate` `none` for template 2, else 0, 5, 8 or 10; nothing else), created and updated by and at; unique per brand and store. Written only by `einvoice_set_provider`. `einvoice_provider_changes` (append-only for every role): `provider_id`, `changed_by`, `changed_at`, `before`, `after`.
- `store_ordering_settings` gains `company_invoice_days` (1–30, 7): the company invoice window after the payment's local date.
- `e_invoices`: `store_id`, `order_id`, `provider_id`, `kind` (`sale`, `replacement`, `adjustment`, `cancellation`), `state` (`queued`, `issuing`, `issued`, `failed`, `replaced`, `adjusted`, `cancelled`), `reason` (`paid`, `company_invoice`, `refund`, `payment_voided`), `original_id` (the document a replacement or correction acted on), `request_id` (a replacement's request), `action` (`issue`, `replace`, `adjust`, `cancel`), `mode`, `amount` (whole đồng, negative for a correction), the provider's `provider_ref` (unique per provider), `invoice_no`, `invoice_series`, `lookup_code`, `lookup_url` (https), `tax_authority_code`, `attempts`, `round_attempts`, `last_error_class` (`app.einvoice_error_classes()`), `last_attempt_at`, `next_attempt_at`, `gave_up_at`, `claim_token` and `claimed_until` (while `issuing`), `queued_at`, `issued_at`, `settled_at`, `cancel_reason` (`order_unpaid`, `nothing_to_invoice`, `nothing_to_change`). Partial unique indexes: one live sale invoice, one company invoice and one queued correction per order. No buyer data.
- `invoice_requests`: `store_id`, `order_id` (unique: one per order), `source` (`app`, `counter`), `customer_login_id` (null once the login is deleted), `requested_by` (the counter's staff member), `client_request_id`, `request_hash`, `tax_code` (normalised), `company_name` (1–200), `address` (1–300), `email` (≤ 254), `status` (`received`, `issuing`, `issued`, `emailed`, `failed`, `cancelled`), `einvoice_id` (the replacement), `email_attempts`, `email_error_class`, `email_next_at`, `email_claimed_until`, `requested_at`, `issued_at`, `emailed_at`, `closed_at`.
- `saved_companies`: `customer_login_id` (deleted with the login), `tax_code` (unique per login), `company_name`, `address`, `email`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; at most 10 per login (`customer_request_invoice`).
- `app.einvoice_reconcile(order, reason, at)`, run by the triggers `orders_einvoice` (after a change of `payment_status`) and `order_refunds_einvoice` (after a refund succeeds) and after every job result, queues and cancels as native-till.md section 17.3 says; `app.einvoice_ready` is the job's rule of what it may take; `app.einvoice_job_settings` and `app.einvoice_job_tickets` (closed) drive the minute job `einvoice-tick`. `app.code_attempt_policies` gains `customer_invoice_request` (calls: 10 per customer, 1,000 per tenant in an hour, an hour's lock); `alert_rule_catalog` gains `einvoice_failed` (operations, immediate, owners and managers, an hour's cool-down). `customer_delete_account` counts the saved companies it deletes and the requests it unlinks; `customer_export_data` adds `invoice_requests` and `saved_companies`.
- Migration `einvoice_foreign_key_indexes` gives every foreign key of these five tables an index led by its columns (`(tenant_id, order_id)`, `(tenant_id, store_id)`, `(tenant_id, request_id)`, `(tenant_id, provider_id)` of `e_invoices`; `(tenant_id, order_id)`, `(tenant_id, store_id)`, `(tenant_id, einvoice_id)`, `customer_login_id` and `requested_by` of `invoice_requests`; the provider tables' keys and authors), so a cascaded delete or set null never scans them.

### Phase 1 gaps (migrations 20261128600000 onwards, 2026-11-28; native-till.md section 18)

- `app.counter_menu` (migration `counter_menu_stamp_flag`) answers each item's `counts_for_stamps` and the brands' `stamp_cards`; no table changes.
- `store_requests` (migration `customer_accept_proposal`) gains `customer_accepted_at` (when the customer accepted the time proposed), `accept_request_id` (the app's request id of the acceptance; unique per login, index `store_requests_accept_key`), `accept_seen_at` and `accept_seen_by` (the first owner or manager who opened or answered it since; a foreign key to the Auth users, set null, indexed by `store_requests_accept_seen_by_idx`); CHECK `store_requests_accept_check`: the moment and the id together, only on an app request with a proposal, a seen mark only after an acceptance. Written only by `app.customer_accept_proposal`, `app.manager_request_read` and `app.manager_answer_request`; `app.request_accepted_unseen(request)` is the inbox's flag (confirmed by the customer, not seen since).
- The trigger `order_refunds_need_shift` (before insert or update on `order_refunds`, `app.order_refunds_need_shift()`; migration `cash_refund_needs_shift`): a refund that becomes a succeeded cash refund recorded by a person needs that person's open shift at the store at its settlement (`app.counter_shift_open`; `42501`, hint `shift_required`); rows without a recorder and rows already settled in cash are not checked. `app.counter_refund` answers `shift_required` before writing.
- `app.staff_shift_reconciliation(shift, at)` (migration `shift_reconciliation`) reads a shift's cash from `order_payments` and `order_refunds` (the person's cash and card, the store's online payments, between the clock-in and the clock-out or now); no table changes.
- `app.manager_day_reconciliation(store, date, at)` (migration `refund_business_day`, ADR-071) subtracts each refund on the day it counts on (`app.refund_business_date`): the revenue and the card and cash by method come from the day's native documents (refund documents included), and the confirmed online refunds of completed orders are taken off the gateway's figure on their own day. It answers two more explanations: `cash_refund_owed` and `cancelled_cash`. The readers of the sales facts count `completed` and `edited` invoices only and sum every one but `voided`: `app.daily_sales_facts`, `app.hourly_sales_facts`, `app.refresh_analytics` (the payment aggregate's `invoices`), `app.evaluate_intraday_rules` and `app.reconcile_alerts` (zero sales), `app.evaluate_voucher_spikes`, `data_quality_summary`, `member_share_by_shift` and `customer_metrics`; `export_control_totals` gains `refund_invoices`.
- `app.stamp_member_codes` gains the index `stamp_member_codes_expires_idx` (`expires_at`); `app.purge_stamp_member_codes(at, limit)` deletes codes expired more than a day before `at` (at most `limit`, 20,000 by default, oldest first), run by the pg_cron job `stamp-codes-purge` (`17 * * * *`, scheduled by migration `member_code_purge` when pg_cron is installed); no API role may run it.
- `app.manager_payment_exceptions(store, status, limit)` (migration `payment_exception_readers`) takes the status `resolved` (`refunded` and `dismissed` together) and answers per exception `resolved_by_name` (from `staff_roster`, else the newest named `zalo_identities` row of the resolver in the exception's tenant) and `account` (`scope` from `payment_providers.store_id`; the bank's name and the last 4 characters of the account number from the latest `payment_provider_changes.after` at or before `received_at`, else the row's `settings`); no table changes.
- `app.einvoice_pill(order)` (migration `einvoice_pill_holding_document`) orders the order's sale and company invoices on their way by what holds the order back (given up, then failing, then the newest queued or being made) and reads the pill from the first; no table changes.

### The customer's notifications inbox (C12, migrations `weekly_promotion_cap`, `order_cancel_refund_messages` and `customer_inbox`, 2026-11-29, ADR-073)

- `app.customer_inbox` (RLS on, no API role; read and marked only through `customer_inbox`, `customer_inbox_unread` and `customer_inbox_read`): `id`, `seq` (identity, the page cursor, newest highest), `tenant_id`, `customer_login_id` (deleted with the login), `kind` (`order_ready`, `order_receipt`, `order_cancelled`, `order_refunded`, `stamp`, `reward`, `offer`), `topic` (generated: `orders` for the order kinds, `offers` for the others), `order_id` (required for the order kinds; `(tenant_id, order_id)` references `orders`, cascade), `params` (count-only, `app.facts_are_count_only`), `title` (1–80) and `line` (1–160) only on an offer (a campaign's text), `dedupe_key` (unique per login: one item per event), `created_at` (the event's time), `read_at`. Indexes on `(customer_login_id, seq desc)`, the unread items of a login, `created_at` (the purge), `tenant_id` and `order_id`.
- Its writers: `app.customer_inbox_add(tenant, login, kind, key, params, order, title, line, at)` (one item per login and key, a login of the tenant only); the triggers `order_events_customer_notices` (after insert on `order_events`, `app.customer_order_notices()`: the decision table of ADR-073, which also queues `order_cancelled` five minutes on for a member's paid order cancelled by the shop, cancels it as `superseded` when a refund comes first, and queues `order_refunded` unless the refund was cash; a failure is a warning, never a failed order move), `stamp_entries_customer_notices` (after insert on `stamp_entries`, `app.customer_stamp_notices()`: `stamp`, or `reward` when the earn completes a card) and `outbound_messages_customer_offer_notices` (after a `birthday` or `win_back` turns `sent`, `app.customer_offer_notices()`: an `offer` for each login of the customer); `app.customer_inbox_offer(tenant, login, key, title, line, at)` for a campaign (GA-066), with the marketing consent only. `app.customer_order_membership(order)` names the membership a paid message goes to.
- `app.customer_inbox_retention()` is 90 days: the readers leave older items out and `app.purge_customer_inbox(at, limit)` deletes them (at most `limit`, 20,000 by default, oldest first), run by the pg_cron job `customer-inbox-purge` (`29 * * * *`, scheduled by the migration when pg_cron is installed).
- `app.code_attempt_policies` gains `customer_inbox_read` (calls: 120 per customer, 10,000 per tenant in 15 minutes) and `customer_marketing` (calls: 10 per customer, 1,000 per tenant in 15 minutes).
- `app.customer_start_marketing(policy, screen, shown_at, at)` records the `marketing` consent through `app.customer_record_consent` with the evidence `{screen_version, shown_at, accepted_at, channel, zalo_app_id, toggle, ticked, source: "inbox_switch", weekly_cap}`.
- `customer_export_data` adds `inbox` (and its count), `customer_delete_account` counts `inbox_items`, `dsr_export` adds `inbox` (the items of the customer's logins); `dsr_delete` removes them with the logins.
- `channel_policies` gains `max_per_week` (above) and `customer_message_settings` gains `order_cancel_route` (below); the outbox gains the kinds `order_cancelled` and `order_refunded` (below), and `app.claim_next_customer_message` now takes every kind `app.customer_message_class` names.

## Row-level security

Every table in `public` has RLS enabled. `anon` holds no privilege on any table. `authenticated` holds `SELECT` on every table except the token caches `pos_account_tokens` and `zalo_tokens` and the OA webhook's event log `oa_events`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` only on the manual-input tables (`item_costs`, `payment_fee_rates`, `item_type_roles`, `holidays`, `targets`), the report settings and mappings (`report_settings`, `item_category_overrides`) and `strategy_reports`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE` without `DELETE` on the legal ledgers `consent_records` and `data_subject_requests`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` on the notification settings `alert_rules` and `channel_policies`, and `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` on the six Zalo configuration tables (`brands`, `zalo_apps`, `oas`, `mini_apps`, `zbs_templates`, `gmf_groups`). On `oa_menus` (phase 19a) it holds `SELECT` and `DELETE`, and `INSERT` and `UPDATE` only on the eight configured columns; it holds no privilege on `oa_users`. On `notification_channels` it holds column-level `SELECT` only, without `address`, `secret_name` and `config`, and on `staff_roster` column-level `SELECT` only, without `phone`, and no write privilege (phase 14a). On `customers` it holds column-level `SELECT` only, without `phone` (#253). Default privileges are set so that a future table created by the migration role starts read-only for `authenticated` and invisible to `anon`. The sync jobs run as `service_role`, which bypasses RLS.

### Roles

| Role | Purpose | Reads | Writes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `owner` | Tenant owner | Everything of the tenant within store scope, plus `outbound_messages`; `data_subject_requests` only when the membership is not store-limited, because the policy requires an unlimited store scope, so a store-limited owner reads zero requests | Manual inputs and plans within store scope; consent records (grant, or revoke by setting `revoked_at`); data-subject requests when the membership is not store-limited |
| `manager` | Store or tenant manager | Same as owner, including the zero-request rule for a store-limited membership | Same as owner |
| `staff` | Cashier or shift lead | Everything of the tenant within store scope, including `consent_records`, except that the 16 revenue tables follow `tenants.staff_revenue_visibility` (all their stores' rows at `all`, the default; none at `store_totals` or `none`, where `today_store_totals()` gives the totals at `store_totals`); of `staff_roster` only their own row; no `data_subject_requests`; of `outbound_messages` only the messages to their own destinations (none in phase 12) | Nothing directly; their own `staff_ops` consent and phone through the functions of phase 14a, and their own rows of the operations tables of phase 14b (the template above) |
| `viewer` | Read-only stakeholder | Same as staff | Nothing |
| `publisher` | Service identity used by the plan-publishing command or routine | `tenants`, `stores`, `holidays`, `dim_calendar` only; no plan, pack, aggregate table, refresh log or live view | Publishes through `publish_strategy_plan` only; no direct insert |

For the notification tables: every authenticated role reads `alert_rule_catalog` and the global `channel_policies`; owners and managers read `alert_rules` (a store-limited manager the tenant rows and those of their stores), the tenant's policy overrides, every destination of the tenant when their scope is unlimited and otherwise their own, the episodes and messages of their stores, and the digests whose stores are all in their scope; staff, viewers and publishers read nothing else; `anon` reads nothing.

For the Zalo configuration of phase 13: owners and managers of the tenant, whatever their store scope, read the six configuration tables and `zalo_identities` (`app.has_tenant_role`); only owners with an unlimited scope write the six configuration tables; staff, viewers and publishers read none of them, not even their own identity; nobody but the service role reaches `zalo_tokens`, and no API role reads `oa_events`, whose day counts owners read through `oa_event_counts`.

For the staff roster of phase 14a: owners and managers read the roster rows whose stores are all in their scope and write them through the roster functions within that scope; staff read their own row; viewers and publishers read none. No API role reads `app.staff_pairing_codes` or `app.staff_pairing_attempts`.

`owner`, `manager`, `staff` and `viewer` are the data roles. `publisher` is deliberately outside them so that a leaked publisher credential cannot read a single sale.

### Store scope

`tenant_members.store_ids` limits a member to some stores of the tenant. A null scope means every store. Store-scoped fact tables filter on `store_id`; `sync_runs` filters on the stores a run covered (see its section); tenant-wide catalogue tables filter on `tenant_id`; tables whose `store_id` may be null (`item_costs`, `payment_fee_rates`, `strategy_reports`) show tenant-wide rows to every member of the tenant and store rows only to members who can see that store.

### Helpers in schema `app`

Every helper sets an empty `search_path`. The membership helpers (`data_roles()` through `can_read_store()`) are `security definer` and `stable`, and read `tenant_members` for `auth.uid()`. `has_active_consent()` and `dsr_deadlines()` are `security invoker`, so they see only what the caller may see. `set_updated_at()`, `keep_created_by()`, `keep_consent_grant()` and `fill_dsr_deadlines()` are trigger functions; only `fill_dsr_deadlines()` is `security definer`, because it must read the tenant row of a request filed from the browser.

| Function | Returns | Used by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `data_roles()` | The four data roles | The two data helpers below |
| `current_user_tenant_ids()` | Tenants of the caller in any role | `tenants`, `strategy_reports`, `holidays` |
| `current_user_data_tenant_ids()` | Tenants of the caller in a data role | Every other tenant-filtered table |
| `current_user_store_ids()` | Stores the caller may see in any role | `stores`, `strategy_reports` |
| `current_user_data_store_ids()` | Stores the caller may see in a data role | Every other store-filtered table |
| `has_tenant_role(tenant_id, roles[])` | Caller holds one of the roles in the tenant | Available to policies and application code |
| `has_store_scope(tenant_id, store_id, roles[])` | Caller holds one of the roles and the store is in scope; a null `store_id` requires an unlimited scope | The two wrappers below |
| `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, store_id)` | `has_store_scope` with `owner`, `manager` | Insert, update and delete on manual inputs; update and delete on plans |
| `can_publish_store_scope(tenant_id, store_id)` | `has_store_scope` with `owner`, `manager`, `publisher` | Insert on `strategy_reports` |
| `can_read_store(store_id)` | Caller may see the store | Application code; no policy calls it per row |
| `set_updated_at()` | Trigger | Every table with `updated_at` |
| `keep_created_by()` | Trigger | Every table with `created_by` |
| `keep_consent_grant()` | Trigger | `consent_records` before update; freezes every column except `revoked_at` and makes a revocation final |
| `has_active_consent(tenant, subject_type, subject, purpose)` | An active consent row exists | Application code and messaging jobs; `security invoker`, so RLS applies to the caller |
| `dsr_deadlines(received, country_code, time_zone, uses_processor)` | The two statutory deadlines of a data-subject request; raises when no second working day lies within 120 days | `fill_dsr_deadlines()` and application code; `security invoker` |
| `fill_dsr_deadlines()` | Trigger | `data_subject_requests` before insert; `security definer` so it can read the tenant row |
| `is_holiday(tenant_id, country_code, d)`, `tet_window(…)`, `is_excluded_day(store_ids, d)` | Holiday and Tết checks | Baselines and forecasts; `security invoker` |
| `sales_invoices()`, `daily_sales_facts()`, `hourly_sales_facts()`, `daily_sales()`, `hourly_sales()`, `summarize_daily()`, `analytics_live_from()` | Analytics computations | The refresh, the live views and the query functions; `security invoker` |
| `analytics_as_of(p_store_ids uuid[], p_at timestamptz default now())` | `date`: the last business date that has ended in every given store at `p_at`, that is the earliest store-local business date (timezone and day start) minus one; the UTC date of `p_at` minus one when no given store is visible | Default origin of `weekday_profile_forecast()` and `seasonal_naive_forecast()`, default `as_of` of `month_pace()`; `security invoker` |
| `daily_net_series(p_store_ids uuid[], p_from date, p_to date)` | `table (business_date date, net_amount numeric, excluded boolean)`: one row per business date with data, the net summed over the stores from `daily_sales()` (so the hot and cold rule applies) and `excluded` from `is_excluded_day()` | `weekday_profile()`, `seasonal_naive_series()`, `forecast_accuracy()`; `security invoker` |
| `weekday_profile(p_store_ids uuid[], p_origin date)` | `table (weekday smallint, level_net_amount numeric, weekday_index numeric)`: seven rows, ISO weekday 1–7; `level_net_amount` is the mean daily net of the clean (not excluded) days among the 28 ending at `p_origin`, `weekday_index` the weekday's mean ÷ the mean of the clean days among the 84 ending at `p_origin`, null when the weekday has no clean day | `weekday_profile_forecast()`, `month_pace()`; `security invoker` |
| `seasonal_naive_series(p_store_ids uuid[], p_origin date, p_from date, p_to date)` | `table (target_date date, forecast_net_amount numeric)`: one row per date from `p_from` to `p_to`, the net (rounded to 2 decimals) of the most recent day with sales on the target's weekday within the 56 days ending at `least(p_origin, target_date - 1)`, holidays not excluded; null when there is none | `seasonal_naive_forecast()`, `month_pace()`; `security invoker` |
| `refresh_analytics(p_tenant, p_from, p_to)` | Rows written | Sync jobs and `public.refresh_my_analytics()`; `security definer`, executable by `service_role` only |
| `channel_kind_available(kind)`, `notify_secret_name_valid(kind, name)`, `facts_are_count_only(jsonb)` | Check helpers of the notification tables | CHECK constraints; executable by `authenticated` and `service_role` |
| `alert_rule_params_valid(rule_key, params)` | The parameters match the catalogue schema | The invoker trigger on `alert_rules`; executable by `authenticated` and `service_role` |
| `current_user_channel_ids()` | The caller's own destinations | `outbound_messages` read policy; `security definer` |
| `zalo_secret_name_valid(name, prefix)`, `mini_app_title_valid(title)` | Check helpers of the Zalo configuration tables | CHECK constraints; executable by `authenticated` and `service_role` |
| `validate_zalo_store_ids()`, `bump_zalo_token_version()` | Trigger | `brands`, `gmf_groups` and `staff_roster` (a store listed twice or of another tenant); `zalo_tokens` (the version moves on every update); executable by no API role |
| `current_user_revenue_store_ids()` | Stores whose revenue rows the caller reads: as `current_user_data_store_ids()`, without a `staff` membership's stores unless its tenant's `staff_revenue_visibility` is `all` | The read policies of the 16 revenue tables (`invoices`, `invoice_lines`, `invoice_payments`, `invoice_events`, `shifts`, `sync_checks` and the ten `agg_*` tables); `security definer` (phase 14a) |
| `can_manage_staff(tenant_id, store_ids[])` | Caller is an owner or manager of the tenant whose scope holds every given store; a null list needs an unlimited scope | `staff_roster` read policy and the roster and pairing-code functions; `security definer` |
| `ops_can_read(tenant_id, store_id)`, `ops_can_write_own(tenant_id, store_id)`, `ops_can_manage(tenant_id, store_id)`, `current_user_ops_store_ids()` | The operations access of owners, managers and staff within store scope ("Operations access: helpers and the policy template") | The four policies of every operations table (phases 14b and 15); `security definer` |
| `claim_ipos_source(tenant_id, source)` | The tenant's recorded iPOS source, `api` or `snapshot`, recorded at the first claim | The runners before they write iPOS data; `postgres` only |
| `custom_access_token_claims(event)` | The token's claims with `memberships` | Supabase Auth's access-token hook; `security definer`, executable by `supabase_auth_admin` only (#205) |
| `zalo_binding_code_hash(zalo_app_id, code)` | The stored form of a binding code | The binding-code and sign-in functions, which run as its owner; executable by no API role |

Policies call the array-returning helpers as `(select app.helper())` so Postgres evaluates them once per statement, not once per row.

### Who can write what

| Table | Insert | Update / delete |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `item_costs`, `payment_fee_rates`, `targets` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, store_id)` and `created_by = auth.uid()` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, store_id)` |
| `item_type_roles` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, null)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`: owner or manager with an unlimited store scope | Same |
| `item_category_overrides` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, null)` and `created_by = auth.uid()` | Same |
| `report_settings` | `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`: owners with an unlimited scope, because the thresholds govern the managers too | Same rule |
| `holidays` | `tenant_id` not null and `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, null)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`; global rows are service-role only | Same, without the `created_by` condition |
| `stores` (profile columns) | — | Update only, of `code`, `opening_hours`, `seat_count`, `opened_on`, `e_invoice_status`, `tran_no_reset_period` and `member_capture_status` (column grant): `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, id)`; `opening_hours` additionally needs an owner of that store and `code` must stay unique in the tenant (`stores_stamp_profile`); name, address, time zone and day start stay with the POS |
| `tenants` (profile columns) | — | Update only, of `legal_form` and `default_language` (column grant): `has_store_scope(id, null, owner)`; `staff_revenue_visibility` only through `set_staff_revenue_visibility()`, by the same owners |
| `payment_kind_overrides` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, null)` and `created_by = auth.uid()` | Same |
| `legal_documents` | `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`, as a draft without dates | Update of `title`, `body_markdown` and `version` only (column grant), same rule, frozen once published; delete of drafts only; publication and retirement through the two functions |
| `strategy_reports` | none: plans are written only by `publish_strategy_plan` | `can_manage_stores(tenant_id, store_ids)`; the content trigger allows only `title` and `archived_at` |
| `consent_records` | `has_tenant_role(tenant_id, owner or manager)`: any owner or manager of the tenant, store-limited or not, because consent is captured at the till | Update only, same rule; the `keep_consent_grant` trigger lets the update set `revoked_at` once and nothing else; no delete |
| `data_subject_requests` | `can_manage_store_scope(tenant_id, null)`: owner or manager with an unlimited store scope | Update only, same rule; no delete |
| `outbound_messages` | Service role only | Service role only; owners and managers read, every member reads the messages to their own destinations |
| `alert_rules` | `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`: owners with an unlimited scope, tenant and store rows; the validation trigger runs as the writer | Same rule; managers write nothing |
| `channel_policies` | `tenant_id` not null and `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`; global rows are service-role only; the floor CHECKs bind the browser too | Same rule |
| `notification_channels` | Through `security definer` functions only: service-side code, and the member's own Zalo Bot link (`start_bot_link`, #229) | Same: the member's own `muted_rules` (`set_my_muted_rules`) and their own stop (`stop_my_notifications`) |
| `brands`, `zalo_apps`, `oas`, `mini_apps`, `zbs_templates`, `gmf_groups` | `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)` and `created_by = auth.uid()`: owners with an unlimited scope; the CHECKs on secret names and titles and the store-list trigger bind every writer | `has_store_scope(tenant_id, null, owner)`; managers and store-limited owners write nothing |
| `zalo_identities` | `app.zalo_sign_in` and `app.zalo_pair` only, reached through `zalo_session_sign_in` and `zalo_session_pair` by `service_role` (#205, #213) | The same, and deletion by `deactivate_staff` and a person's own `withdraw_staff_ops_consent`; owners and managers read |
| `staff_roster` | `add_staff` by an owner or manager with every listed store in scope; no table privilege | `update_staff`, `deactivate_staff`, `reactivate_staff` by an owner or manager whose scope holds every store of the row, old and new; the pairing step (status, login, `paired_at`); `link_my_phone` by the person, phone only; never deleted from the browser |
| `app.staff_pairing_codes`, `app.staff_pairing_attempts` | `issue_pairing_code` (codes), the pairing step (both); no API role reads them | The same |
| `consent_records` with purpose `staff_ops` | Never from the browser (restrictive policy); only at the person's own acceptance of the published staff notice | Never from the browser (restrictive policy); only the person's own `withdraw_staff_ops_consent` |
| `oa_events` | `oa-webhook` as `postgres` (#207); no policy and no grant to an API role | The same |
| `zalo_tokens` | Service role only (the token jobs); no policy and no grant to an API role | Service role only |
| `consent_records` with purpose `notifications` | Never from the browser (restrictive policy); only at the member's own "ĐỒNG Ý" to the notice in the Bot chat, or their e-mail code | Never from the browser (restrictive policy); only the member's own withdrawal (`stop_my_notifications`, "DỪNG" in the Bot chat) or `fnb notify unlink` |
| Every other table | Service role only | Service role only |

Tenant-wide rows (null `store_id`, or tables without one) can be written only by owners and managers whose membership is not limited to some stores. `created_by` must equal the caller on insert and is frozen by trigger afterwards, so attribution cannot be forged.

## Business date

`invoices.business_date` is assigned by the sync, never by the database, with this rule:

```
local_time     = sold_at at time zone stores.timezone
business_date  = (local_time - make_interval(mins => stores.business_day_start_minutes))::date
```

A store with `business_day_start_minutes = 300` (05:00) books a sale at 00:30 local time to the previous calendar day; with the default 0 the business date is the local calendar day. Store timezones are IANA names, so a zone with daylight saving is handled by Postgres. `invoice_payments.business_date` copies the invoice value. Shifts keep their raw timestamps; reports attribute a shift to the business date of its `opened_at` using the same rule.

## Storage rule for `raw`

Storage on the free tier is finite, and a payload stored on its own row is expensive: an iPOS line carries about a hundred fields, about 3 kB as `jsonb`, and a row under 2 kB is never compressed. Kept that way, the payloads took about two thirds of the database after the full backfill of both shops, well over the 500 MB plan. The sync therefore keeps every payload, but not on the sales rows:

- The sync writes null to `invoices.raw`, `invoice_lines.raw` and `invoice_payments.raw`. The payloads of a store-day's sales go into its `sale_payloads` document, which compresses about ten times, because the sales of a day repeat the same field names and many of the same values.
- The `invoice` part of a record is the full payload the POS returned for the sale, so any future column can be back-filled without a re-sync.
- A line or tender payload leaves out the fields whose value equals the invoice payload's (store, receipt number, timestamps, cashier, customer on iPOS). Read a field missing from it from the invoice payload; every other field is kept as returned.
- `shifts.raw`, `invoice_events.raw` and master data `raw` keep the payload of that entity as returned, on the row.

With this rule the same history fits under the 300 MB ceiling. This is a sync-side rule: the database accepts any JSON. `mapSale` drops the fields shared with the invoice (`packages/sync/test/unit/payloads.test.ts`), and the Postgres repository writes the records through `app.store_sale_payloads` (`supabase/tests/19_sale_payloads.sql`). The in-memory repository keeps the payloads on its rows. Migration `20261108062820_sale_payloads.sql` moves payloads already on the rows into `sale_payloads`; a database that held sales before it returns the space after `vacuum (full, analyze) public.invoice_lines, public.invoice_payments, public.invoices;`. The seed writes the demo tenant in the same shape: its invoices, lines and payments have null `raw` and each of its store-days has a document, so a fresh reset in CI matches production.

## Tenant-consistent composite foreign keys

A row that carries both `tenant_id` and a reference to a child of the tenant (a store or a POS account) references the pair, not the child's id alone:

`stores` and `pos_accounts` declare `unique (tenant_id, id)`; every store-scoped table declares `foreign key (tenant_id, store_id) references stores (tenant_id, id)` and every account-scoped table declares `foreign key (tenant_id, pos_account_id) references pos_accounts (tenant_id, id)`.

The database therefore refuses a row that names tenant A together with a store of tenant B, even when written by the service role or by a bug in a policy. Tables that follow the pattern: `stores`, every catalogue table, `store_items`, `devices`, `invoices`, `invoice_lines`, `invoice_payments`, `invoice_events`, `sale_payloads`, `shifts`, `sync_runs`, `sync_checks`, `item_costs`, `payment_fee_rates`, `item_type_roles`, `targets`, `strategy_reports`, every `agg_*` aggregate table, `alert_rules`, `alert_events` (store, account and destination), `notification_channels` (its member, as (`tenant_id`, `user_id`) to `tenant_members`), `outbound_messages` (destination, store and digest, each `on delete set null` of the column only), and the Zalo tables of phase 13: `zalo_apps`, `oas`, `mini_apps` and `zalo_tokens` to `brands`, `zbs_templates` and `gmf_groups` to `oas`, and `zalo_identities` to `zalo_apps`, each declaring `unique (tenant_id, id)` in turn where another table references it; `app.zalo_binding_codes` to `zalo_apps` and to `tenant_members`, and `oa_events` and `app.oa_event_days` to `oas` (#205, #207); and from phase 14a `app.staff_pairing_codes` to `staff_roster` (#213), which declares `unique (tenant_id, id)` for it, and every operations table to `stores` (the template of #216). The store lists `brands.store_ids`, `gmf_groups.store_ids` and `staff_roster.store_ids` are arrays, which a foreign key cannot check, so the trigger `app.validate_zalo_store_ids()` refuses a store of another tenant with `23503`, as a foreign key would, also for the service role. `invoice_events.invoice_id` references `invoices.id` alone with `on delete set null`, because the event must outlive the invoice; its `store_id` and `invoice_external_id` keep it attributable. The legal tables carry `tenant_id` and no store; `consent_records.subject_id` and `data_subject_requests.subject_id` deliberately have no foreign key (see the consent model), and `outbound_messages.consent_record_id` references `consent_records.id` alone with `on delete set null`.

The notification tests (26 to 32) build their own fixture tenants (`notify-it-pgtap-…`), the phase 10 tests (33 to 39) theirs (`theme-pgtap-…`), the phase 11 tests (40 and 41) theirs (`pack-pgtap…`, `export-pgtap…`), the Zalo configuration test (42) `zalo-it-pgtap-a` and `zalo-it-pgtap-b`, the iPOS source test (43) `ipos-source-…`, the Zalo identity and events tests (44, 45) `zalo-it-pgtap-identity-…` and `zalo-it-pgtap-events-…`, the phase 14a tests (46 to 49) `roster-pgtap-a` and `-b`, `ops-rls-pgtap-x` and `-y`, `consent-pgtap-c` and `-d` and `pairing-pgtap-p` and `-o` (47 also reads the demo tenant's seeded window to compare report functions by role), and destinations on `_IT` secret names and pass a tenant filter to every scanning function. The analytics tests scope every count to the demo tenant, and the report tests to their own fixture tenants (and the demo tenant where they compare with the seed), so further tenants in the database do not change their outcome. The seed is synthetic and the tests create their own fixture users and tenants inside a rolled-back transaction.
